# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project

Mobile-first 2.5D space game (inspired by Interstellar Pilot 2) with a fully autonomous economy and factions that run without player input. The world is a graph of sectors; each sector is its own instance and the only way between them is via jumpgates. Stack: engine-independent C# core + Godot 4.7.2 (.NET build) as the view. The user communicates in German.

**Architecture is the top priority.** Features must be added as separate modules/systems behind clear seams (registration, interfaces, events, data files), not by growing central classes. Keep `SpaceSim.Core` free of Godot and file IO.

## Commands

The .NET 10 SDK is installed; `SpaceSim.Core` targets `net8.0`, the test and tool projects `net10.0`, the Godot project `net9.0` (the Godot 4.7 Android template only accepts net9.0 for C#).

```
dotnet build                       # solution (Core, tests, balance tool; NOT the Godot project)
dotnet test                        # all unit tests (~6–7 min, includes long deterministic runs and the fleet/trade-chain measurements)
dotnet test --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Economy_RunsStably_WithoutPlayer"   # single test
dotnet run --project tools/SpaceSim.Balance -- --ticks 60000 --every 5000    # headless economy report (also: --seed N --world file.json --csv out.csv --security off --fuel on|off --research on|off --dump on --strict on --systemtime off); exit code 1 if prices degenerate, 2 if the map splits, 3 (only with --strict on) if the Phase 15 HINWEIS block lists anything (Phase 17: EXPORTNACHFRAGE lines do not count); prints ms/tick and "Rechenzeit je System (Top 5)"

dotnet build godot                 # build the Godot C# project
godot-mono --path godot            # run the game (use godot-mono, NOT godot: plain godot cannot run C#)
godot-mono --path godot -e         # open the editor
godot-mono --headless --path godot --fixed-fps 60 -- --selftest   # scripted headless run: autopilot across sectors, docking, panels, save/load; prints SELFTEST OK, uses its own save file
```

`godot-mono` is a symlink in `~/.local/bin` to `~/.local/opt/godot-mono/`. Godot prints harmless "Leaked unsafe reference"/ObjectDB warnings when quitting from code.

## Core architecture (`src/SpaceSim.Core`)

**Two layers.** Simulation layer: every sector exists as plain data in `Universe` and is always simulated. Presentation layer: only `Universe.PlayerSector` is materialized as a Godot scene. Core must never reference Godot; the view must not invent simulation state.

**Modules and systems.** `SimulationRunner` builds its system list from `ISimModule`s (`Modules/DefaultModules.cs`: Production, Research (Phase 13), Export (Phase 14), Orders (Phase 12: commands, hangar fees; since Phase 27 also the executors of police and pirates), Jobs (Phase 6), Resources, Trade, Upgrades (Phase 7), Construction (Phase 8; since Phase 20 also `FactionBuilderSystem`), Security (Phase 11; since Phase 27 the faction/group decisions only give commands — `PoliceCommands`, `PirateCommands`; since Phase 28 `NavySystem`, warships of factions at war), Combat (Phase 23: `ShipSystemsSystem`, shield/bank recovery of ships that are not full; since Phase 24 first the round combat `CombatSystem` over the active encounters and `RearmSystem`; since Phase 28 the encounters also run station guns and missiles, and `StationRepairSystem` repairs damaged stations), Lifecycle (since Phase 20 starting with `SupplyPanicSystem`), Actors (Phase 9; since Phase 15 also station maintenance and subsidies from the faction treasuries), Viability (Phase 22: profitability measurement, capital balance within firms, faction minimum stock), Politics (Phase 10; since Phase 28 `WarSystem`, war and peace)) via `SimulationBuilder.Add(system, interval)`. Order matters (production/market before AI, lifecycle last). A system with `interval > 1` runs every n ticks and receives `dt = n` (simulation LOD) — it must scale by `dt`. To add behavior: write an `ISimSystem`, register it in a module (or a new module in `DefaultModules()`).

**Events.** `Universe.Events` (`SimEvents`) is a synchronous bus (`TradeCompleted`, `MineDelivered`, `StationBuilt/Removed`, `ShipSpawned/Removed`). Stats, UI toasts etc. subscribe; systems publish. The bus is not persisted — re-subscribe after loading (the view does this in `Main.AttachSession`).

**Content is data-driven.** `Content/default-world.json` (embedded resource; since Phase 21 an 11-sector map with rings, three factions, two herrenlose sectors and two pirate groups — see the Phase 21 section; the former 4-sector world lives on as the test resource `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/Content/classic-world.json`, `TestWorlds.Classic()`) defines goods (26 JSON goods (Phase 23 appended `ammo`, so module goods start at id 27): six raw ores, refined goods, components, end goods, the kit tiers `ship_kit_light` (id 22, formerly `ship_kit`) / `ship_kit_medium` / `ship_kit_heavy`, `propellant`; referenced by string key `Good.Key`, `GoodId` = index+1, with a UI `color`; since Phase 5a every module additionally gets an automatic good appended behind them, see the Phase 5a section), asteroid classes (ore mixes), station types with recipes (22, incl. the three module factories), factions, sectors, gates, stations, belts (fields), ships. Ore chain: 6 ores (iron, ice, copper, silicon, titanium, platinum) → refined (steel, water, hydrogen, copper, wafers, titanium, platinum, energy) → components (electronics, hull plates, fuel cells, life support) → end goods (food, consumer goods, luxury goods, ship kit). `DemoUniverse` has `GoodId` constants for the default world; `ContentTests` guards them and the chain's consistency. `WorldBuilder.Parse/Build` validates and throws `WorldFormatException` with the offending name — including the slot sections: duplicate keys, unknown slot type/size/class/module keys, unknown goods in a module's `cost`, unknown `grants` names, module larger than its slot, module slot type mismatch, fit length ≠ slot count, empty required slot, and the power balance (Σ powerOutput ≥ Σ powerDraw; Σ peakDraw − (powerOutput − powerDraw) ≤ Σ powerStorage). `DemoUniverse.Create()` is just `WorldBuilder.BuildDefault()`. Core never reads files; callers pass JSON text. The file also has optional `slotTypes`/`modules`/`shipClasses`/`shipModels` sections (missing sections = empty registries on `Universe.SlotTypes/Modules/ShipClasses/ShipModels`) and `goods[].form` (`solid` default, `liquid`; content only, not persisted, old saves stay v3-loadable). `goods` carry `Good.Form`; `Model/ShipStats.Compute(class, fit)` derives power/cargo/hull/mass/thrust/speed (clamped to `ShipStats.MaxSpeed`)/upkeep/capabilities/laser tier as a pure function. Phase 3a added all of this as data only; Phase 3b wires it up: every ship carries `ModelKey` ("" = no model, legacy ships keep the old flat values), its `Slots` list (default: the model's class slots) and its `Fit` (module key per slot, null = empty), plus the derived `Ship.Stats` — `Ship.RecomputeStats(u)` recomputes after Fit/Slots changes and on load (without a resolvable model the fallback is hull 100). `Ship.MaxHull` is now an instance property from `Stats.Hull` (the const is gone; the view uses `ship.MaxHull`); `CargoCapacity` stays a field for its many consumers but is set from `Stats.Cargo` at creation/load when a model exists; capabilities come from `Stats.Capabilities` unless explicitly set (`CapabilitiesSet` semantics unchanged, a saved value wins over the model). `Travel.Plan/Start/Estimate/Ticks` take an optional ship and use `Travel.SpeedOf(ship)` = `Stats.Speed` when a model is present, else the `ShipSpeed` constant — all unit models are 8, so travel is unchanged. World JSON: `ships[]` groups and `player` accept an optional `model`; if absent, `WorldBuilder.NewShip` picks the unit model by role+cargo (`UnitModelFor`: trader/100→`unit_trader_100`, trader/60→`unit_trader_60`, miner/100→`unit_miner_100`, miner/60→`unit_miner_60`, player→`unit_player`, else "" e.g. Fighter); an unknown explicit key throws `WorldFormatException`. `FleetSystem.Expand` copies model/slots/fit from the parent ship. Save v4: `ShipSave` gains `ModelKey`, `Slots`, `Fit`, and the four slot-content sections (`SlotTypes`/`Modules`/`ShipClasses`/`ShipModels`) are persisted in the save like the other content (goods, asteroid classes, station types incl. recipes), so a save is self-contained; `MigrateV3ToV4` sets only `ModelKey` per role/cargo and injects the embedded default-world slot registry (v3 saves contain no slot content), and on load `Slots`/`Fit` are filled from the model when the save lacks them; `UniverseHash` additionally hashes `ModelKey` and `Fit`. The unit models reproduce the old flat values exactly (cargo 100/60, hull 100, speed 8, role-derived capabilities), so behavior, `UniverseHash` roundtrips and the balance run are unchanged apart from the hash value. Tuning knobs live in `EconomyRules` (JSON `rules` section, also saved).

**Space and travel.** Entities reference each other by typed ID structs in dictionaries on `Universe`. Jumpgates are the edges of the sector graph (`Universe.FindRoute`, per-tick cached via `CachedRoute`/`IsReachable`; since Phase 10 with a `MobilityProfile` — the cache is split by profile, closed/destroyed gates block everyone, sector locks block per actor, see the Phase 10 section). Ships outside the player's sector have no physics: `Travel.Plan` turns a trip into contiguous `Leg`s (one per sector, detouring around obstacles via `PathPlanner`), `Transit` stores them, `ArrivalTick` is the last leg's end. `Travel.Start` consumes real fuel from the ship's tank (Phase 4b; see the Phase 4 sections at the end). `Travel.Estimate` is the cheap (straight-line) variant for AI scoring. Obstacles (`Obstacle`, built by `Universe.GetObstacles`) are asteroids (`Asteroid.ToObstacle()`, generated by `Demo/AsteroidGenerator`) and station bodies. Ships target `Station.DockPoint(shipId)` / `ResourceField.WorkPoint(shipId)`, never centers. `Ship.Position`/`Sector` are only updated on arrival; mid-trip location comes from the legs.

**Ship index.** `Universe.Tracker` (`ShipTracker`) maps sector → parked ships / ships with a leg in that sector. Always mutate ship placement through `Universe.AddShip/RemoveShip/StartTransit/FinishTransit/MoveShip`, never by writing `Ship.Sector`/`Transit` directly. `Materializer.Snapshot(u, sector, fractionalTick)` uses the index to derive where each NPC ship is (moving ships by interpolating the active leg, parked ones at `Ship.Position`); the view renders that.

**Capabilities and controller (Phase 2).** `ShipRole` is still the stored current-task label (save format, display), but systems decide by capability, not by role: `Ship.Capabilities` (`[Flags] Capability`: `Cargo`, `Mining`, `Combat`, `Construction`, `Salvage`, plus `Jump`/`Stealth` which exist but are unused until the deferred jump-drive/cloak modules, no `Research`) and `Ship.Controller` (`Ai`/`Player`, checked via `Ship.IsPlayer`). Both are saved (`ShipSave.Capabilities` int / `Controller` string, nullable, so the format stays v3; old saves without them derive from the role) and part of `UniverseHash`. `CapabilityDefaults.ForRole/ControllerFor` derive them from the role at the creation points (`WorldBuilder.NewShip`, `UniverseSerializer.FromSave` for old saves, `FleetSystem.Expand`) and in `Universe.AddShip` only when the creator never set them (`Ship.CapabilitiesSet/ControllerSet` flags, set by the property setters), so an explicit `Capability.None` is a valid value. Ships with a resolvable model (Phase 3) always take `Capabilities` from their fit (`Ship.RecomputeStats`), so removing a module takes effect even after load; the saved value only counts for model-less ships. `TraderSystem`/`MinerSystem` keep the role as the task filter and additionally require `Has(Capability.Cargo)`/`Has(Capability.Mining)`; a ship that lacks it is released via `JobRelease.Release` (order and reservation dropped, a running transit is flown to its end and finished without trading/mining, cargo stays) instead of being skipped — skipping froze such ships mid-transit with a ghost reservation; `FleetSystem`, `UpkeepSystem`, `EconomyStats` and `StationLifecycleSystem` count economically active AI ships via `Ship.IsEconomyAi` (Controller Ai with Cargo or Mining); `TradeActions` (PlayerSold/PlayerBought) recognize the player via `IsPlayer`; `Materializer` skips only the active ship (`Universe.PlayerShip`), so parked own ships are rendered (Phase 5c). Pure display (ship colors in SectorView/SectorScreen, `ShipSnapshot.Role`) may still use the role.

**Economy.** Price per station and good = `BasePrice * clamp(Target / max(Amount,1), 0.25, 4)` (`MarketSystem`). `ProductionSystem` runs station recipes. `TraderSystem` scores routes by profit per tick (margin × units / `Travel.Estimate`) with stock reservations by other traders; since Phase 5a a trader buys at most what the selling target still accepts (`Station.AcceptableUnits`); since Phase 14 traders buy only where a recipe produces the good (`Station.Produces`, never at consumers or stock-only entries), plan and buy only what the buyer can pay, and carry a rest that the buyer no longer takes to the next buyer (`TradeActions.BestBuyerFor`; since Phase 19 never dumped above the acceptance — the rest stays aboard and an AI trader jettisons it in space when no reachable station takes it) — see the Phase 14 and 19 sections. `MinerSystem` mines belts and refuses to mine when the mix is worth less than `MinPriceFactor` × its base value. Prices: besides the stock price, a factory bids at most `InputWillingness` of its products' value for inputs (`MarketSystem`, stops price spikes from bankrupting processors; with several recipes using the same input the highest of their ceilings counts, Phase 5a; since Phase 16 the product is valued at min(own price, best buyer bid), see the Phase 16 section); stations accept only up to `Target × OverstockFactor` (`Station.AcceptableUnits`; default world 2.5 since Phase 19, code default 3) and production pauses when outputs exceed that (`ProductionSystem`) — since Phase 15 demand-aware: a recipe whose products no other station is short of pauses already at `Target × NoDemandStockFactor` (1), inputs needed only by such paused recipes are accepted only up to `Target`, stock-only shelves accept up to `Target × ShelfOverstockFactor` (1; export goods keep the overstock), and a station never accepts deliveries of a pure product of its own (`StockEntry.NoIntake`) — see the Phase 15 section. Departures (Phase 22): the simulation removes ships and stations only through `Simulation/Departures` and only for (a) destroyed, (b) bankrupt or (c) long unprofitable (`DepartureReason`, event `ShipDeparted`/`StationDeparted`; player sale/rental return are the only other reasons), and faction decisions keep the faction viable (`FactionViability`) — see the Phase 22 section. Money flow (`UpkeepSystem`): station income (faucet; since Phase 14 also the export sink's 18 % markup, see there) and upkeep, wealth taxes (since Phase 9 a transfer into the treasury of the responsible faction, see the Phase 9 section; since Phase 15 only on station money above its working capital, and a full treasury passes the excess out of the world — fiscal brake, see the Phase 15 section), ship upkeep, fuel, ship/station purchases (sinks); without sinks credits pile up at ships. `StationLifecycleSystem`: bankrupt stations are removed unless `BankruptcyProtected` (formerly `Essential`), derived `Critical` (last reachable producer of a good) or a build site (`Universe.RemoveStation` cancels referencing orders), scarce goods (stock ratio > `BuildPriceRatio`) trigger a new producer (Phase 15: not if one of its raw-ore inputs is itself scarce — `StationLifecycleSystem.UpstreamProducer`; Phase 18: the build follows the chain to the scarcest manufactured input, and kits/modules never trigger a second yard or module works) funded by an investor drawn by the Phase 9 weighted draw (`Investors`: actor treasuries, station or ship operating accounts) — since Phase 8 as a build site that needs materials and a construction ship (see the Phase 8 section), direct build only as fallback. Since Phase 19 the lifecycle also reviews its stock: an unprotected station whose products are not scarce is retired (since Phase 22: when it is long unprofitable, independent of free slots; before: activity below 10 % with all slots taken), and the slot goes to a persistently scarce good (`StationReview`, see the Phase 19 and 22 sections). Since Phase 20 a part of the map cut off by a closed/destroyed gate panics when it loses the last producer of a good it needs and builds a replacement with priority (`SupplyPanicSystem`, see the Phase 20 section). `FleetSystem`: rich ships (since Phase 9 drawn by weight, not the richest) buy ships up to `MaxTraders/MaxMiners`, counted as capacity units since Phase 6 (see the Phase 6 section: cargo ÷ 100 for trade, laser tier for mining; the AI chooses the model) — but a new ship needs a **ship kit** of its class's tier (`FleetSystem.KitKeyFor`: `ShipClass.Kit`, fallback `EconomyRules.ShipKitGoodKey` = `ship_kit_light`; made by shipyards from hull plates, electronics and fuel cells, i.e. from titanium/platinum chains) from a reachable yard, which receives `NewShipCost`; the ship appears at the yard. Idle broke ships are abandoned (reason b; since Phase 20 only with an empty hold; faction construction ships never), and since Phase 22 traders/miners are retired only when they are long unprofitable (reason c, `ProfitMeter` with grace, 20000-tick window and hysteresis — this replaced the Phase 15/20 idle retirement; followed by a purchase block) (player money is never involved: these systems only touch economy-AI ships, which are their own wallet); since Phase 20 the capacity limits apply per reachable part of the map (`FleetCapacity`) and a faction treasury buys ships for a part whose fleet collapsed; idle ships back off their re-planning (`TraderSystem.IdleBackoff`). Scarcity/glut for station construction is measured on stock ratios, not prices, and only goods some station type can answer count (a producer type for scarcity, a recipe consumer type for glut; Phase 5a — otherwise module goods on stock without any consumer would block every consumer build). Idle AI ships only re-think every ~10 ticks (`Ship.NextThinkTick`).

**Determinism.** All randomness goes through `Universe.Rng` (`DeterministicRng`, state saved). `Diagnostics/UniverseHash` hashes simulation state; tests assert same seed → same hash and save → load → continue is bit-identical. Never use `System.Random`/time/hash-order-dependent iteration inside systems.

**Persistence.** `Persistence/SaveGame` DTOs (versioned, currently v17 — v17 Phase 14 planets/export batches/goods categories (additive, no migration: old saves are irrelevant by the user's decision), v16 Phase 13 research, v15 Phase 12 commands/squadrons/hangar/rental, v9 Phase 6 multirole, v10 Phase 7 quality/upgrades, v11 Phase 8 construction/breaker/warehouse, v12 Phase 9 actors, v13 Phase 10 gate access/sector control, v14 Phase 11 police/wanted/pirates, see the phase sections; `MigrateV1ToV2` converts the old field-owned ore into asteroid ore, `MigrateV2ToV3` the single-ore fields/asteroids into `legacy_<good>` classes (mine orders now reference a field index), `MigrateV3ToV4` assigns ships their `ModelKey` (unit model by role/cargo) and injects the default-world slot content, `MigrateV4ToV5` fuel + refineries, `MigrateV5ToV6` module goods/factories/kit tiers (Phase 5a), `MigrateV6ToV7` moves the player ship's credits into the player wallet (Phase 5b), `MigrateV7ToV8` adds outfitter/ship-dealer content, the trade station and the skiff models (Phase 5c), `Migrate` hook in `SaveGameJson`) are separate from the runtime model; `UniverseSerializer` converts both ways (slot content sections included, `Ship.RecomputeStats` after loading); derived state (Events, Tracker, caches) is rebuilt. When adding persistent state to `Universe`/`Ship`/`Station`, update DTOs + serializer + `UniverseHash` and bump `SaveGame.CurrentVersion` with a migration if the format breaks. `OfflineCatchUp` replays missed time (capped at 10k ticks, `SimClock` = 4 ticks/s; ~0.3 ms/tick on desktop; since Phase 11 without raids on the player, who cannot react offline).

**Navigation/avoidance/collision.** `Navigator.Next` gives the player autopilot the next waypoint (route gates + `PathPlanner`). `Collision.Resolve` (circles) is applied to the player in the view (`PlayerShip`): hard impacts on asteroids/stations (> `HardImpact`) damage the hull unless the ship is in `Grace` (after undock/respawn/jump); NPC ships only push softly and never damage; a lateral-damping flight assist and braking keep the ship from drifting into obstacles, and the autopilots slow down near obstacles (`Main.ClearanceScale`). Docking makes the ship fly into the station, become invisible and collision-free and puts out any fire — since Phase 26 it no longer repairs the hull (repairs only through the `repair` service; the slow hull regeneration in flight is gone too); undocking reverses it; `Avoidance.Desired` is a purely cosmetic sidestep between NPCs/player in the view. Neither feeds back into the simulation, so two NPC fleets may still cross in the data model. `PathPlanner` recursion is bounded by `MaxDepth` and a `MaxExpansions` node budget. Keep asteroid sizes in the view equal to `Obstacle.Radius`.

**Asteroids, ore and respawn.** `Asteroid` (Model/Asteroid.cs) carries its own finite ore (`Ore`/`MaxOre`) and a `ClassKey`; `AsteroidClass` (content) defines a weighted ore mix (`Yields`), `ChunkChance` per shot and `OreFactor` (richness). Ore type is rolled **per chunk**, not per asteroid. Asteroids shrink while mined (`Radius` depends on remaining ore) and are removed at 0 (`AsteroidDepleted`). Ore-bearing asteroids belong to the belt of the nearest `ResourceField` with the same class (`Sector.FieldFor`, `MiningRules.BeltRadius`); background rocks have no class/ore and are just obstacles. A field is only a belt descriptor (`ClassKey`, `Position`, `TargetAsteroids`, `RespawnTicks`); its ore is the sum of its asteroids (`Belt.OreIn`). `AsteroidRespawnSystem` (ResourceModule, interval 10) regrows missing belt asteroids with random ore, deterministically via `Universe.Rng`, never within 45 units of the player ship. Placement rules are shared by world generation and respawn (`BeltPlacement`).

**Player mining.** `Mining/OreChunks` is a real-time, non-persisted micro-simulation for the player's sector: the laser is fired **one shot at a time** (`TryFire`; a trigger that arrives during the reload time `ShotInterval` returns `Cooldown`, holding does nothing — the automatic mode simply re-triggers every frame). Every shot goes through the shared rule `Mining/LaserShot.Roll`: with the asteroid class's `ChunkChance` it releases a chunk of a random ore type (class weights) whose size is random (`MiningRules.ChunkTable`, small common, large rare), capped by the asteroid's ore. Each shot also gives the shooter a **recoil** impulse (`RecoilImpulse`, away from the asteroid): real velocity change for the player (`Main.OnShot`), a decaying visual kick for NPC markers (`SectorView.Kick`) because NPC positions are simulation data. The ore leaves the asteroid when the chunk is released; the tractor beam (`Update`) pulls chunks in and loads them (partially if the cargo is nearly full); uncollected chunks expire and their ore is lost. `Mining/MiningAutopilot` is the automatic mode (seek asteroid → approach → fire + tractor → when full go to the best buyer, dock, sell → repeat; other sector if this one is empty; since Phase 18 cargo below `MiningAutopilot.RestUnits` (1 unit) counts as a rest, not a load — no buyer trip, no stop); it only returns a `MiningStep` (what the view should do), so it is tested with a kinematic harness in `MiningTests`. `Trading/TradeActions.SellAll/Quote/BestBuyer` implement selling with the same payout rules as NPC trade. NPC miners fire the same shots inside the simulation (`ShotTimer` reload, `u.Rng`, max one shot per tick, ore taken from the asteroid into cargo, `MinerShot` event per shot). A `MineOrder` names a belt (`Field` index) and a destination station; since chunks are mixed, the miner scores stations by the expected value of the mix (`MixValue`), sells what the station accepts via `TradeActions.SellAll` and drives leftovers to the next best buyer (or dumps them); fields with other miners are penalised (`CrowdPenalty`, herd protection). The view draws NPC lasers/flying chunks/recoil only from those `MinerShot` events when they happen in the player's sector, so what you see is exactly what the simulation did. Expected NPC yield: `MiningRules.ExpectedOrePerSecond`.

**Diagnostics.** `EconomyStats.Snapshot` and `RouteRecorder` (event-based) feed the in-game economy panel and `tools/SpaceSim.Balance`. Since Phase 15 `Diagnostics/MarketWatch` samples every market entry and station cash (cap shares, empty cash, goods without trips) for the balance tool's HINWEIS block — the price average per good hides permanent states. Use the balance tool after any economy change: it caught money explosions and herding that unit tests missed.

## Godot project (`godot/`)

`godot/SpaceAdventure.csproj` (Godot.NET.Sdk/4.7.2) references the Core and is deliberately **not** in `SpaceSim.slnx`. `scripts/Main.cs` is only the composition root (wiring, steering priority throttle slider/turn buttons > pointer > autopilot, gate jumps, docking). Components: `Game/GameSession` (universe + fixed-step simulation), `Game/SaveService` (user:// file, atomic write; `UseFile` for tests), `World/SectorView` (builds sector from Core data, renders NPC ships), `World/PlayerShip` (flight model, collision), `World/MiningView` (laser, tractor, chunks, target ring, NPC mining visuals), `World/Fx`/`Sfx` (particles; procedurally generated audio, no asset files), `Ui/Hud` (composition root) + `Ui/Kit/` (reusable building blocks: `UiKit` palette, `KitButton` with states, `KitWindow`, `KitBar`, `KitSlider`, `KitConfirm` (two-tap confirmation, Phase 18), `IScreen`/`ScreenRegistry`) + `Ui/Screens/` (`FlightHud` flight HUD, `CommandMenu` hamburger menu, dock screens, and the command center of UI.md section 4: `CommandCenter.cs` base — full-screen with left rail (`CommandAreas`), content right, "← Flight" bottom left — plus one class per area: `UniverseScreen` (sector map from Core data, switchable layers faction/ships/prices/security, route via `Universe.FindRoute`, "plan route/autopilot" sets the Main autopilot target), `SectorScreen` (top-down sector view: stations, belts, gates, NPC ships via `Materializer`, player), `FactionsScreen` and `MyFactionScreen` (real actor data since Phase 9: actors, treasuries, size class, relations, personality), `FleetScreen` (Phase 12: real fleet list, command grid from the executor registry, targets, automation limits, rules of engagement, squadrons, remote switch), `AssetsScreen` (tabs + ship detail window; Phase 12: remote switch, commands, send home, sell, real berth fee and value), `CommsScreen` (placeholder inbox/negotiation, counter badge on the hamburger menu), `SystemScreen` (save/load/new game with start-package chooser, Phase 5c)) + `Ui/Panels` (map, economy debug, dock) + `UiPlaceholders.cs` (the single place for invented display values: comms messages, faction events, upkeep/income of "Meine Fraktion" — dimmed and labelled, never saved), `Ui/TouchButton` (multi-touch aware; `Ui/Pointer` unifies mouse and touch — on touch devices only touch events are handled). Plain primitive meshes are used; there are no art assets yet. Autosave every 30 s and on pause/close. `SectorView.SyncAsteroids` mirrors the Core asteroid list every frame (growing on respawn, shrinking while mined, bursting when depleted). The `--selftest` asserts zero hard collisions during autopilot, auto-mining and selling, plus slider behavior (50 % accelerates, reverse capped at 20 %) and the weapon button (reloading after a shot); it also opens and refreshes every command-center area without exception, checks that the universe sector selection yields the hop count matching `Universe.FindRoute`, that "plan route" sets an autopilot target and that "← Flight" closes everything; and checks the orbit camera (default view == old fixed offset (0,38,24), rotated camera still looks at the ship at the set distance, screen→world roundtrip of the ship center with a rotated camera, the menu action "cam_reset" resets, swipes steer nothing, invert/zoom/target-lock menu actions work). Since Phase 14 the selftest and the UI shots build their world with fuel and research switched on (`GameSession.New(configure)`), and the selftest ends with a new game from the default world (both off) checking that the fuel gauge, the emergency call, the research area/rows are gone and that planets and the export sink show up. `--ui-shots <dir>` (not headless, needs DISPLAY) loads a demo world and saves review PNGs (flight HUD, HUD with rotated camera, hamburger menu, dock areas, command-center areas) via `RenderingServer.FramePostDraw` + `Viewport.GetTexture().GetImage().SavePng()`. The pure Core display functions for the command center live in `SpaceSim.Core/Trading/SectorEconomy.cs` (`PriceLevel` per sector, `TotalValue` = the ship's own credits + cargo at base prices — a player ship has no own credits since Phase 5b —, `PlayerNetWorth` = player wallet + value of all player ships) with unit tests. All player-money displays (HUD, dock screens, command center, selftest) read `Universe.WalletOf(ship)` / `Universe.PlayerWallet`, never `Ship.Credits`.

Space plane is XZ (`Vec2.Y` → world Z), tilted perspective camera for 2.5D. Controls (UI.md section 2): ◀ ▶ turn buttons bottom left (hold to turn, keys A/D or arrows), horizontal throttle slider bottom right from STOPP to MAX (forward only; it stays until changed, key W/up raises it, X = STOPP) and a separate hold button "RÜCK" between the turn buttons and the slider (80×56 px, half the old edges but the 56 px minimum touch target) for reverse (max 20 % of max speed, takes precedence over the slider; keys S/down; pure mapping in `SpaceSim.Core/Flight/Throttle.cs`). Camera: freely orbitable around the ship and always aimed at it — pure Core math in `SpaceSim.Core/Flight/OrbitCamera.cs` (yaw full 360°, pitch clamped 15–85° above the plane, distance clamped, `Reset`, soft `Follow`; the default view equals the old fixed offset (0, 38, 24)) with unit tests; Godot only applies `cam.Position = ship + Follow(offset)` + `LookAt(ship)`. Input: touch/mouse never steers the ship. One finger or left-mouse drag orbits ("grab the world": swiping right moves the camera left; `OrbitCamera.InvertDrag` flips it), a short tap (< 14 px, < 0.45 s) selects a target; two fingers pinch-zoom; right/middle mouse drag also orbits, wheel zooms. The hamburger menu (`CommandMenu`, bottom left) holds the camera settings: zoom −/+, free camera or **target lock** (`CameraMode.TargetLock`: `OrbitCamera.FollowTarget` rotates the yaw so the selected asteroid, else the autopilot target in the same sector, lies ahead of the ship; horizontal drag then has no effect), invert drag, reset. The "Kommando" button top right (next to map/time, shows the comms counter) opens the command center. The camera setting is not saved in the save game and is kept across docking/undocking and jumps; while docked/menus/full-screen screens are open, camera gestures are ignored (input belongs to the UI). Everything screen↔world (picking stations/gates/asteroids, hold-to-fly pointer, target marker, firing-arc cones) is computed with the rotated camera (`UnprojectPosition`/ray-plane); the arc overlay draws around the projected ship nose. Godot's Y rotation is counter-clockwise, so turning right *decreases* `PlayerShip.Heading` (`PlayerShip.HeadingChange`, guarded by a selftest check); touch/mouse never steers the ship: one finger or left mouse drag orbits the camera, a short tap selects a target (station/gate/asteroid). Priority: slider/buttons > pointer > autopilot; manual input cancels autopilot and auto-mining. Weapon bays on the right edge, one button per bay (laser, tractor): state (ready/outside arc with turn hint/reloading bar/no target) is the pure Core function `SpaceSim.Core/Weapons/FiringArc.cs` over `WeaponBay` data (facing/arc/range; the mining laser is a 360° turret so gameplay is unchanged), the firing arcs are drawn as cones around the ship, "Alle Feuer" fires only ready in-arc weapons. Top bar: hull, shield, fuel, power and credits real (Phase 23; below the credits three 96×56 power-distribution buttons Waffen/Schild/Antrieb, tapping the active one = balanced), sector chip, map and time-lapse buttons, target card with autopilot/auto-mining switches and the context button ("Andocken" near a station, "Springen" at a gate). Mining: tap an asteroid to select it, the laser bay button (key F) fires once per shot (green when reloaded, grey with bar while reloading), the tractor bay (key G) toggles the tractor beam, "Auto-Mining" (key M) runs the automatic mode; slider/turn input cancels it. Docking opens the dock menu and full-screen dock areas (UI.md section 3, `godot/scripts/Ui/Screens/`: `DockMenu`, `DockAreaScreen` + `TradeScreen`/`StationInfoScreen`/`HangarScreen`/`RepairFuelScreen`/`OutfitterScreen`/`ShipyardScreen`/`PlaceholderAreaScreen`; the area list is built from the data in `DockAreas`, the service registry instance lives on `GameSession.Services`, screens are registered in `ScreenRegistry`). The trade area draws its rows purely from `IStationService.Offers` and executes only via `IStationService.Execute` (`buy:<id>`/`sell:<id>`/`sell_all`; the UI never calls `TradeActions` directly), with list/tile toggle, tappable rows (quantity stepper, sum, buy/sell), the result message from `ServiceResult.Message`, a ship column (cargo, hold, credits real) and the best known selling station per good via `SpaceSim.Core/Trading/MarketInfo.cs` (`BestBuyerForGood`, `Classify` for scarcity/glut, with tests). Areas whose service the station does not offer are dimmed and open a short placeholder page (texts centralized in `UiPlaceholders.cs`); missions do not exist yet ("Bald"), the taxi is real since Phase 12; "Reparatur & Tanken" is real since Phase 4c, "Ausrüster"/"Werft" since Phase 5c (see the Phase 5c section). Undo and price trends in trade and the hangar is real since Phase 12 (own and foreign ships, switch/home/sell, rental); reputation, customs and lock level in the station info are real since Phase 10. Dock/undock logic (fly-in, hull repair, grace, autosave) stays in `Main.cs`, only the UI changed; "Abdocken" works from every area.

**Station models (round 8).** `World/StationModels` loads `res://assets/stations/<typeKey>.glb` (Blender export from `tools/blender/`, one mesh, origin centred, plane radius exactly 1) once per type (PackedScene cache) and instantiates it per station in `SectorView.BuildStation`, scaled to `Station.BodyRadius` (= `Obstacle.Radius`; the model never reaches beyond the collision circle, which equals the plane radius). Build sites use `build_site`; types without a model (custom worlds) keep the old primitive body. The material `Accent` gets the owner's colour (`SectorView.AccentOf`: faction colour as on the maps, outlaws red, unowned/free grey) through surface override materials cached per (source material, variant) — meshes and original materials stay shared; abandoned sites are desaturated and dark, disabled stations ("AUS") dark with emission off; `SectorView.SyncStations` re-applies only when owner colour or state changes (no rebuild). Station labels sit 2.5 above the model's top. Shadows stay off; ambient light slightly raised plus a weak shadowless fill light from the opposite side. The `.glb.import` files are checked in, `.godot/` stays ignored, the Android preset exports all resources. Selftest stage 89 (three busiest sectors: every station has its model, plane radius ≈ `Station.BodyRadius` ±10 %, accent = owner colour, disabled darkens without rebuild, overrides shared); `--ui-shots <dir> --ui-shots-stations` writes 90 (yard/HQ in Delta), 91 (pirate hideout), 92 (busiest sector).

**Jumpgate models.** `World/GateModels` loads `res://assets/gates/gate.glb` (tunnel with customs station, opening radius 1, pass-through along Z, station at +X; `tools/blender/gate_model.py`, `gate.json`) and `gate_destroyed.glb` once and instantiates one per gate in `SectorView.BuildGate` (partial `SectorView.Gates.cs`), scaled 10.5 (opening ≈ the 10-unit jump radius; the station stays inside the 35-unit asteroid clearance), rotated so the pass-through axis points from the sector interior to the gate. The look comes from Core data, read only (`GateModels.LookOf`: `Gate.State`, plus "denied for you" via `AccessInfo.ForGate`): field shader `shaders/gate_field.gdshader` (unshaded additive; open blue pulse with waves, restricted orange warning stripes, denied denser/redder, closed only a faint red lock grid), Glow/Signal colours from gate.json, Accent = sector owner's faction colour, destroyed = wreck model with the ember shader. Overrides are shared per (material, look); `SectorView.SyncGates` re-evaluates per tick/access change — no rebuild. No model → the old ring. Selftest stage 90; `--ui-shots <dir> --ui-shots-gates`. Gate labels use the same label rules as stations (fixed screen size, hidden under the HUD) and carry the tag of the target sector's owner ("Jumpgate → [UNI] Beta"). After new or changed `.glb` files run `godot-mono --headless --path godot --import` once, otherwise headless runs cannot load the models (the selftest then fails on stage 90).

**Android:** `godot/export_presets.cfg` has an "Android" preset (arm64, debug keystore from editor settings, SDK paths already set in the user's editor settings). The .NET export templates are installed (`~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/4.7.2.stable.mono/`), `godot/project.godot` enables ETC2/ASTC (required by the export) and `godot/SpaceAdventure.sln` must exist (without it the C# assemblies are not compiled into the APK). The debug APK is ~100 MB (arm64, signed with the debug keystore); it has not been tested on a device (touch input, performance; C# on Android is experimental). Export command: `godot-mono --headless --path godot --export-debug "Android" ../build/space-adventure.apk` (the "No project icon specified" message is harmless). `/tmp` is a small volume: keep big downloads on `/home`.

**Station services (`Services/`).** `IStationService` (`Key`, `IsAvailable`, `Offers`, `Execute`) with value types `ServiceOffer` (key, text, price, amount, good, stock, held), `ServiceRequest` and `ServiceResult`; `StationServices` is the registry (registration order, duplicate key throws, no static state; `Default()` registers `trade`, `refuel`, `repair`, `outfitter`, `shipdealer`, `upgrade`, `storage`, `court`, `bounty`, `hangar`, `taxi`, `rental`, `research` (Phase 12; `research` Phase 13; `upgrade` since Phase 7: a successful `upgrade` call publishes `ServicePerformed` also when the roll fails, because material and credits are consumed), `storage` (Phase 8, warehouse)). `TradeService` ("trade") wraps `TradeActions.Buy/Sell/SellAll` unchanged. Offer keys are strict: `buy:<GoodId>`, `sell:<GoodId>`, `sell_all` (anything else fails without event or state change; the good comes from the key). `ServicePerformed` is published only on success, in addition to `PlayerBought/PlayerSold`. Station types list their services (`StationType.Services`, JSON `services`, default `["trade"]`; `WorldBuilder` rejects unknown/duplicate keys with `WorldFormatException`; saved in `StationTypeSave.Services`, old saves without the field load as `["trade"]`, no version bump). `StationServices.ForStation(u, station)` returns the available services in type order. Wired into the UI: the dock menu/areas (`Ui/Screens/`, registry instance on `GameSession.Services`) build their area list from `ForStation` and the trade area uses `Offers`/`Execute` exclusively (UMSETZUNGSPLAN Phase 1, step 3, done in UI stage 2).

## Planned: ship building (seams already in place)
Steps (1) and (3) are done: the kit tier comes from the ship class (`ShipClass.Kit`, Phase 5a) and ships are bought with the shared rule `Simulation/ShipPurchase` (kit + one module good per fitted module from the yard stock; player via the `shipdealer` service, AI via `FleetSystem.Expand`, Phase 5c). Step (4) is done as well: station construction consumes steel/hull plates/electronics through build sites (Phase 8). Original plan, in order: (1) a `ShipClass` content section (key, role, cargo, hull, speed, `kit` good + extra material costs) and one kit/recipe per class, so different hulls need different ore chains (e.g. miners need plating, freighters need life support); (2) `Ship.ClassKey` + save field; (3) player purchase at the dock panel (`TradeActions`-style `BuyShip`, pays the yard, consumes the kit); (4) station construction consuming steel/hull plates instead of only credits. Titanium and platinum chains are deliberately the gate for ship kits.

## Not yet implemented

Combat depth (since Phase 24 NPC raids and intercepts run as round combat, since Phase 25 the player fights in it in real time with real loss of the active ship — see the Phase 24/25 sections; since Phase 26 module damage, fire, module repair, wrecks and salvage; since Phase 27 police and pirates run through the order executors with squadrons, reinforcement, escort, pursuit, refuge escape and player surrender; since Phase 28 missiles, station guns, station attack/sieges, warships and war between factions with capture behind `allowStationCapture` — still missing: a black market for loot, tribute and alliances, government crisis after losing an HQ, player-founded factions, fleet commands "attack/pursue" for the player's own fleet), research beyond the first small project set (recipe unlocks, ore/goods unlocks, "improvements" such as reload/chunk-chance bonuses, research missions, early access for funders, station destruction effects — see Phase 13), tribute (war exists since Phase 28), the player faction (joining/founding factions, setting own locks), jump drive/cloak (deferred), missions, NPC use of the breaker beyond Rockbreaker II, station wear/decay (Phase 15 adds a permanent maintenance material demand, but nothing decays without it), player commands "attack/pursue" for the own fleet, factions commanding convoys across borders, real 3D models/textures, on-device Android testing.

## Phase 3c (English summary): laser tier and cargo form in the hold

**Laser tier.** `LaserShot.Roll(rng, cls, rock, chunkChanceMult = 1f, chunkSizeMult = 1f)` scales the chunk chance (`ChunkChance * chunkChanceMult`, clamped to 1) and the chunk size (`* chunkSizeMult`, still capped by the asteroid's remaining ore). The RNG call order is unchanged at factor 1, so determinism holds. The player path (`OreChunks.TryFire`) and NPC miners (`MinerSystem.Mine`) pass `ship.Stats.LaserChunkChanceMult/SizeMult`; a ship without a model has 1/1.

**Cargo form.** `ShipStats` additionally derives `CargoSolid`, `CargoLiquid`, `CargoAny` (sum of `capacity` over the cargo modules by `holds`; `Cargo` stays the total). New rule on `Ship`: `HasSeparatedHolds` (model with at least one solid/liquid cargo module), `CapacityVolumeFor(form/good)` (solid+any resp. liquid+any; total capacity without separated holds) and `FreeVolumeFor(u, good)` / `FreeVolumeForForm(u, form)` — a ship without a model or with only `any` modules behaves exactly as before (one shared hold, `CargoCapacity - UsedVolume`); with separated holds the `any` space is shared, legal iff `usedSolid <= solid+any`, `usedLiquid <= liquid+any` and `usedSolid+usedLiquid <= solid+liquid+any` (all in volume = units × VolumePerUnit). All former `CargoCapacity - UsedVolume` sites now use the per-good rule: `Ship.FreeUnits` (hence `TradeActions.Buy/MaxBuyable`), `TraderSystem` route planning (capacity per good, skips goods that don't fit at all) and the buy amount on arrival, `MinerSystem` (ore is solid: mine-time estimate, affordability, cargo-full check, per-shot load), `OreChunks.TryFire` (cargo-full check, in-flight chunks counted per form) and `MiningAutopilot.NearlyFull`. `CargoCapacity` remains the total. `Good.Form` is now persisted in the save (`GoodSave.Form`, optional string, additive — no version bump, old saves load as solid).

**Content.** `default-world.json` gained only test-example modules that no start-world ship uses: `cargo_solid_s` (holds solid, capacity 30), `canister_bay_s` (holds liquid, 30), `cargo_universal_70` (holds any, 70). Test-only ship classes/models (e.g. the `tanker_test` canister ship) are injected by mutating the default world JSON in tests, not in the default world.

**Tests.** New `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/CargoFormTests.cs`: `Roll` at factor 1 is identical to before (same RNG sequence/results), chance factor 2 raises the hit rate (fixed seed, 2000 rolls), chunk sizes scale and stay capped by the asteroid; player and NPC wiring use the ship's laser tier; solid-only ship cannot buy water (`MaxBuyable` = 0, `Buy` → `NoSpace`), tanker buys water but no iron, universal hold shares between forms, no-model ship behaves as before, every good has a form and every unit model carries all goods of the start world, and a long run with a test tanker in the world keeps the volume invariant for every ship and the tanker never carries or orders solids. Unit models are unchanged in behavior: all previous tests pass unmodified and the 20000-tick balance run is byte-identical apart from the timing on the hash line (hash value unchanged).

## Phase 4a (English summary): fuel data model, no consumption yet

**Good.** `propellant` ("Treibstoff", `form: liquid`, volume 1, basePrice 32 — between water 18 and hydrogen 36) is appended at the END of the `goods` list, so all existing `GoodId`s stay stable; `DemoUniverse.Propellant = new(23)` and `ContentTests` guards it. It has no recipe and no station trades it yet (Phase 4b): `ContentTests.EveryGood_IsProducedAndUsedSomewhere` exempts it explicitly, and the balance tool skips goods without any market entry in its stability verdict, so its column prints 0.0 and the verdict is unchanged.

**Content.** `ShipClassDef.baseFuel` (float, default 0; unit classes get 200), the free module stat `fuelCapacity` (e.g. later tank modules), and `ModuleDef.fuel` — a list of `{ good: <good key>, efficiency }` on drive modules, exposed as `Module.FuelTypes` (`FuelType(GoodId, Efficiency)`). `WorldBuilder` validates per module with the module name in the `WorldFormatException`: unknown good in `fuel`, good not `liquid`, `efficiency <= 0`, duplicate good. `chem_engine_s` names `propellant` with efficiency 1.0; `fuelUse` stays a plain module stat (consumption only in Phase 4b).

**ShipStats.** Pure function as before, additionally derives `FuelCapacity` (= class `baseFuel` + Σ `fuelCapacity` stats) and `FuelTypes` (union over all fitted modules, highest efficiency per good when several drives allow the same fuel).

**Ship.** `Ship.Fuel` is a `Dictionary<GoodId, float>` kept separate from cargo, total ≤ `Stats.FuelCapacity` (clamped in `RecomputeStats`, excess removed from the highest GoodId down); model-less ships have an empty tank and capacity 0. Helpers: `FuelAmount(good)`, `TotalFuel`, `FuelSpace`, plus `FillFuel()` (full tank of the first allowed fuel). New ships start full: `WorldBuilder.NewShip`, `FleetSystem.Expand`, and `Universe.AddShip` when the tank is empty.

**Persistence.** Save v5: `ShipSave.Fuel` (`Dictionary<int,float>`), `SaveGame.CurrentVersion = 5`, and `MigrateV4ToV5` — model ships get a full tank of their first allowed fuel (propellant in the default world); since the content sections in a v4 save are at v4 state, the migration appends `propellant` at the end of the goods list (GoodIds do not shift, station markets untouched — the good has no market yet) and upserts the changed slot-content entries (modules with `fuel`, classes with `baseFuel`) from the default world by key, the same way `MigrateV3ToV4` injected the slot registry (custom save entries survive); `ModuleSave.Fuel` stores good keys (like `cost`) so ids never shift, `ShipClassSave.BaseFuel` added. `UniverseHash` includes `Fuel` (hash value changes; nothing else). Old v2/v3/v4 saves load via the chain migration — verified against a real v2 save (every ship ends up with a full propellant tank). `Travel.Start` still charges the flat credit fuel cost; no consumption, no new stations, no economy change.

**Tests.** New `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/FuelModelTests.cs` (15 tests): all four `fuel` validation failures, `chem_engine_s`/baseFuel content, `ShipStats` values incl. tank module and multi-drive union, new/bought ships start with a full tank, model-less ship empty, `Travel.Start` still credit-flat with untouched tank, save v5 roundtrip incl. hash and persisted content, migration v4→v5 (v4 JSON synthesized from a current save) with behavior identical to a fresh world, and the v2→v5 chain from a synthetic v2 save. 265 tests green, `dotnet build` 0 warnings, Godot selftest OK, and the 20000-tick balance run is identical to before apart from the new `propellant` column (all 0.0) and the hash value on the hash line.

## Phase 4b (English summary): fuel economy — real consumption, refinery supply, no flat credit charge

**Content.** New station type `fuel_refinery` ("Treibstoffraffinerie", recipe 3 hydrogen + 1 energy → 4 `propellant` per 5 ticks; buildCost 9000 / upkeep 0.12 / income 1.5 like the other large refineries) and two start-world instances (id 25 in Beta at (-100,-60), id 26 in Alpha at (40,-115), `essential`, stock hydrogen 60/60, energy 120/100, propellant 100/100, default credits 10000), placed more than 101 units from every belt center and >50 from gates/stations so asteroid belts still reach their target count. `chem_engine_s` gains the module stat `fuelUse: 0.002`; `ShipStats` derives `FuelUsePerUnit` (sum of `fuelUse` stats). The dock shows propellant automatically through the existing `trade` service (market entries come from the recipe outputs) — no new service in 4b.

**Consumption.** `Travel.Start` no longer charges credits; it deducts `Refuel.Consume(ship, distance)` from the tank, where `distance` is the sum of the leg lengths (legs lie within sectors, so gate jumps stay free) and the units are `distance × Stats.FuelUsePerUnit / efficiency` of the active fuel. `EconomyRules.FuelCostPerUnit` stays as data only. New `Travel` helpers: `EstimateDistance` (straight-line in-sector distance), `PlannedDistance` (planned legs incl. detours), `EstimateFuel`. Player flight does not go through `Travel.Start`, so the player tank is untouched in 4b (tanking service comes in 4c).

**NPC refueling** (`Simulation/Refuel.cs`, pure functions over the universe, deterministic ordering): `ActiveFuel` (on-board fuel with highest efficiency, else first allowed), `Consumption`, `Consume`, `CurrentStation` (dock-point match), `FuelStations`/`NearestFuelStation`, `BuyFuel`/`TankUp` (market price, books stock, station credits, ship credits into the tank — not the hold — and publishes `FuelBought`), `EmergencyBuy` (2× base price, money sink paid to no station, rounds the amount up so the next fuel station is truly reachable, publishes `EmergencyRefuel`), `FuelPrice`/`TripFuelCost`, and `PrepareTrip` → `Depart` / `FuelStop` / `Stuck`. `PrepareTrip` treats the tank as sufficient only if it covers the trip **plus a reserve** (`MinFuelReserveFraction` = 0.75 × capacity) — otherwise refueling demand would hit all at once when the starting tanks run dry and no ship could reach a refinery anymore. It tanks at the current station if it sells fuel; else flies to the nearest reachable fuel station as a `GoToOrder` (tank on arrival in `TraderSystem`/`MinerSystem.Arrive`, then the real order is re-picked; a pending sell/deliver leg is re-attached to the best buyer); else buys emergency fuel and then flies to the fuel station; `Stuck` (broke / no fuel station) leaves the ship idling so `FleetSystem` abandons it as before. Both AI systems call `PrepareTrip` before every `Travel.Start` (buy leg, sell leg, field, delivery, leftover delivery). No ship may keep a ghost reservation: `GoToOrder` never reserves stock and is always paired with a transit.

**Route scoring.** `TraderSystem` subtracts `Refuel.TripFuelCost` (consumption of ship→buy→sell × fuel price at the current/buy station, else the average over fuel stations, else base price) from `margin × units`.

**Events / persistence.** New `FuelBought` and `EmergencyRefuel` events (bus only, not persisted). No new save fields — the save stays v5 (`Ship.Fuel` was already hashed and persisted in 4a), so save→load→continue remains bit-identical.

**Balance tool.** New `tank`/`notk` columns (cumulative `FuelBought`/`EmergencyRefuel` counts, also in the CSV) and a summary line: tank buys + cost, emergency buys + cost, propellant's average price ratio over the last 25%.

**Calibration.** `fuelUse` 0.002 with tank 200: 0.005 made propellant scarce (2.5–4× base) and squeezed the fleet, 0.003 hit the 3.0 warning and miners collapsed; 0.002 keeps the 20000-tick run green. 20000-tick balance run (default seed): exit 0, "Preise im Zielbereich", 14 traders / 8 miners (unchanged), 20 station tank-ups (24857 Cr), 0 emergency buys, propellant ≈ 0.61× base in the tail, energy and hydrogen columns in range (0.5–1.7, no degeneration vs the powerplant). Money: the old flat fuel sink is gone; fuel payments now flow ship→refinery, so ship credits end lower (~22k vs ~62k before) and station credits higher (~99k vs ~49k) — no knob was changed, the shift is the intended flow to the refinery.

**Tests.** New `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/FuelEconomyTests.cs` (8 tests): consumption counts only in-sector legs (gate jumps free) and leaves credits untouched, model-less ships consume nothing; tank-up at the current station with exact stock/credits/cost bookkeeping; station without fuel → fuel stop as `GoToOrder` + tank on arrival; emergency buy (2× base, sink, amount = ceil to reach the next station, then fuel stop) and broke → `Stuck`; refinery produces propellant (H2/energy down, stock up) and sells to a ship with correct credits/stock; trader scoring subtracts fuel cost (huge `fuelUse` → no order; control case picks one); determinism (same seed → same hash, save→load→continue bit-identical); 20000-tick invariants (no negative or over-cap tank, no ship in transit past its arrival, no ghost `GoToOrder`, tracker consistent, player tank still 200). `FuelModelTests`/`ContentTests` were adjusted where 4b changed their assumptions (refineries now trade propellant, `Travel.Start` is no longer credit-flat, tanks are not full after a run). 273 tests green, `dotnet build` 0 warnings, Godot selftest OK.

**Fuel supply fix.** `fuel_refinery` has a second recipe without inputs (base production, 1 propellant per 20 ticks) so fuel never runs out when the hydrogen chain stalls; over 60000 ticks propellant stays out of the warning band on all tested seeds (before: 4× base on 5 of 8). **Known weaknesses.** On some seeds food/life support tip into the warning band at 60000 ticks (traders fall into back-and-forth hauls of single goods; independent of fuel). Blocking non-producer→non-consumer trade saturates the whole economy (all prices ~0.3×) and was rejected.

## Phase 4c (English summary): refuel/repair services, player fuel, emergency call — plus fixes from the phase 1–4 review

**Services.** `Services/RefuelService` ("refuel"; offers `refuel:fill`, `refuel:25`, `refuel:50`, `refuel:100`; books through `Refuel.BuyFuel` at the station's market price into the tank, never the hold; available when the station stocks a fuel some drive module allows, amount and price > 0) and `Services/RepairService` ("repair"; offers `repair:full`/`repair:half` only while the hull is damaged; consumes `hull_plates` from the station stock = missing hull × `EconomyRules.RepairPlatesPerHull` (0.05, JSON `rules.repairPlatesPerHull`, saved with the rules, additive); the ship pays plate price × plates × `LaborFactor` 1.5 to the station; plate shortage → partial repair). Keys are strict like `trade`; `ServicePerformed` only on success. Station types: `fuel_refinery` has `["trade","refuel"]`, `plating_works` and `shipyard` have `["trade","repair"]`. Prices are 0 until the first tick has run (`MarketSystem`), so tests run one tick before using the services.

**Player fuel.** `Flight/PlayerFuel.ConsumeFlown(ship, distance)` (same rule as `Refuel.Consume`) and `ThrottleCap(ship)` (1.0, or 0.2 "Notantrieb" with an empty tank; 1.0 for ships without a fuel system). `Main._Process` multiplies the speed scale by the cap and books the distance the player ship moved per frame (not while docked/in transition). `Refuel.PlayerEmergency(u, player)` → `EmergencyResult`: only with an empty tank; buys at 2× base price (money sink, `EmergencyRefuel` event) the straight-line need to the nearest fuel station × `PlayerEmergencyMargin` 1.5 (min 5 units); too few credits → message, no purchase. The view checks "docked". Hamburger menu entry "Notruf" (`CommandMenu` action `emergency`, `Main.CallEmergency`).

**UI.** `Ui/Screens/RepairFuelScreen` (`dock.repair`, registered in `Hud`): rows purely from `IStationService.Offers` of `refuel` and `repair`, executes only via `Execute`, shows `ServiceResult.Message`. `DockAreas.Build` marks "Reparatur & Tanken" available when `ForStation` yields `refuel` or `repair` (sub label "Lager leer" when the type lists the service but it is not deliverable). Fuel gauge in the flight HUD (`FlightHudData.Fuel/FuelCapacity`, red below 20 %) and in the dock ship panel is real. Automatic hull repair on docking and slow hull regeneration in flight stayed until Phase 26, which removed both (the repair service is now the only way to repair). Selftest covers: fuel consumed by the autopilot flight, repair at the yard through the screen, refuel through the screen (station context pointed at a refinery), empty tank → throttle cap, emergency call. `--ui-shots` adds `11b-dock-reparatur-tanken.png`.

**Fixes found while reviewing phases 1–4.**
- `MigrateV4ToV5` now also adds the `fuel_refinery` station type and the default-world refineries (per default sector that exists in the save and has no refinery; default position or mirrored fallbacks clear of stations, gates and belt centres; obstacle rocks at the site removed). Worlds whose goods lack `hydrogen`/`energy` keys (very old saves) get only the input-less recipe. Verified against a copy of a real v2 save.
- `Refuel.PrepareTrip`: the hard requirement is the trip's consumption; the 75 % reserve is only the wish level for topping up where the ship already is. `Refuel.TankUp(u, ship, st, minUnits)` spends at most `FuelBudgetShare` (30 %) of the ship's credits beyond the trip need — before, AI ships spent all working capital on fuel and could not buy cargo. A detour to a fuel station happens only below `DetourFuelFraction` (25 %) and when the budget buys at least `MinWorthwhileStopUnits`. If no fuel station is reachable at all, the ship emergency-buys for the trip itself instead of being `Stuck`.
- A `Stuck` ship keeps its `IdleSince` (the planners reset it to -1 before `PrepareTrip`), so `FleetSystem.Abandon` finally removes broke, empty ships.
- `TraderSystem.PickOrder` scores with the revenue the buyer station can actually pay (`paidUnits × price − units × buy price`) instead of skipping stations that cannot pre-pay 25 % of a full load; that skip starved stations whose scarce input had become expensive (food at 4× base). `TraderSystem.CanAfford` remains for the miner.
- `fuel_refinery` has a second, input-less recipe (1 propellant per 20 ticks), see the Phase 4b note above.

**Balance after these changes.** Default seed: "Preise im Zielbereich" at 20000 and 60000 ticks, 14 traders / 8 miners, 0 emergency buys. Seeds 1–8: 5 of 8 green at 20000 and at 60000; the remaining warnings are mostly single goods in glut (~0.3× base). NPC behaviour changed on purpose (hash differs from 4b). Always build the balance tool before running it (`dotnet run` without `--no-build`, or `dotnet build -c Release` first): a stale Release binary silently reports old results.

**Known gaps (not fixed, see UMSETZUNGSPLAN Phase 4).** Fuel is barely noticeable for the player (a three-sector flight costs ~1 of 200 units); repair costs nothing in practice; no rescue/tow station; tank size comes from the class (`baseFuel`), not a tank module; `ShipStats.Upkeep`/mass/thrust are computed but unused, the player ship flies with the fixed `PlayerShip.MaxSpeed`.


## Phase 5a (English summary): modules as goods, three module factories, stocks, kit tiers

**Module goods.** `WorldBuilder.Build` appends one good per module behind the JSON goods, ordered by module key (ordinal), so all JSON `GoodId`s and the `DemoUniverse` constants stay stable (`DemoUniverse.FirstModuleGoodId` = 26 in the default world). Rules live in `Content/ModuleGoods.cs` (shared with the migration): key = module key, name = module `name` (new optional field, default key), `form` solid, volume = max(1, module mass), `BasePrice` = round(Σ cost × ingredient base price × `EconomyRules.ModuleMarkup`) (new knob, 1.3, saved with the rules), colour from the slot type (`slotTypes[].color`, new optional field). A JSON good with a module's key throws `WorldFormatException`.

**First module set (Mk1).** Existing unit-model modules keep all values (`reactor_mk1`, `chem_engine_s`, `mining_laser_1`, the cargo modules), so the start fleet is unchanged. New: `power_bank_s` (storage, powerStorage 20; steel 2, copper 2, fuel cells 1), `fuel_tank_s` (cargo slot, fuelCapacity 100; steel 3, titanium 1), `tractor_beam_s` (tool; electronics 2, wafers 1), `shield_mk1` (shield, shieldCapacity/shieldRegen as free stats; platinum 1, wafers 2, electronics 1), `armor_plating_s` (armor 40; hull plates 2, titanium 1). Shield/tractor stats are data only (no consumer yet). Prices 94 (cargo) … 497 (shield).

**Module factories.** Station types get an optional `moduleFactory { slots, maxTier, ticks, target, inputTarget }`; `WorldBuilder` expands it into one recipe per matching module with `cost` (inputs = cost, output = 1 module good, `ticks` 20), ordered by module key, and adds stock entries for the outputs (`target` 5) and inputs (`inputTarget` 10) unless `stocks` names them. Three types: `drive_works` „Antriebs- und Reaktorwerk“ (reactor, storage, engine, cargo), `weapons_works` „Waffen- und Schildwerk“ (weapon, shield, armor), `systems_works` „Systemwerk“ (tool, utility); buildCost 15000, upkeep 0.15, income 1. Instances: 27 Antriebswerk Alpha (-110,-90), 28 Systemwerk Beta (-60,-120), 29 Waffen- und Schildwerk Gamma (0,95), all `essential`, >101 from belt centres and >50 from gates/stations (Delta is full at 8 stations). Default-world rules raise `maxStations` 30→33 and `maxStationsPerSector` 8→9 so the lifecycle keeps the same headroom — without that the three factories used up the station cap, no electronics/wafer/copper producer could be built and electronics starved (life support/consumer goods at 3.5–4×).

**Stocks (storage without recipe).** `StationType.Stocks` (`StationStock(Good, Amount, Target)`, JSON `stocks: { good: [amount, target] }`, validated: known good, two values, target > 0) creates market entries in addition to the recipes; for recipe goods it sets start/target. `StationFactory.Create` applies them, so lifecycle builds get them too. `Station.IsStockOnly(good)` = in the market but no recipe uses or produces it. AI traders deliver to such entries through normal trade but never buy from them (otherwise modules shuttled yard ↔ factory and the factory bought its own product back at 4×). Player/dock trade is unaffected. First user: the shipyard stocks `reactor_mk1`, `chem_engine_s`, `mining_laser_1`, `cargo_universal_100` ([2, 5]) and `titanium` [10,10] / `platinum` [5,5] as input targets.

**Kit tiers.** `ship_kit` was renamed to `ship_kit_light` (same id 22); `ship_kit_medium` (24, 1200 Cr, volume 6) and `ship_kit_heavy` (25, 2100 Cr, volume 10) are appended. Shipyard recipes: light unchanged (2 hull plates + electronics + fuel cell, 20 ticks), medium (4 plates, 2 electronics, 2 fuel cells, 2 titanium; 40 ticks), heavy (6 plates, 3 electronics, 3 fuel cells, 2 platinum; 60 ticks). Medium/heavy start at [16, 5] (already above the overstock cap, so they do not draw hull plates away from light kits while no class uses them). `ShipClass.Kit` (JSON `kit`, saved, validated against goods; unit classes `ship_kit_light`); `FleetSystem.KitKeyFor(u, ship)` = class kit, else `EconomyRules.ShipKitGoodKey` (default now `ship_kit_light`). `FleetSystem.Expand` consumes the parent's class kit.

**Market/lifecycle/trader changes.** `MarketSystem` uses the highest willingness ceiling over all recipes of a station per input (identical for single-recipe inputs, which were all inputs before 5a). `StationLifecycleSystem` only considers goods a station type can answer (see Economy above). `TraderSystem` buys (and scores) only `min(capacity, stock, floor(buyer.AcceptableUnits))` — before, the rest of a full load was dumped unpaid above the buyer's cap, which with the small input targets of the factories pushed titanium/steel averages to 0.25× (60000-tick runs: 3 of 8 seeds green); with the cap 7 of 8. All three change NPC behaviour (different hashes).

**Persistence (v6).** New optional save fields: `SlotTypeSave.Color`, `ModuleSave.Name`, `ShipClassSave.Kit`, `StationTypeSave.Stocks`. `MigrateV5ToV6`: rename `ship_kit` → `ship_kit_light` (good and rule), append the two kit tiers, upsert slot types/modules/classes from the default world by key, append a module good per module without one (ordered by key; price from the save's goods, fallback default world), add the module-factory types (only types whose outputs are all module goods) and their default stations (shared `AddDefaultStations` helper, also used by v4→v5 for refineries), and for existing default types with fully resolvable recipes append missing recipes by output (shipyard kit tiers) and the type's stocks to type and stations. Worlds without good keys (very old saves) get only what resolves. Verified with the real v2 save (loads, 20000 ticks, 908 trades, fleet 14 → 14).

**Who takes the modules in 5a.** Only the shipyard stock (≈25–30 units over 20000 ticks per balance run, ~13k Cr); everything else stays on the factories' shelves at the overstock cap (≈0.31× base). Real consumers (outfitter, ship dealer) come with Phase 5c.

**Tests.** New `ModuleGoodsTests` (19): one good per module behind the JSON goods in key order, good properties (form/volume/price/colour/name), first module set incl. titanium/platinum, key collision, factory recipes = cost and every module in exactly one factory, start-world placement and belt counts, "every model is buildable" (fixpoint over recipes from asteroid ores, modules and class kit), moduleFactory and stocks validation, stocks entries incl. lifecycle build, traders deliver to but never buy from stock-only entries, max willingness ceiling, lifecycle filter, kit per class and fallback, FleetSystem consumes the class kit, v6 roundtrip + bit-identical continuation, determinism, migration v5→v6 (synthesized v5 → same ids and behaviour as a fresh world; realistic v5 without factories → factories, yard tiers and stocks added, fleet trades). Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 5→6, module count 8→13, propellant no longer the last good (`FuelModelTests`), `ToV4Json` now built on `ToV5Json`, `ContentTests` (kit key, all constants, kit/module goods exempt from "used somewhere", shipyard has three recipes), `Miners_DoNotMine_WhenPriceTooLow` forbids station builds (a new station would legitimately open a fresh ore market). 306 tests green. Selftest: the yard's trade area offers and sells a module good.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed: 20000 and 60000 ticks "Preise im Zielbereich" (exit 0), 13/8 traders/miners at 20000 and 14/8 at 60000, 0 emergency buys, propellant ≈0.33× in the tail. Seeds 1–8: 8 of 8 green at 20000 (13–14 traders, 8 miners), 7 of 8 at 60000 (seed 3: hull plates 0.28×); before 5a 5 of 8 each. Modules delivered to the yard stock: 28 (20000 ticks) / 49 (60000). Module goods sit at ≈0.31–0.33× base (overstock cap), medium/heavy kits at 0.31×. Simulation ~15 % slower (more goods in the trader loop).

## Phase 5b (English summary): player wallet — money belongs to the person, not the ship

**Model.** `Model/Wallet.cs`: `ICreditAccount` (one property `Credits`) and `PlayerWallet : ICreditAccount` (`Credits`, `Faction`; since Phase 9 replaced by `Treasury` of the player actor, `Universe.PlayerWallet` is now a read-only shortcut to it). `Ship` implements `ICreditAccount` explicitly over its `Credits` field. `Universe.PlayerWallet` holds the person's money; `Universe.WalletOf(ship)` returns the wallet a ship books on — `PlayerWallet` for player-controlled ships (`IsPlayer`), the ship itself for NPCs (implicit actors, unchanged). Phase 9 replaces that switch by "the wallet of the ship's owner"; callers already go through `WalletOf`, so ships of a future multi-ship player all book on the same wallet. Player ships keep `Ship.Credits = 0`; money stays with the person when a ship is lost or swapped, cargo and fuel stay with the ship (POLITIK.md "Spielerkasse").

**Who books via `WalletOf`.** `TradeActions.Buy/Sell/SellAll/MaxBuyable`, `Refuel.BuyFuel/TankUp/EmergencyBuy/PlayerEmergency` and the fuel-budget check in `PrepareTrip`, `RefuelService` (`MaxFill`, credit check), `RepairService` (offers/execute). Hence the `trade`/`refuel`/`repair` services book for the player on the wallet. NPC-only systems keep `ship.Credits` on purpose: `TraderSystem` (buy/sell on arrival), `UpkeepSystem` (ship upkeep and wealth tax — they only touch `IsEconomyAi` ships, so the player is treated exactly as before, i.e. not taxed), `FleetSystem`, `StationLifecycleSystem` (investors are `IsEconomyAi`). `WorldBuilder` puts `player.credits` into the wallet (faction = player faction) and creates the player ship with 0 credits. `FactionsScreen` faction wealth sums stations + ship credits and therefore no longer includes the player's money (the person is not the faction treasury).

**Persistence (v7).** `SaveGame.PlayerWallet` (`PlayerWalletSave { Credits, Faction }`), `CurrentVersion = 7`, `MigrateV6ToV7`: credits of every player-controlled ship (controller "Player", or role "Player" in saves without a controller field) move into the wallet and the ships keep 0; wallet faction = faction of the saved `PlayerShip`; NPC ships untouched. `UniverseHash` additionally mixes the wallet (credits, faction) — the hash value changes, nothing else. Verified against the real v2 save (wallet 1120.28 = old ship credits, ship 0, 20000 ticks, 872 trades).

**Diagnostics.** `EconomySnapshot` gains `PlayerCredits` (wallet + any stray player-ship credits) and `TotalCredits` (stations + economy-AI ships + player); the balance tool prints/writes a `plCredits`/`playerCredits` column, the in-game economy panel shows "Spieler".

**NPC behaviour unchanged — method.** (1) Before/after comparison: an identical temporary test (custom bit-exact hash over stations, markets, all ships incl. NPC credits, cargo, fuel, orders/phases, asteroids and RNG state, excluding only the player's money) ran 20000 ticks on `git archive HEAD` (5a) and on the 5b tree for the default seed and seeds 1–3: all four hashes identical. (2) The 20000-tick balance CSV is byte-identical to 5a apart from the new player column. (3) Permanent test `WalletTests.NpcWirtschaft_LiestDieSpielerkasseNie_20000Ticks`: two worlds with wallets 500 and 9,999,999 run 20000 ticks; after equalising the wallet the full `UniverseHash` is identical and the wallet was never touched.

**Tests.** New `WalletTests` (9): start money in the wallet / NPCs are their own wallet; trade (buy, sell, sell_all via `TradeService`) books on the wallet and keeps wallet + ships + stations constant; refuel and repair book on the wallet with the same conservation, the emergency call is the same money sink as before (paid from the wallet); credits on the player ship itself are ignored; NPC economy never reads the wallet (20000 ticks); v7 roundtrip + bit-identical continuation; migration v6→v7 (synthesized via `WalletTests.ToV6Json`, hash equal to the original), the v5 chain, and old saves without a controller field. Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 6→7; tests that set or read the player's money now use `u.WalletOf(player)` (trade, refuel, repair, emergency, mining autopilot, service failure snapshots); `TotalValue` test uses an NPC ship plus a new player-ship/net-worth test; the save demotion helpers (`ToV5Json`, `ToV3Json`) first call `ToV6Json`. Selftest: all money checks use the wallet, plus "trade books on the wallet, the player ship has no credits".

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Identical to 5a (NPC state bit-identical): default seed 20000 ticks "Preise im Zielbereich", 13/8 traders/miners, 22 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys; 60000 ticks green, 14/8, 0 emergency buys; seeds 1–4 at 20000 all green (14/8, 14/8, 14/8, 13/8), 0 emergency buys.

## Phase 5c (English summary): outfitter, ship purchase, trade station, skiffs, start packages

**Shared fit rule.** `Model/FitRules.Check(slotTypes, modules, class, slots, fit)` is the single pure rule for a valid fit (fit length = slot count, known module, slot type, module size ≤ slot size, required slots filled, power balance Σ output ≥ Σ draw and peak − surplus ≤ storage), returning a `FitIssue(Kind, Slot, Message)` or null. `WorldBuilder.LoadSlotContent` uses it for ship models (same messages, prefixed with the model name) and the outfitter uses it at runtime, so content validation and refitting can never diverge. `FitRules.SlotIssue`/`PowerIssue`/`Describe` are the parts.

**Outfitter service (`Services/OutfitterService`, key `outfitter`).** Available at stations whose market holds module goods. Offers per slot: `remove:<slot>` for filled non-required slots (Price = the station's buy-back price, 0 = module goes to the hold) and `fit:<slot>:<module>` for every module of matching slot type and size that is in the ship's hold (free) or in the station stock (market price). Amount = 1 only if the change is valid and affordable; `OutfitterService.Preview(u, station, ship, slot, module)` (pure) returns before/after `ShipStats`, the `FitIssue` and a cargo problem — used by `Execute` and by the UI. Rules: required slots (reactor, engine) only by swap; the swapped-out/removed module is sold to the station at market price when it accepts (`AcceptableUnits ≥ 1`, credits), else put in the hold; the existing cargo must still fit the new hold (`CargoProblem`, same form rule as `Ship.FreeVolumeForForm`); money via `Universe.WalletOf`. After the change `Ship.RecomputeStats`, `CargoCapacity = Stats.Cargo`, hull clamped to `MaxHull`. Any failure (strict key parsing, slot type, size, power, peak, required slot, cargo, stock, credits) changes nothing and publishes nothing; success publishes `ServicePerformed` and `ShipRefitted`. Ships without a model cannot be refitted. `OreChunks.TryFire` returns `MineResult.NoLaser` and the mining autopilot stops when the fit has no `Mining` module.

**Ship purchase.** `Simulation/ShipPurchase` is the shared buying rule: `Materials(u, class, fit)` = 1 kit of the class (`KitKeyOf`) + one module good per fitted module (aggregated, GoodId order; null if a good is missing), `Missing(station, needs)`, `Price` = Σ units × station market price (base price if not traded) × (1 + assembly), `Consume`, `Sells(u, station, class)`, `Create(...)` (new ship at the dock point with copied slots/fit, full hull and tank). Station types carry an optional `ShipDealerConfig` (JSON `shipDealer: { classes, maxSize, assembly }`, validated against the ship classes, saved in `StationTypeSave.ShipDealer`): the shipyard sells all classes (assembly 0.05), the new trade station only `skiff` up to size S (assembly 0.10). The refinery gets no ship dealer (its planned classes "light miner"/"small tanker" do not exist yet). **Service `shipdealer` (`Services/ShipDealerService`):** offers `buy:<model>` per model the station may sell (ordinal key order), Amount = 1 if all materials are in stock and the wallet can pay; `Quote(u, station, buyer, model)` gives stats, price, materials and what is missing. `Execute` (player only) consumes kit + modules from the station stock, pays the station from the player wallet, creates a player-owned ship (`Controller.Player`, role Player, wallet faction) at the dock point, publishes `ServicePerformed`, `ShipPurchased`, and makes it the active ship.

**Several player ships.** No new save field was needed: every ship with `Controller.Player` belongs to the player and books on the wallet; `Universe.PlayerShip` is the active one (now part of `UniverseHash`). `Simulation/PlayerFleet`: `Owned`, `ParkedAt(u, station)` (own, not active, no transit, same sector, within 30 of the station centre) and `SwitchTo(u, station, id, out message)` — the previous active ship is parked at its dock point, `PlayerShip`/`PlayerSector` switch, `ActiveShipChanged` is published. Parked own ships are ignored by all AI systems (role Player) and shown by `Materializer` like other ships. The view: after a purchase the shipyard screen updates `DockContext.Ship`; `Main.OnActiveShipChanged` (event) resets dock context, ore chunks and auto-mining; the hangar lists own ships parked here with "Übernehmen" (`Main.SwitchShip` → `PlayerFleet.SwitchTo`); the dock menu shows them as chips. Switching from afar (command centre) stays a Phase 12 topic.

**AI fleet (`FleetSystem.Expand`).** The AI prefers the same rule: if a reachable station that sells the parent's class has the kit and all modules of the parent's fit, it consumes them and the yard receives `ShipPurchase.Price`; otherwise it falls back to the pre-5c rule (kit only, `NewShipCost`), so module shortages never block fleet growth. `EconomyRules.FleetBuysModules` (default true; false = always kit only). New event `FleetShipBuilt(ship, yard, withModules, price)`; the balance tool prints "KI-Neubauten: n mit Modulen, m nur aus dem Bausatz". Without the fallback, seed 2 stalled at 12 traders because the drive works could not deliver engines (see weaknesses).

**Production order.** `ProductionSystem` now runs the recipes of a station that are due in the same tick in order of output scarcity (Σ market price / Σ base price, ties by list order). Only stations with several recipes are affected (module factories, shipyard kit tiers, refinery); before, the module key order let cargo modules on overstock eat the steel/electronics the scarce reactor needed. Changes NPC behaviour (different hashes).

**Content.** New station type `trade_station` "Handelsstation" (no recipes, `stocks` only: `ship_kit_light` and the skiff/outfitter modules at [1,3]; services `trade`, `outfitter`, `shipdealer`; buildCost 8000, upkeep 0.08, income 0.8) and one instance "Handelsstation Beta" (id 30, Beta (-120,60), the hub sector, essential); `maxStations` 33→34 so the lifecycle keeps its headroom. Shipyard services `trade, repair, outfitter, shipdealer`, `shipDealer.assembly` 0.05, additional stocks `cargo_universal_60` [2,5] (needed by AI miners) and `cargo_solid_s`, `canister_bay_s`, `cargo_universal_70`, `tractor_beam_s`, `fuel_tank_s` [1,3].
