Skiff class `skiff` (hull 60, base mass 6, base fuel 150, slots reactor/engine/tool/cargo/cargo/utility, kit `ship_kit_light`) with the Mk1 models `pip` (laser, 30 solid + 30 liquid, speed 8.0), `nomad` (no laser, cargo 70 any + 30 solid, 8.4) and `prospector` (laser, 60 any, 9.4). Start packages `player.startOptions` (`StartOptionDef`: key, name, description, model, credits, optional sector): `miner` = Prospector/300 Cr, `trader` = Nomad/1500 Cr, `generalist` = Pip/800 Cr; `WorldBuilder.BuildDefault(seed, startOption)` / `Build(def, seed, startOption)` (unknown key or model throws), `DefaultStartOptions()`. Without a choice the player entry applies as before (`unit_player`, 500 Cr), so all old tests and the selftest are unchanged. `WorldBuilder.NewShip` now sets `Hull = MaxHull` (skiffs have 60). In the UI "Neues Spiel" (command centre → System) opens a chooser with the three packages plus "Standard".

**UI.** `DockAreas.Build` enables "Ausrüster" and "Werft" from `ForStation` (`outfitter`/`shipdealer`). `Ui/Screens/OutfitterScreen`: slot list (type, size, required, fitted module), power balance and stats from `ShipStats`, offers of the selected slot only from `Offers` with source/price, before→after diff (cargo, hull, speed, free power, storage, tank, mining) or the reason it is not possible, execution only via `Execute`, message from `ServiceResult.Message`. `Ui/Screens/ShipyardScreen`: one row per `buy:` offer with price, stats, materials in stock or missing (from `Quote`), buy button. `--ui-shots` adds 11c/11d (outfitter cargo and tool slot), 11e/11f (shipyard at the yard and at the trade station) and 22b (start packages). Selftest at Werft Delta: areas enabled per `ForStation`, swap the cargo module via the outfitter screen (stats 100→60, wallet = −price + buy-back, wrong slot type rejected, swap back), buy a Prospector via the shipyard screen (kit consumed, new ship active, old ship parked and owned, dock context updated), hangar shows the parked ship and "Übernehmen" switches back.

**Persistence (v8).** `StationTypeSave.ShipDealer` (optional). `MigrateV7ToV8`: missing ship classes/models of the default world are appended (skiffs; only if their modules exist), existing station types get missing services, the ship dealer and missing stock entries (type and stations, price = base), and recipe-less stock types of the default world (the trade station) are added with their default stations (`AddDefaultStations`) when all their goods resolve; `MaxStations` += stations added. Verified with the real v2 save (types without keys get no trade station; the yard gets outfitter/shipdealer but cannot sell ships without a resolvable `ship_kit_light`; 20000 ticks, 872 trades, fleet 14 → 14).

**Who takes the modules now.** (1) AI new builds at the shipyard (reactor, engine, cargo module, laser for miners) whenever the yard has them in stock — 2–4 of ~7–10 builds per 20000 ticks, 18 of 28 over 60000 ticks; (2) the player via outfitter installs and ship purchases (yard and trade station stocks); (3) refilling the stock-only entries of the yard and the trade station (traders deliver; ≈70–90 module units per 20000 ticks vs. 28 in 5a).

**Tests.** New `OutfitterShipyardTests` (14): content (skiffs valid under `FitRules`, trade station, services, dealer classes, refinery without dealer), unknown dealer class throws, swap changes `ShipStats` and books wallet/stock/station, invalid installs rejected without any state change or event (slot type, size, power, peak, required slot, cargo no longer fits, strict keys, credits), removal to station or hold and install from the hold, no laser → `NoLaser`, player purchase (kit + modules consumed, yard paid, new ship active/owned/full, old ship parked and rendered, events, switch back, foreign ship refused), purchase refused without material or money and at the trade station for non-skiffs, trade-station assembly fee, AI with `FleetBuysModules=false` and the kit-only fallback, start packages, determinism + save roundtrip with two player ships, migration v7→v8 (synthesized via `ToV7Json`). Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 7→8, model/class counts, service lists of the registry/shipyard, `LifecycleTests` expansion price (the AI now pays the material price and consumes the modules). 330 tests green.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000 ticks: "Preise im Zielbereich", 14/8, 41 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys; 60000 ticks green, 14/8, 0 emergency buys. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green, 14/8 each, 0 emergency buys. Titanium/platinum (new demand through tanks, armour, shields, kit tiers) stay in range: titanium ≈0.3–0.5×, titanium ore ≈0.3–0.5×, platinum ≈0.7–0.9×, platinum ore ≈0.8× in the tail (5b: platinum ore ≈0.5×).

**Known weaknesses.** The module factories are under-capitalised (income 1, 10000 start credits sunk into overstocked modules nobody needs yet — power banks, shields, armour, extra cargo modules): when their electronics run out they cannot pay a trader's load at the scarcity price, so reactors/engines stay scarce (reactor 2–2.9× in the 20000-tick tails, below the 3.0 warning; at 60000 ticks ≈0.6×). Tried and rejected: traders delivering only what the buyer can pay (deadlocked poor shipyards, fleets fell to 9–11 traders), lower factory targets (0.29× warnings for modules without consumers), factory income 2 (small effect). Skiff prices at the trade station are high (≈4000–5000 Cr) while its module stocks are low. Ship switching only at the station where the ship is parked; no remote switch, berth fees, selling ships.

## Phase 6 (English summary): multirole — job assignment, capacity limits, new classes and models

**Jobs.** `Simulation/ShipJobs.cs`: `IShipJob` (`Key`, `Role` = the stored label, `Needs` capability, `Limit(rules)`, `Units(stats, cargo, rules)`, `FleetValue(stats, rules)`, `Score(u, ship)`) with `TradeJob` ("trade", `ShipRole.Trader`, `Cargo`, limit `MaxTraders`, units = cargo ÷ `EconomyRules.TradeCapacityUnit` 100, score = `TraderSystem.BestTrade`) and `MineJob` ("mine", `ShipRole.Miner`, `Mining`, limit `MaxMiners`, units = laser chance mult × size mult, score = `MinerSystem.BestMine` × solid hold = credits/tick). `ShipJobs.Default` is the registry (order trade, mine = expansion order as before); `ShipJobs.Of(model|u, ship)`, `IsMultirole`, `UnitsOf`, `UsedUnits(u, job)` (Σ units of AI ships with that role, id order). `TraderSystem.BestTrade`/`MinerSystem.BestMine` are the old `PickOrder` scoring extracted as pure evaluations (no behaviour change; the miner's ore rate now includes the ship's laser tier, identical at Mk1). Content: `shipModels[].jobs` (optional list; validated: known key, no duplicate, fit must grant the job's capability), runtime `ShipModel.Jobs`, saved in `ShipModelSave.Jobs`. Empty = player model / not an AI purchase candidate; one job = specialist; two = multirole. Unit models: `unit_trader_100/60` → `["trade"]`, `unit_miner_100/60` → `["mine"]`; skiffs, `unit_player`, Swift and Cistern have none.

**`JobAssignmentSystem`** (new `JobModule` "Jobs", registered before Resources/Trade so the executor picks the new job in the same tick). For AI ships whose model allows ≥2 jobs the fit can do, when idle (no transit/order, both phases idle, empty hold, `NextThinkTick` due): after `EconomyRules.JobMinTicks` (600) since `Ship.JobSince`, it rates each job with `Rated` = `Score / (1 + JobCrowdPenalty (0.5) × load)`, load = used units of the job (without the ship) ÷ limit (herd protection at job level); switches only if `ShouldSwitch` (pure: min duration, other > 0, other > current × (1 + `JobSwitchMargin` 0.25)) and — while the current job still earns anything — only into a job with room (`HasRoom`: used + own units ≤ limit + `FleetCapacityOvershoot`), otherwise multirole ships fled a full job and the fleet kept buying for the one they left. A switch sets `Role`, `JobSince`, clears order/phases and publishes `JobChanged(ship, from, to, fromScore, toScore)`. Specialists are never touched; `TraderSystem`/`MinerSystem` stay the executors (role filter unchanged).

**FleetSystem.** Limits count capacity units (`ShipJobs.UsedUnits`), not ships: unit models count 1 each, so the start fleet behaves as before; a Behemoth counts 5, a Tinker 0.6 when trading. Expansion per job (trade, then mine): the richest AI ship of that role (≥ `FleetExpandCredits`) buys via `FleetSystem.ChooseModel` (pure) when `FleetChoosesModels` (default true): candidates are models whose `jobs` include the job and whose fit has the capability; a candidate counts only if a reachable station selling its class has kit + all modules in stock (same `ShipPurchase` rule as the player), the buyer can pay price + `NewShipStartCredits`, and used + units ≤ limit + `FleetCapacityOvershoot` (0.5). Score = (`FleetValue(job)` + `FleetFlexWeight` (0.75) × Σ `FleetValue` of the model's other jobs) ÷ price, ties by model key. `FleetValue`: trade = cargo units × min(1.5, speed/8); mine = laser tier × min(1, hold/60) × (0.5 + 0.5 × min(1.5, speed/8)). If no candidate qualifies (yard without the modules, content without jobs, old saves), the pre-Phase-6 rule applies unchanged (clone the parent, with modules if in stock, else kit-only at `NewShipCost`). New ships start with `JobSince = Tick`; events `FleetShipBuilt` and new `FleetModelChosen(ship, model, job, price)`.

**Content.** New modules (as goods with factory recipes via the 5a mechanism): `chem_engine_m` (engine M, thrust 240, fuelUse 0.003, powerDraw 4; steel 5, electronics 2, titanium 1 → drive works) and `mining_laser_2` (tier 2, chunk chance ×1.3, size ×1.25, powerDraw 3; electronics 3, copper 2, platinum 1 → systems works, whose `moduleFactory.maxTier` is now 2). Both have small factory stocks [1, 3] and yard stocks [1, 2] (small targets bound the stock-only price at 2× and keep unused modules above the 0.3× floor; a first draft with `cargo_universal_200`/`canister_bay_m` and target-2 stocks produced 0.27× warnings for modules nobody buys, so those two were dropped). New `ShipClass.ToolEfficiency` (JSON `toolEfficiency`, default 1, must be in (0,1], saved in `ShipClassSave`, multiplies the laser chunk chance in `ShipStats`): the multipurpose hull of the light freighter has 0.85 ("shared power"). Classes (no combat classes; construction ship and asteroid breaker stay Phase 8 because their modules/mechanics do not exist): `courier` (S, light kit) — *Swift* (30 solid, speed 11.4, player only); `light_freighter` (M, light kit, base mass 16, tool 0.85, slots reactor/engine M/tool/weapon/cargo/cargo/utility) — *Mule* (120, engine M, 7.7, trade), *Hauler* (160, 5.5, trade), **Tinker** (laser Mk1 + 60 universal, empty weapon slot, 5.9, jobs trade+mine); `heavy_freighter` (L, heavy kit) — *Behemoth* (5 × cargo 100 = 500, engine M, 4.4, trade); `miner_light` (S, light kit) — *Pickaxe* (laser + 60, 8.4, mine); `miner_heavy` (M, medium kit, hull 200) — *Rockbreaker* (laser Mk2 + 160, engine M, 6.3, mine); `tanker` (M, medium kit) — *Cistern* (6 × canister bay = 180 liquid, player only). Every kit tier is used by a class. The yard (all classes) sells everything; the trade station still only skiffs. No extra material per model yet (kit tier + modules only). Default-world rules: `fleetFlexWeight` 0.75.

**Persistence (v9).** `ShipSave.JobSince`, `ShipModelSave.Jobs`, `ShipClassSave.ToolEfficiency`; new `EconomyRules` fields are additive. `UniverseHash` additionally mixes `Role` and `JobSince`. `MigrateV8ToV9` (key-based, additive, also runs for older chains): appends missing modules and a module good for each module without one, missing classes/models, copies `jobs` to existing default models without a job list, and adds missing recipes (per recipe, only if all goods resolve) and stock entries to existing station types and their stations (factories get the new module recipes, the yard its stocks). Verified with the real v2 save (loads, 20000 ticks, 872 trades, fleet 14 → 14; the v2 yard cannot sell models because its goods have no keys, so the fleet clones as before).

**UI.** The shipyard lists all 16 models (multirole marked "Mehrzweck"); selftest checks that the yard offers Tinker, Behemoth and Rockbreaker and shows one row per offer.

**Tests.** New `MultiroleTests` (15): content (classes, models, jobs, kit tiers, factories), invalid jobs rejected, `ShouldSwitch` rule, specialists never reassigned, Tinker switches mining → trading only after the minimum duration when mining yields nothing, 20000-tick run with four Tinkers (no two switches of a ship closer than `JobMinTicks`, every switch beats the hysteresis), generalist worse than specialist in controlled scenarios (mining: lone ship, ore per working tick Tinker 0.383 vs unit miner 0.467 (−18 %), Rockbreaker 0.845; trading: unlimited luxury route, Tinker ≈4500 vs unit freighter ≈9800 Cr/tick), capacity counting (Behemoth = 5 units blocks trader growth at 10 ships), model choice (Tinker wins a mining gap through flexibility, a specialist without flex weight; overshoot/money/no-jobs limits), `FleetSystem` buys the chosen model and consumes material, determinism + save roundtrip with Tinkers, migration v8→v9 (`ToV8Json` demotion), 20000-tick "no job dies out". Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 8→9, module/class/model counts, factory stock targets (new modules have their own), kit-tier test (classes now use all tiers), and the clone-rule tests set `FleetChoosesModels = false` (FleetSystem tests in `ModuleGoodsTests`, `LifecycleTests`, `OutfitterShipyardTests`); fuel tests compare against each ship's own tank capacity; the 20000-tick fit test accepts any bought model whose jobs include its role. 345 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", 14/8 traders/miners (14.6/8.0 units), 45 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys, AI model choice: 1 Hauler, 1 unit freighter; 60000: green, 14/8, 0 emergency buys, chosen 1 Hauler … 10 unit freighters, 2 Mules, 1 Tinker. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green (14/8, 14/8, 12/8, 14/8), 0 emergency buys. Multirole is rarely bought in these runs (0–1 Tinker per run, 0 job switches): mining gaps are mostly filled early by kit-only clones because the yard seldom has a laser in stock; with tool efficiency 1.0 (first draft) 1–4 Tinkers were bought and switched 1–23 times per run. Module bottlenecks (tail average price/base from the printed table, Phase 5c → Phase 6): reactor default 2.10 → 2.90, seeds 1–4 2.65/2.65/2.90/2.65 → 2.90/1.65/2.25/2.90, 60000 0.70 → 0.35; engine S default 2.60 → 2.70, seeds 2.05/2.05/2.10/2.05 → 2.00/1.35/2.05/2.70, 60000 0.50 → 0.40 — the same 2–2.9 band as before (mixed per seed), the factories' under-capitalisation is unchanged.

## Phase 7 (English summary): quality grades and upgrades

**Content.** `modules[].upgrade { to, cost }` → `Module.UpgradeTo`/`UpgradeCost` (validated after the models: target exists, same slot type, not larger, not itself, known goods > 0; saved in `ModuleSave.UpgradeTo/UpgradeCost`). Upgrade paths: `reactor_mk1 → reactor_mk2` (electronics 1, platinum 1), `chem_engine_s → chem_engine_s_mk2` (electronics 1, titanium 1), `mining_laser_1 → mining_laser_2` (electronics 1, platinum 1), `cargo_universal_60 → cargo_universal_70` (titanium 1, hull plates 1). New tier-2 modules (goods + drive-works recipes, `drive_works.moduleFactory.maxTier` 2, factory stocks [1,3], yard stocks [1,2]): `reactor_mk2` (powerOutput 14; steel 4, electronics 3, platinum 1) and `chem_engine_s_mk2` (thrust 190, fuelUse 0.0018; steel 3, electronics 2, titanium 1). `ShipClass.MaxSlotUpgrades` (JSON `maxSlotUpgrades`, default 1, heavy freighter and heavy miner 2, saved). The shipyard lists the new service `upgrade`. `EconomyRules`: `FounderQuality` (true), `QualityChances` [0.70, 0.20, 0.08, 0.02], `UpgradeChances` [0.9, 0.6, 0.3] by source tier (higher tiers take the last value), `UpgradePityStep` 0.1, `SlotUpgradeChance` 0.15, `UpgradeLabor` 0.5, `SlotUpgradeCost` {hull plates 2, titanium 1}, `BonusSlotCost` {hull plates 3, titanium 2}, `UpgradeMinCredits` 3000, `UpgradeBudgetShare` 0.2 (all additive in the saved rules).

**Quality grades (`Simulation/ShipQualityRoll`).** `Model/ShipQuality.cs`: `ShipQuality { Standard, Improved, Elite, Masterwork }` (German names via `ShipQualityNames`) and `UpgradeKind`. `RollGrade` draws exactly one `Universe.Rng` value; `Roll(u, cls, slots, fit, yard, baseNeeds)` applies the grade and falls back one grade at a time when it is not possible: Improved = one module upgraded (random among slots with an upgrade path), Elite = 2–3 modules upgraded, otherwise one non-required slot one size larger (slot material from stock), Masterwork = Elite plus a bonus slot (copy of the first cargo slot with the same module), bonus slots and enlarged slots limited by `MaxSlotUpgrades`; the final fit must pass `FitRules`. The extra cost comes from the yard stock: better modules, the bonus module and slot material must be in stock in addition to what the purchase consumes anyway, while the replaced base modules are no longer consumed (`QualityOutcome.Materials` = the full need); the buyer pays the normal price. Used by the player purchase (`ShipDealerService`, message names the grade), the AI fleet (both the model-choice and the clone path; the clone now copies the parent's *model* — class slots and factory fit — not its individual quality/upgrades) and the founder ships of the start world (`WorldBuilder`, AI ship groups only, without material; `rules.founderQuality` false disables it — tests that rely on the plain unit fleet use `QualityUpgradeTests.WithoutFounderQuality()`). The player's start ship does not roll. Event `ShipQualityRolled(ship, rolled, grade)`. `Ship.Quality` is saved and displayed; its effect lives in `Slots`/`Fit`.

**Upgrades (`Simulation/ShipUpgrade`, shared rule).** `Quotes(u, station, ship)` lists every upgrade with chance, pity, materials, missing stock, price, affordability and an issue text: `module:<slot>` (module → `UpgradeTo`, chance by the module's tier, the resulting fit must pass `FitRules`), `slot:<slot>` (non-required slot one size larger) and `bonus:<slotType>` (new empty slot of a non-required type present on the ship; slot upgrades share `SlotUpgradeChance` and the class limit — "Slot-Upgrades ausgeschöpft"). Price = material at the station's price × (1 + `UpgradeLabor`), paid via `Universe.WalletOf(ship)` to the station; material comes from the station market and is consumed on success and failure. Chance = base + `UpgradePityStep` × `Ship.UpgradePity[slot]` (key −1 for bonus slots), capped at 1 — every failure raises the next chance, success resets the counter, so with the defaults a module upgrade is certain after 1 failure and a slot upgrade after at most 9. `Attempt` rolls `Universe.Rng`, applies (fit, slot size, or a new empty slot + `null` fit entry), recomputes stats/cargo/hull and publishes `UpgradeAttempted(ship, station, kind, slot, from, to, success, chance, price)`.

**Service `upgrade` (`Services/UpgradeService`, registered after `shipdealer`).** Offers = one per quote (`Amount` 1 only when possible; `Held` = pity), strict keys, `Describe` for the text. `Execute` refuses (no state change, no event) unknown keys, issues, missing material and missing credits; otherwise it attempts and returns `Success` = executed, `Units` 1/0 = upgrade succeeded/failed, and publishes `ServicePerformed` in both cases (material and credits are gone either way).

**NPCs (`Simulation/UpgradeSystem`, new `UpgradeModule` after Trade, every tick).** Economy-AI ships with a model, idle (no transit/order), with ≥ `UpgradeMinCredits` and sitting at the dock point of a station whose type lists `upgrade` pick the module upgrade with the best expected gain per credit (Δ `FleetValue` of their current job × chance ÷ price, price ≤ `UpgradeBudgetShare` × credits) and attempt it with the same rule. NPCs never do slot upgrades (they do not refit).

**UI.** No new dock area (the dock menu has no room for a ninth tile): the outfitter area got a section "Verbessern" — for the selected slot the `module:`/`slot:` offers and the `bonus:` offer of that slot type, each with chance, material, price, pity and the reason it is not possible, executed only via `UpgradeService.Execute` (`OutfitterScreen.ExecuteUpgrade`; green on success, orange on a failed roll). `DockAreas` enables "Ausrüster" when `outfitter` or `upgrade` is offered (sub "… Module · Upgrades"). Slot list marks bonus slots and shows the quality; the shipyard header shows the grade chances; hangar (active and parked ships), assets list and ship detail show model and grade. Selftest: upgrade row shown for the engine slot, upgrade via the screen (engine Mk2, speed 8.0 → 9.5, credits and electronics consumed), unknown upgrade key refused, bought ship has a grade and the message names it. `--ui-shots` adds `11g-dock-ausruester-upgrade.png`.

**Persistence (v10).** `ShipSave.Quality` (string, omitted for Standard), `ShipSave.UpgradePity` (omitted when empty), `ModuleSave.UpgradeTo/UpgradeCost`, `ShipClassSave.MaxSlotUpgrades`; bonus slots live in the already saved per-ship slot list. `UniverseHash` additionally mixes quality, the slot list (type, size) and the pity counters. `MigrateV9ToV10` runs the shared `AddMissingDefaultContent` (since Phase 7 also adds missing services to existing station types; used by v8→v9 too), sets the upgrade paths on default modules without one and `MaxSlotUpgrades` of the default classes; ships load as Standard with no pity. Verified with the real v2 save (loads, 20000 ticks, 872 trades, fleet 14 → 14, the yard offers `upgrade`).

**Tests.** New `QualityUpgradeTests` (12): grade frequencies over 40000 rolls with a fixed seed within ±1 pp and same seed → same sequence; founder ships roll without material (stocks equal a world without the roll, deterministic, player ship Standard); player purchase "Improved" takes the better module from the yard and not the replaced one, price unchanged; "Masterwork" with full stock (bonus cargo slot, both Mk2 modules, cargo 200) and the fallback to Standard without material; AI new build rolls and consumes the better module; upgrade success (engine Mk2, faster, material/credits/station credits, events, no further offer); pity (failure keeps the module and still consumes material/credits, next try certain; with default rules +10 pp per failure, slot upgrade certain after 9 failures; tier chances 90/60/30 %); slot-upgrade limit per class (bonus slot added, further slot upgrades refused without any change/event, the bonus slot takes a module via the outfitter); invalid requests change nothing; `UpgradeSystem` (rich idle trader at the yard upgrades its engine, poor ships and the player never); save roundtrip with quality, bonus slot and pity bit-identical incl. continuation; migration v9→v10 (`ToV9Json` demotion). Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 9→10, module count 15→17, service lists (registry and shipyard now include `upgrade`; `ToV7Json` also strips `upgrade`), `MultiroleTests.ToV8Json` now demotes via `ToV9Json`; tests that assume the plain unit start fleet use the world without founder roll (`Startwelt_SchiffeHabenModellUndWerteWieBisher`, `Migration_V3ToV4…`, `Kapazitaet…`) or only check Standard ships (speed 8); the 20000-tick fit test accepts quality fits that pass `FitRules`. The Phase 6 mining comparison now counts ore per working tick (the lone ship otherwise idles for lack of demand; the earlier 6 % gap was noise). 357 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", 14/8 traders/miners, 26 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys; new builds rolled Standard/Improved/Elite/Masterwork 5/3/1/0, reached 6/3/0/0 (yard lacked material for one Elite); fleet now 15 Standard, 6 Improved, 1 Elite; 0 NPC upgrade attempts. 60000: green, 12/8 (trade 14.0/14 units: 4 Haulers), 0 emergency buys, rolled 19/7/1/0 → reached 21/6/0/0, 0 NPC upgrades. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green (14/8, 14/8, 11/8, 14/7 — trade/mine units 14.4/8.6, 14.4/8.6, 12.9/8.6, 14.3/8.3), 0 emergency buys, NPC upgrades 0, 3 (2 succeeded), 0, 2 (2). Mining units exceed 8 because founder miners with laser Mk2 count 1.625. Module bottlenecks (tool line, Phase 6 → Phase 7): reactor Mk1 default 2.86 → 2.86, seeds 2.86/1.66/2.28/2.86 → 1.54/2.77/1.84/2.86, 60000 0.35 → 0.38; engine S default 2.67 → 2.67, seeds 2.00/1.35/2.04/2.67 → 2.38/2.46/1.12/1.92 — unchanged band; reactor Mk2 sits at ≈2.5× (small stocks, little production).

**Known weaknesses.** NPC upgrades are rare (0–3 attempts per 20000 ticks): only rich traders that end a delivery at the yard qualify. Quality grades of new AI builds are often reduced by missing Mk2 stock at the yard. Slot enlargement only matters for players (bigger modules via the outfitter); NPCs never refit. Bonus slots from the upgrade service are empty until the player fits a module. No Mk3 modules exist yet, so the 60 %/30 % tiers are only exercised by the rule tests.

## Phase 8 (English summary): construction sites, construction ships, emergency build, Essential split, asteroid breaker, warehouse

**Build sites.** A build site is a `Station` of the content type `build_site` ("Baustelle", no recipe, service `trade`) with a `Station.Site` record (`Model/BuildSite.cs`: target `TypeKey`, `Required` bill, investor station/ship, `Budget`, `Builder`, `StartedTick`, `LastProgressTick`, `BlockedSince`, `AssemblyTicks`, `Emergency`, `Abandoned`). Being a station it is an obstacle, rendered, dockable and part of `StationsTrading`, so traders, the player's trade area and the dock UI work unchanged. Its market holds one entry per material: `Amount` = delivered, `Target` = required; `Station.AcceptableUnits` returns exactly the missing amount (0 when abandoned); `MarketSystem` bids a fixed `BasePrice × ConstructionPriceFactor` (1.25); `UpkeepSystem` skips sites (no upkeep, no wealth tax on the escrow); `TradeActions.Buy/MaxBuyable` refuse a live site (abandoned sites sell their stock); `EconomyStats` excludes sites from price averages and counts them separately (`EconomySnapshot.Sites`, `Builders`; `Stations` = finished stations). `StationLifecycleSystem` only decides *whether* to build: it excludes sites from the scarcity/glut measure, allows at most `MaxActiveSites` (2) live sites and never two of the same type, takes investors only from finished stations/AI ships, and — when `ConstructionRules.UsesSites` (rule `ConstructionSites` true, the site type exists and the bill resolves) — calls `ConstructionRules.StartSite` (investor pays `BuildCost × StationBuildCostFactor` into the site as escrow, `ConstructionStarted`; an abandoned site in the chosen sector is reused with its material). Otherwise the old direct build applies (very old saves without good keys). Sites count against `MaxStations`/`MaxStationsPerSector`.

**Bill (cycle-free).** `ConstructionRules.Bill(u, type)`: the type's own list (`stationTypes[].construction`, validated: known goods, > 0, never a good the type produces) or the rule: `BuildCost × ConstructionMaterialShare` (0.5) split by `ConstructionMaterials` weights (steel 0.4, hull plates 0.35, electronics 0.25) into units at base price (≥ 1); goods the type produces are dropped and their weight goes to the rest (steelworks need no steel, plating works no hull plates, electronics fabs no electronics). null = a material good is missing in this world (fallback to direct build). Content test: no type requires a good only it produces; every material has another producer type present in the start world.

**ConstructionSystem** (new `ConstructionModule` "Construction" after Trade/Upgrades, before Lifecycle; runs every tick). Sites: assembly runs (`AssemblyTicks += dt`) while everything is delivered and the assigned builder is parked on site (or after an emergency build); after `ConstructionAssemblyTicks` (200) `ConstructionRules.Complete` replaces the site in place by a station of the target type (same id/position/owner, `StationFactory` market and recipes, 2000 start credits as before), pays the builder `ConstructionFeeShare` (10 %) of the budget from the escrow, the rest of the escrow is the credit share (vanishes like the old build cost), publishes `StationBuilt` + `ConstructionCompleted`. Expiry: no progress for `ConstructionExpiryTicks` (8000) and not blocked → escrow back to the investor, material stays (`Abandoned`, "Verlassene Baustelle", reused by the next project in the sector, sellable to the player) or the site disappears without material (`ConstructionAbandoned`); abandoned sites without stock are removed. Builders (role `ShipRole.Builder`, capability `Construction`, `Ship.IsAiBuilder`) are the executors of the new job `BuildJob` ("build", limit `MaxBuilders` 4, 1 unit per ship, registered in `ShipJobs.Default` after trade/mine so `FleetSystem` can also buy them): they take an unassigned site (own faction first, then travel time), fetch missing material at the nearest supplier (`ConstructionRules.Suppliers`: finished stations that produce the good or hold it as stock-only — warehouse, trade station — never consumers' inputs, price ≤ `BasePrice × ConstructionMaxBuyFactor` 2.0), paid from the escrow, deliver (`ConstructionDelivered` "builder"), and wait on site for assembly; refuel via `Refuel.PrepareTrip` like traders; order `BuildOrder(Site, Supplier, Good)` (saved as kind "build"); leftover cargo is sold at the best buyer. Traders deliver through normal trade: `TraderSystem.BestTrade` subtracts what other traders already bring to a site (incoming reservation, sites only), the trade is booked as usual and `ConstructionRules.OnDelivered` marks progress (`ConstructionDelivered` "trader"/"player"); a trader whose seller no longer trades the good (site completed) is re-routed to the best buyer instead of dumping. A builder that loses its rig is released and the site becomes free.

**Emergency build (Notbau).** A site is blocked (`ConstructionRules.IsBlocked`) when no builder is assigned and none is reachable, or a missing good has no reachable supplier at the price cap, or the escrow cannot pay one unit at the cheapest supplier. After `EmergencyBuildTicks` (2000) blocked — × `EmergencyCriticalFactor` (0.5) for critical sites — and if the owner faction's `LastEmergencyBuildTick` is older than `EmergencyBuildCooldown` (3000, the brake: one emergency build per faction per cooldown), `ConstructionRules.Emergency` creates only the missing materials for `BasePrice × EmergencyBuildMarkup` (2) from the escrow (money sink; an uncovered rest is waived and reported), sets `Emergency` (assembly without builder) and the faction timestamp, publishes `EmergencyConstruction`. Blocked sites do not expire.

**Essential split (small).** `Station.Essential` is now `Station.BankruptcyProtected` (JSON `essential` stays an alias, `bankruptcyProtected` accepted; save field `BankruptcyProtected`, old `Essential` still read). `Critical` is derived, never saved (`Simulation/StationCriticality`): `IsCritical(u, station)` = the only reachable finished producer of one of its goods (such stations are not removed for bankruptcy), `IsCriticalSite(u, site)` = the target type produces a good no reachable finished station makes, or a good of `rules.criticalGoods` (steel, hull plates, light ship kit). The rest of the split (`Government`, `Capturable`, `Destructible`, `Immortal`) followed in Phase 10.

**Content.** Modules `construction_rig_s` "Bauausleger" (tool S, grants `Construction`; steel 4, hull plates 2, electronics 2) and `split_laser_s` "Spaltlaser" (tool S tier 2, stat `splitPower`, powerDraw 4; titanium 2, fuel cells 1, hull plates 1, electronics 1) as goods with systems-works recipes (factory stock [1,3], yard stock [1,2]). `ShipStats.SplitPower`. Classes `construction_ship` "Bauschiff" (M, medium kit, hull 150, two tool slots) and `breaker` "Asteroidenbrecher" (M, medium kit, hull 220, two tool slots, armor, `maxSlotUpgrades` 2). Models `foreman` (rig + 160 cargo, engine M, job `build`; second tool slot empty — the planned laser would make it a miner by capability), `quarry` (split laser + 60, player only), `rockbreaker_ii` (split laser + laser Mk2 + 160, reactor Mk2, job `mine`). `WorldBuilder.UnitModelFor(Builder)` = `foreman`. Start fleet: one foreman per faction (`ships[]` role `builder`: faction 1 in Alpha, faction 2 in Delta). Station types `build_site` and `warehouse` "Lagerhaus" (services `trade`, `storage`; stocks steel [30,40], hull plates [10,15], electronics [10,15]) with one start instance "Lagerhaus Gamma" (id 31, (-110,75), protected); `maxStations` 34 → 35.

**Asteroid breaker.** `Mining/AsteroidSplitter`: `Split(u, sector, rock, by)` splits an ore asteroid into 2–4 fragments of the same class (random weights, radius from the area share, ≥ `FragmentMinRadius` 1.2, too-small shares are dropped), ore × (1 − `SplitDustLoss` 0.1) distributed, placed near the origin inside the same belt with the placement clearances of `BeltPlacement` (asteroid spacing by current radius), at most `FragmentLimitFactor` (2) × `TargetAsteroids` per belt, sequential ids via `Universe.NewAsteroidId` (`NextAsteroidId` now hashed), all randomness from `Universe.Rng`; on failure nothing changes but the RNG. Event `AsteroidSplit(sector, asteroid, fragments, dust, ship)`. `Fire(u, ship, asteroidId)` checks split laser and laser range and returns fragments or an issue text. NPC: `MinerSystem` ships with a split laser split rocks with radius ≥ `NpcSplitMinRadius` (4.5) instead of shooting (Rockbreaker II). Player: `WeaponBays.For(stats)` adds the bay "Spalter" (2 s reload) when the fit has a split laser; `Main.FireSplitter` uses the same rule, the view mirrors the fragments.

**Warehouse.** `Services/StorageService` ("storage"): `store:<GoodId>` moves cargo into `Station.PlayerStorage` for `StorageFeePerUnit` (0.5) per unit paid via `WalletOf` to the station, up to `StorageCapacity` (500) per station; `fetch:<GoodId>` is free; strict keys, player ships only, `ServicePerformed` on success. For AI construction the warehouse is a supplier through its stock-only construction materials (traders fill it, builders buy there).

**UI.** Sites render as an orange scaffold (grey when abandoned) labelled "Baustelle: <type>" (`SectorView`), as a diamond with a material bar in the command centre sector view with status/material lines in the detail card (`SectorScreen`), and the station info shows target type, material %, builder and status (`Simulation/ConstructionInfo.Of`, pure, tested) plus "Schutz" (protected/critical) for finished stations. New dock area "Lager" (`Ui/Screens/StorageScreen`, `dock.storage`, rows only from `Offers`, execution only via `Execute`) replaces the Taxi placeholder tile at stations offering `storage`. Toasts for new sites/emergency builds in the player sector. The flight HUD moves "Alle Feuer" below a third bay. Selftest: build site in the player sector, dock, deliver steel through the trade screen (wallet credited, no buy-back), station info and sector view; split laser fitted → "Spalter" bay → split via the bay; warehouse in Gamma → store and fetch through the storage screen. `--ui-shots` adds 24 (flight with site), 25 (station info at a site), 26 (sector view with site), 27 (storage), 28 (HUD with splitter).

**Persistence (v11).** `StationSave.BankruptcyProtected` (old `Essential` still read), `StationSave.Site` (`BuildSiteSave`, together the save's build-site list), `StationSave.PlayerStorage`, `StationTypeSave.Construction`, `FactionSave.LastEmergencyBuildTick`, order kind "build"; new rules are additive. `UniverseHash` additionally mixes protection, type key, site data, player storage, faction emergency ticks, asteroid base radius and `NextAsteroidId`. `MigrateV10ToV11`: `AddMissingDefaultContent` (modules/goods/recipes/stocks, classes, models), adds the `build_site` type and the `warehouse` type with its default station (only if its goods resolve; `MaxStations` += added), one foreman per faction with a full tank (only if the model exists and the bill resolves), `Essential` → `BankruptcyProtected`. Verified with the real v2 save (loads, 20000 ticks, 872 trades, fleet 14 → 14, no builders because its goods have no keys, one direct-build fallback, roundtrip hash equal).

**Tests.** New `ConstructionTests` (22): content and start builders, cycle-free bills (and the `construction` validation), lifecycle starts a site with escrow/limits/no duplicates, direct-build fallback, builder fetches/delivers/assembles (fee, `StationBuilt`), traders deliver via normal trade at the fixed bid, player delivery via `TradeService` (no buy-back, never more than required), builder release without rig, emergency build after the deadline replacing only what is missing (cost = base × markup), critical sites earlier and the faction brake, expiry/refund/abandoned stock/reuse, derived criticality protects the last producer, `BankruptcyProtected` alias and old save field, splitting (2–4 fragments, ore minus dust, sequential ids, belt, determinism), belt limit and minimum size, NPC Rockbreaker II splits while mining, storage service (fee, capacity, strict keys, player only), display status and splitter bay, v11 roundtrip with sites/storage/emergency bit-identical, migration v10→v11 (`ConstructionTests.ToV10Json`), 20000-tick run (sites built, emergency share ≤ 50 %, fleet holds). Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 10→11, module/class/model counts, service registry and type service lists (`storage`), lifecycle/glut tests expect `ConstructionStarted`, tests over the "unit start fleet" exclude the builders (tank 200, speed 8, unit models), demotion helpers `ToV9Json`/`ToV7Json` first call `ToV10Json` and `ToV6Json` strips the Phase 8 rules, three purchase tests fix the quality roll to Standard (the extra founder ships shift the RNG), `LongRun_StaysBounded` checks capacity units (Phase 6 semantics) instead of ship counts, the Phase 6 migration test ignores the new module goods' ids. 379 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", 14/7 traders/miners (14.2/8.3 units), 39 tank-ups, 0 emergency fuel buys; 4 sites started, 4 completed, 0 emergency builds (0 %), 0 expired, material delivered builder 225 / trader 94 (steel 243, hull plates 51, electronics 25). 60000: green, 12/7 (14.1/8.3 units), 0 emergency fuel buys, same 4 builds. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green, 14/8, 13/8, 14/8, 17/7 ships (seed 4: 16.3 trade units through multirole job switches), 0 emergency fuel buys, 4 sites each, 0 emergency builds; 2–3 builders (the fleet bought a third foreman in seeds 2 and 3).

**Known weaknesses.** Material demand is not permanent: the lifecycle reaches `MaxStations` (35) after ~10000 ticks and stations rarely go bankrupt, so steel/hull plates are only demanded in the first build wave (≈310–540 units per run); a maintenance/wear consumer would be a later topic. The emergency build never triggered in the balance runs (only in tests); its share is measured. NPC breakers only exist if the fleet buys a Rockbreaker II (0 splits in the balance runs); Quarry is player-only. Founder ships with better quality grades are unaffected by the builder change, but the extra founder rolls shift the RNG, so per-seed numbers differ from Phase 7. The warehouse's player storage has no UI outside the dock and no fee over time.

## Phase 9 (English summary): actors and factions, treasuries, Zukauf, sparse relations, personality

**Model.** `Model/Actor.cs`: `Actor` (Id, Name, `ActorKind` State/Guild/Outlaw/Independent/Player/Free, `Membership` = `FactionId?`, `Treasury` (`Model/Wallet.cs`, an `ICreditAccount`), `ShipsUseTreasury`, `Personality` (Aggression, Greed, Cooperation, Lawfulness, 0..1), sparse personal `Relations`, `CreatedTick`) and `ActorSizeClass` (Single, SmallFirm, Firm, Corporation, State) with German names in `ActorNames`. `ActorId` (`Model/Ids.cs`): a faction's actor has the same id number as the faction (implicit conversion `FactionId → ActorId`); other actors get ids from `ActorId.FirstGenerated` (1000): the collective group "Freie Kapitäne" (`Universe.FreeActor`, 1000) and the player (`Universe.PlayerActor`, 1001), both created by the `Universe` constructor (`EnsureCoreActors`), plus promoted single ships (`Universe.NewActorId`, counter saved). `Faction` keeps only the political part (main relations as deviations, territory, emergency-build brake). `Ship.Faction` is gone: **`Ship.Owner`** (an `ActorId`) and **`Station.Owner`** (now an `ActorId`; start stations keep the faction's id). `Universe.FactionOf(actor|ship|station)` gives the membership, `Universe.ActorName`. Start world/migration: traders and miners are **free single ships** (owner = the Free group, implicit actors; JSON `ships[].owner: "free"`, default `"faction"`), construction ships and other roles belong to their faction actor, player ships to the player actor (`AddShip` fills a missing owner: player → player actor, else Free). Factions in JSON gain `kind` (state/guild/outlaw), `personality`, `relations` (faction id → value, deviations only) and `treasury`; the default world: Union state (lawful, low greed), Freie Händler guild (greedy), +0.25 to each other.

**Wallet of the owner.** `Universe.WalletOf(ship)` = the owner's `Treasury` if the owner has `ShipsUseTreasury` (the player), else the ship itself (operating account `Ship.Credits`, unchanged NPC behaviour); player-controlled ships without a resolvable owner fall back to the player treasury. Factions and promoted single actors run their ships as "Eigenbetriebe" with own operating accounts — NPC wallets were deliberately **not** merged.

**Size and classes (`Simulation/Actors.cs`, `ActorRules`).** `Index(u)` = holdings per explicit actor in one pass (ships except the Free group's, stations incl. sites, sectors via `Sector.Owner`, money = treasury + operating accounts of own ships and finished stations); `Size` = ships × `ActorShipWeight` (1) + stations × `ActorStationWeight` (3) + sectors × `ActorSectorWeight` (10) + money / `ActorMoneyScale` (50000); `ClassOf` by `ActorSizeThresholds` [2, 6, 15, 30]. A free single ship is sized on its own (`SingleShip`). `EconomyStats.Actors(u)` lists all actors (`ActorStat`); the Free group shows its ship count but class Single.

**Lazy promotion and decline.** `ActorRules.Promote(u, ship)`: a free single ship becomes an `Independent` actor the moment it invests (FleetSystem purchase, station funding; `EconomyRules.PromoteInvestors`): it owns itself and what it buys, is member of the faction owning its current sector, and rolls its personality via `u.Rng` around `IndependentPersonality` ± `PersonalitySpread`. Event `ActorPromoted`. `ActorSystem` (new `ActorModule` "Actors", after Lifecycle, interval 20) removes independents without ships, stations or treasury (event `ActorRemoved`).

**Zukauf (`Investors`).** Station construction (`StationLifecycleSystem`) and fleet expansion (`FleetSystem.Expand`) no longer take "the richest" but a **weighted draw** over `u.Rng` (soft first access, POLITIK.md decision): candidates in fixed order (treasuries by actor id, stations by id, ships by id) with weight = (money − threshold + 1) × `InvestAppetite[class]` ([0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1]) × (0.5 + greed). Stations: actor treasuries (not player/Free, `TreasuryInvests`), station operating accounts, economy-AI ships, each needing cost + `InvestorReserve`; ships: AI ships of the role with ≥ `FleetExpandCredits` (treasuries do not buy ships yet). The station/ship belongs to the actor behind the money (after promotion); a free investor picks sectors of the faction it would join. Treasury-funded sites store `BuildSite.InvestorActor` and get refunds on expiry. Event `InvestorChosen(purpose, source, holder, class, amount, candidates)`.

**Treasuries.** `UpkeepSystem`: the wealth taxes (formerly destroyed) flow — share `TaxToTreasuryShare` (1.0; 0 = old sink) — into the treasury of the responsible faction: members pay their faction, free single ships the faction owning their sector (`UpkeepSystem.TaxAuthority`). Payers are charged exactly as before. Events `TaxCollected(actor, amount)` per step and treasury, `TaxSunk` for the rest. Treasuries are spent only on station investments.

**Relations (`Relations`).** Sparse: `Effective(u, a, b)` = personal deviation of a (towards b or b's faction) → faction main relation (same faction: `RelationSameFaction` 0.5) → default by kind (`RelationDefault` 0, state/guild vs Free/Independent `RelationFree` 0.1, anyone vs outlaws `RelationOutlaw` −0.6). `Set` stores only deviations (removes the entry when it equals what would apply anyway), `StoredEntries`, `IsStored`, `Label` (verbündet … feindlich), `OfShip`. Nothing changes relations from events yet (no blockades, raids or alliances before Phase 10/11); entries stay at 2 in all runs.

**Persistence (v12).** `SaveGame.Actors` (`ActorSave`), `PlayerActor`, `FreeActor`, `NextActorId`; `ShipSave.Owner` (old `Faction` read only), `BuildSiteSave.InvestorActor`; `PlayerWallet` is read only (migration). `MigrateV11ToV12`: one actor per faction (same id, kind/personality from the default world when id and name match), Free group and player actor (ids above the largest faction id, at least 1000), the v7 player wallet becomes the player treasury with the wallet's faction as membership; ships: player-controlled → player, trader/miner → Free, others → their faction actor. `UniverseHash` mixes actors (kind, membership, treasury, personality, relations), faction relations, ship and station owners, the actor ids/counter and `InvestorActor` (the separate player-wallet mix is gone). Verified with the real v2 save (actors Union/Freie Händler/Freie Kapitäne/Du, wallet 1120.28, 14 free ships, 20000 ticks, 883 trades, fleet 14 → 14, save → load hash-equal).

**UI.** `FactionsScreen`: all actors except the player (factions, independents by size, the Free group) with kind, size class, holdings, treasury and relation to the player; detail with membership, size number, holdings, sectors, real personality bars and relations to the factions/Free group (stored deviation or "Vorgabe"); only "Letzte Ereignisse" and "Kontakt aufnehmen" remain placeholders. `MyFactionScreen`: size class and progress bar to the next class, own ships/stations by owner, treasury, membership, relations of each faction to the player; upkeep/berth/income, wanted level and the ability locks remain placeholders. Hangar/assets/dock/station info show the owner name. The economy panel shows "Kassen". Placeholders removed from `UiPlaceholders.cs`: relation to player, kind, personality, relations, size, progress, membership, reputation. Selftest: factions list from actors (no player, Free group present), guild detail opens, player ship owned by the player actor and booking on its treasury. `--ui-shots` promotes one free trader before 16/17 so the list shows every kind.

**Construction cleanup.** `ConstructionSystem.StepBuilders`, `ConstructionRules.ReleaseBuilders` and `Universe.StationsTrading` iterate in id order instead of dictionary insertion order (verified: 20000-tick balance hash unchanged before the Phase 9 changes).

**Tests.** New `ActorTests` (15): start-world actors/owners, wallet of the owner, invalid faction/owner content, size classes, weighted draw (weights, shares over 4000 draws, no RNG use for 0/1 candidates), treasury-funded site with refund on expiry, promotion on ship purchase (membership, ownership, second purchase stays the same actor), decline, tax flows into treasuries with payers unchanged, money explainable over 20000 ticks (treasuries = taxes − treasury investments + refunds), sparse relations (defaults, deviations added/removed), no relation explosion over 20000 ticks, determinism + v12 roundtrip, migration v11→v12 (hash equal to a fresh world, bit-identical continuation) and the old chain. `ActorTests.ToV11Json` demotes v12; `ConstructionTests.ToV10Json` and `WalletTests.ToV6Json` call it first. Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 11→12, `"PlayerWallet"` → `"Actors"` in the v7 roundtrip test, player faction → `Player.Membership`, builders found via `Owner`, the lifecycle site test gives stations little money (the investor is drawn now), the v6→v7 migration test disables treasuries/promotion (a v6 save cannot hold them). 394 tests green.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", 13/7 ships (capacity trade 14.3/14, mine 8.3/8), 41 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys; money in circulation stations 53.6k + ships 87.6k (before 51.5k + 90.1k), treasuries 0.98M (= the former tax sink, 1.02M taxes − 36k invested), 6 promoted actors, relations 2 entries. 60000: green, capacity 14.4/8.3, 0 emergency buys, treasuries 2.6M, 6 promoted / 3 declined, Zukauf: ships 24 from ship accounts (6 single, 14 small firm, 4 firm), stations 3 from treasuries + 1 from a station. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green, capacity trade 14.2–14.5 / mine 8.0–8.6, 0 emergency buys. Module bottlenecks unchanged band (reactor 2.0–2.9).

**Known weaknesses.** Treasuries only grow (tax ≈ 50 Cr/tick) and are spent only on stations, so they dwarf all other money and make the states win nearly every station draw; spending (police, subsidies, tribute) comes with Phases 10/11. Promoted actors keep per-ship operating accounts; their treasury stays 0. Relations never change yet; personality only affects Zukauf (greed). The player cannot join, leave or found a faction yet (membership comes from the start entry).


## Phase 10 (English summary): gate access, checkpoints, lock levels, sector control, government stations

**Gate states and mobility profile.** `Gate.State` (`Model/Access.cs`: `GateState` Open/Restricted/Closed/Destroyed) replaces the old switch (`Gate.Open` is now a derived property: open or restricted; setting it closes/reopens). Closed/destroyed is physical (only via `GateAccess.SetState`, which publishes `GateStateChanged`, bumps `Universe.AccessVersion` and revalidates running trips; nothing in the simulation closes gates yet); Restricted is derived from the lock levels (`GateAccess.SyncRestricted`). `Gate.MainRoute` (JSON `gates[].mainRoute`, both ends) is the gate exception of a sector lock: at most `EconomyRules.MainRouteMaxLevel` (3) applies there. `MobilityProfile(Actor, Laden, Stealth, IsPhysical)` (`GateAccess.ProfileOf(u, ship)`; `Travel.ProfileOf(u, ship?)`, null ship = `Physical`). `Universe.FindRoute/CachedRoute/IsReachable` take an optional profile (profile-less = physical gate state only, as before); the route cache is keyed by (from, to, profile) and invalidated per tick or by `TouchAccess()`; while nothing is restricted (`Universe.AccessRestricted` false — the normal start world) every profile is normalised to `Physical`, so routing costs and results are unchanged. All AI planners route with the ship's profile (`Travel.Plan/Estimate/EstimateDistance`, trader sell legs and miner deliveries with the laden profile so an embargo excludes them at choice time, `MinerSystem`, `FleetSystem` yards, builders incl. releasing an assigned site that became unreachable).

**Checkpoints (`Simulation/GateAccess.cs`).** `IAccessCheck` (`Key`, `Check(in AccessContext)`) with the registry `AccessChecks` (order policy, cargo, detection; duplicate key throws): `PolicyCheck` (members of the sector owner always pass; exit denied only at level 5; entry: level 4–5 nobody else, level 3 no enemies (relation ≤ `HostileRelation` −0.15), allies (≥ `AllyRelation` 0.5) free, everyone else pays `level × GateTollPerLevel` (8 Cr)), `CargoCheck` (level ≥ 2 embargo: laden enemy ships denied; `rules.contraband` goods denied from level 1, checked on the real cargo when a ship passes), `DetectionCheck` (stealth > `GateSensorStrength` passes undetected — deterministic, no module has stealth yet). `GateAccess.Evaluate(u, gate, profile, ship?)` combines the physical state, the exit side of the gate's sector and the entry side of the far sector → `GatePassage` (policy, toll, reason, toll recipient). `Travel.Start` routes with the profile and pays the tolls of all gates on the route at departure (`PayToll`: from `WalletOf(ship)` into the sector owner's treasury, `TollPaid`); an unreachable target no longer "teleports": no trip, order released, cargo kept, `RouteBlocked`, think again soon. `TryPlayerJump` is the player's checkpoint (deny with reason, toll only if the wallet covers it); `BlockReason` explains an unreachable target.

**Lock levels (`Simulation/Politics.cs`, `FactionPolicySystem`, new `PoliticsModule` after Actors, interval 20).** Decision taken: locks per **sector** (`Sector.Access`: `SectorAccess` with `EntryLevel` 0–5, `ExitLevel` 0/5, `Against`, `SinceTick`, `ExpiresTick`, `CooldownUntil`, `Enforced`) with gate exceptions (main route). The sector owner's level follows its worst relation to another faction: thresholds `LockThresholds` [−0.15, −0.3, −0.45, −0.65, −0.85] shifted by (aggression − 0.5) × `LockAggressionShift` 0.2; lowering needs `LockHysteresis` 0.1 above the threshold; any change after `LockMinTicks` (2000; raising from 0 only after `CooldownUntil`); a lock expires after `LockMaxTicks` (12000, then `LockCooldownTicks` 4000 without a new one); capped by the control stage (`StageMaxLevel` [0,0,0,2,5]: only Dominance allows 1–2, Control 3–5; contested at most `ContestedMaxLevel` 1; a lower cap forces the level down at once). Enforcement costs `LockUpkeepPerLevel` (0.5 Cr/tick per level) from the owner's treasury (`TreasuryUpkeep` "lock"); unpaid → `Enforced` false = a lock on paper (level 0 for checks). `FactionPolicySystem.SetLevel` sets a level by hand under the same rules (player faction later, tests, scenarios). Every change publishes `SectorAccessChanged`, re-syncs gate states and calls `AccessEnforcement.Revalidate`: AI ships in transit whose remaining legs cross a now-denied gate are re-planned from their interpolated position (extra distance costs fuel, `ShipRerouted`) or stopped where they are (order released, cargo kept, `RouteBlocked`) — no ghost orders behind closed gates. Traders without order but with cargo first sell it to the best reachable buyer (`TraderSystem.SellStranded`).

**Relations now move (`FactionPolicySystem.StepRelations`, every `RelationStepTicks` 100).** Per ordered faction pair: drift `RelationDriftRate` (0.05) towards a rest value = `RelationRestBase` + 0.5 × (cooperation − 0.5) − 0.5 × (aggression − 0.5) + `RelationTradeWeight` (0.4) × share of current trade orders between the two factions' stations; minus `ContestOpinionLoss` (0.02) per sector contested between them; minus `LockOpinionLoss` (0.005) × levels the other side enforces against it × (0.5 + trade share) (retaliation). Stored sparsely via `Relations.Set` (still 2 entries in the start world). Start world: Union→Guild ≈ 0.17–0.25, Guild→Union ≈ 0.22–0.30 in all runs — friendly, no lock ever.

**Sector control (`SectorControl`, `SectorControlSystem`, interval 50).** Influence = station weights (`StationType.Influence`, JSON `influence`, default 1; HQ 6, sector HQ 4, company seat 3, police 2, office 1.5; build sites 0) per faction of the station owner; unaffiliated owners count in the total. `Sector.Owner` is now derived: the leader takes over only with `OwnerSwitchMargin` (0.1) ahead of the current owner (sectors without stations keep their owner); `Faction.Territory` follows; `SectorOwnerChanged`. Stage of the owner (`Sector.Control`): Presence (a station), Influence (largest share), Dominance (> `DominanceShare` 0.5 and an authority or police station), Control (Dominance + authority (HQ or sector HQ) + patrol coverage — until Phase 11 an HQ or police station counts as coverage); `Sector.Contested` = two factions > `ContestedShare` 0.3; `SectorControlChanged`. `Sector.Influence` is stored for display/hysteresis. The world builder and loading a pre-v13 save compute it once.

**Government stations and the rest of the Essential split.** `StationType.Government` (`GovernmentKind`, JSON `government`: headquarters, company_seat, sector_hq, office, police), `Capturable`/`Destructible` (JSON, default true; government types never capturable), `Station.Immortal` (JSON `immortal`, saved). `StationProtection`: `IsGovernment` (HQ, company seat, sector HQ, office), `IsOfficial` (also police), `IsCapturable` (rule `AllowStationCapture`, false), `IsDestructible`, `CountEconomic` — official stations do not count against `MaxStations`/`MaxStationsPerSector` (start-world build headroom unchanged), are always bankruptcy-protected and pay their upkeep from the owner's treasury (`UpkeepSystem`, `TreasuryUpkeep` "government"; if the treasury is short, the station pays what it has and the rest stays unpaid — reported, no debt). Content: types `headquarters` "Hauptquartier" (upkeep 6), `company_seat` "Firmensitz" (2), `sector_hq` "Sektor-HQ" (3), `office` "Büro" (0.5), `police_station` "Polizeiwache" (1.5; type only, police logic is Phase 11), all without market/services. Start world: "Hauptquartier der Union" (id 32, Alpha (130,−25)), "Sektor-HQ Beta" (33, (−70,95)), "Gildenhaus Gamma" (34, HQ, (105,0)), "Sektor-HQ Delta" (35, (60,−95)), placed > 110 from belt centres; all three gate pairs are main routes (the start map is a star around Beta: every gate is the only link, so no faction lock can cut free traders off). Resulting stages: Alpha/Gamma Control, Beta/Delta Dominance. Company seat and office have no instance (no firm founds one yet).

**Display (`Simulation/AccessInfo.cs`, pure).** `ForSector` (owner, stage, influence, lock text, effective level, what the sector demands from the player, denied), `ForGate` (state, verdict), `ForStation` (reputation = owner's relation to you, customs, lock level, notes). **UI:** universe map — gate lines grey/orange (restricted)/red with a dot (denied for you)/red dashed (closed), faction layer with stage and "umkämpft", security layer real (halo by level, red if you cannot enter, sub label "Stufe n · …"), detail card with lock, "Für dich", influence, gate verdicts and the route by the player's profile (reason when unreachable); sector view gate rings/labels and gate/sector cards real; station info card "Politik & Hinweise" real; star map gate colours. Jumps check `TryPlayerJump` (denied: toast with reason, the ship bounces off; toll: paid and toasted, `Main.LastJumpMessage`); the autopilot (`Navigator.Next(..., ship)`) and "Route planen" respect the player's profile and name the reason. Placeholders removed: `SecurityText`, `PoliticsSoon`. Selftest: universe security layer, Gamma locked at level 3 against the Union → gate restricted, jump refused with the reason, universe route −1, station info shows level and customs, neutral at level 1 → jump allowed with 8 Cr toll, lock lifted. `--ui-shots` adds 29 (security layer with locks), 30 (faction layer, embargo detail), 31 (sector view with a denied gate), 32 (station info politics).

**Persistence (v13).** `GateSave.State`/`MainRoute` (`Open` still written), `SectorSave.Access` (`SectorAccessSave`), `Influence`, `Control`, `Contested`, `StationTypeSave.Government/Influence/Capturable/Destructible`, `StationSave.Immortal`; new rules additive. `UniverseHash` mixes gate states/main route, sector owner, access (levels, against, times, enforced), influence, stage, contested and immortal stations. `MigrateV12ToV13`: main-route flags for the default gate pairs (same ids and sectors), the government types (or their government kind on an existing key) and their default stations where the sector exists; v10→v11 no longer adds recipe-less government types. After migration the first sector-control pass runs on load. Verified with the real v2 save (loads, 4 government stations, all gates open/main route, 20000 ticks, 874 trades, fleet 14 → 14, save → load hash-equal).

**Diagnostics.** `Diagnostics/MapConnectivity` (largest mutually reachable block per profile: `Physical`, `Economy` = free traders empty/laden, `Factions`). The balance tool prints `sperr`/`iso` columns (locked sectors, isolated sectors), a politics summary (lock changes, tolls, re-planned/blocked trips, owner changes, treasury upkeep of government stations and locks as share of taxes, per-sector owner/stage/lock/influence, relations, sectors closed to a faction) and **warns with exit code 2** when the map splits physically or for free traders (prices still exit 1 first). A test world with hostile factions and no main routes (`--world`, Guild→Union −0.95) shows the mechanics end to end: retaliating locks to level 5, free traders cut off from Alpha/Gamma, "WARNUNG: Karte zerfallen", exit 2, prices still in range.

**Fixes found on the way.** `FleetSystem`'s clone fallback ignored the capacity limit (a cloned 1.6-unit Hauler could overshoot `MaxTraders + FleetCapacityOvershoot`; `LongRun_StaysBounded` caught it after the RNG shifted) — it now checks it like `ChooseModel`. Traders choosing a sell leg through an embargo looped buy → blocked → stranded (fixed by the laden-profile estimate).

**Tests.** New `PoliticsTests` (19): start world (government stations, stages, open main routes, connected map), content validation (government kind, influence, gate state), Essential split (government/capturable/destructible/immortal), checkpoint semantics per level/direction/embargo/allies/closed gate, main-route cap + paper lock + detection + contraband + registry, profile routing with a detour and the per-profile cache, `Travel.Start` without teleport, toll into the treasury, player jump (deny reason, toll only if payable) and the display functions, navigator with profile, policy (cap by stage, min duration, hysteresis, expiry/cooldown, unpaid enforcement), contested sectors lowering relations until a lock emerges from the mechanics, 20000-tick start world (no lock, friendly, map connected, government upkeep ≈ 18 Cr/tick covered, fleet holds), enforcement of a level-5 lockdown (trips re-planned/stopped, never across a denied gate, no ghost orders), sector control (owner hysteresis, territory, stages), government upkeep from the treasury, v13 roundtrip with locks/destroyed gate/influence bit-identical, migration v12→v13 (`PoliticsTests.ToV12Json`) and the old chain. Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 12→13, `ActorTests.ToV11Json` first demotes via `ToV12Json`, money-explainability tests include `TreasuryUpkeep`/`TollPaid` and skip official stations (they are not tax payers), `LongRun_StaysBounded` counts `CountEconomic`, the service list test expects government types without services, the v6→v7 migration test disables government upkeep (no such stations before v13). 413 tests green.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", capacity trade 14.8/14, mine 7.6/8 (14 traders / 7 miners), 35 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys, 0 lock changes, map connected; government upkeep 359,820 Cr = 35 % of the taxes into treasuries (treasuries 0.64M instead of 0.98M). 60000: green, trade 15.2/14 (55 Tinker job switches), mine 8.5/8, 0 emergency buys, treasuries 1.57M instead of 2.6M (upkeep 40 % of taxes). Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green, trade 13.2/13.9/14.5/14.5, mine 8.0/7.6/8.0/8.3, 0 emergency buys, 0 locks, map connected. Simulation ≈ 5 % slower (profile lookups in the planners).

**Known weaknesses.** With the start content nothing ever locks (relations stay friendly; contest needs foreign stations in a sector, which investors never build); locks appear only in tests or custom worlds. Tolls are paid at departure for the whole route, not at the gate; NPC route scoring ignores tolls. Detection is deterministic (no stealth module exists). No event closes or destroys gates (only the API). Exit levels 1–4 have no effect (only lockdown blocks exit). No player faction/policy UI, no tribute, war or diplomacy; "Unbeteiligte reagieren auf Betroffenheit" is not modelled. Company seat and office have no instances or services; police patrol coverage is a stand-in (HQ/police station). Trade capacity can exceed the limit through multirole job switches (Phase 6 behaviour, 15.2/14 at 60000 ticks).

## Phase 11 (English summary): situation map, police, wanted level, pirates, abstract encounters

**Situation map (`Simulation/ThreatMap.cs`, `ThreatMapSystem`, new `SecurityModule` "Security" after Construction and before Lifecycle, interval 20).** One view per sector (the owner's — there is only one responsible police per sector): `Sector.Threat` rises through reported incidents (`ThreatMap.Report`: raid `RaidThreat` 1, repelled raid 0.5, smuggling at a checkpoint 0.3), decays (`ThreatDecayPerTick` 0.0004) and is pushed down by patrols; `Sector.Security` (smoothed, `SecuritySmoothing` 0.25) = base protection of the owner's stations (`StationType.Security`, JSON `security`: HQ 1.5, sector HQ 0.5, police station 2) + strength of the owner's police ships currently in the sector ÷ `PoliceStrengthUnit` (10); `Sector.Traffic` (smoothed) = cargo value at base price of AI ships in the sector — the "value of the sector". `RiskOf`/`RouteRisk` give the expected loss share of a laden trip (`RiskPerThreat` 0.03 × threat ÷ (1 + security), ≤ `MaxSectorRisk` 0.25 per sector, ≤ 0.5 per route, via the route cache). `ThreatMap.HasCoverage` replaces the Phase 10 stand-in "HQ or police station": coverage = owner security ≥ `CoverageSecurity` (1) (other factions: their station base only); start stages are unchanged (Alpha/Gamma Control via HQ, Beta/Delta Dominance), Beta/Delta reach Control while a patrol is there. `WorldBuilder` and loading a pre-v14 save without map values measure the map once (`Initialize`). With `rules.securityActive` false the map stands still and police/pirates do nothing.

**Encounters (`Simulation/Encounters.cs`).** `IEncounterResolver.Resolve(u, attackers, defenders, destroyBase)` → `EncounterOutcome` (winner, strengths, win chance, hull damage per side, loser destroyed, loot share). `CombatRules.Strength(ship)` = weapon power (new stat `weaponPower`, `ShipStats.WeaponPower`) + shield ÷ 20 (`ShipStats.Shield`) + current hull ÷ 50 + 0.5; win chance A^k ÷ (A^k + D^k) (`EncounterExponent` 1.5). `AbstractEncounterResolver` (default `Universe.Encounters`, not saved, replaceable by a real combat system) always draws exactly five numbers from `Universe.Rng`; loot `LootMin..LootMax` (0.3–0.6), damage up to `LoserDamage`/`WinnerDamage`, destruction `destroyBase × √superiority` (≤ 0.5). `CombatRules.Damage` applies damage (lead ship full, helpers half) with an optional floor.

**Pirates (`Simulation/PirateSystem.cs`, interval 5).** An outlaw actor (new world section `actors[]`: key, name, kind outlaw/independent, personality, treasury; stations and ship groups reference it by `owner` key) with a hideout (`StationType.Hideout`, JSON `hideout`; type `pirate_hideout`, influence 0, no services; not counted against the build caps via `StationProtection.IsEconomic`, bankruptcy-protected) and raiders (role `ShipRole.Pirate`, model `corsair` of the restricted class `raider`). Start world: "Rote Korsaren" (treasury 6000), "Versteck der Roten Korsaren" (id 36, Delta (215,−195), outside the asteroid placement range), three Korsars. A raider at home picks a hunting sector by weighted draw (`HuntWeight` = (traffic + 500) ÷ (1 + aversion × security)), lurks near a gate (order `PatrolOrder`, `Ship.DutySector`) for at most `PirateLurkTicks` (2500), raids with chance `RaidRatePerTick` (0.008) × dt ÷ (1 + 0.3 × security); victims (`Victims`): AI ships (not police/pirates) and — if `PlayerRaids` — the active player ship that are in the sector right now, not within `StationSafeRadius` (35) of a station, carrying ≥ `MinLootValue` (200) at base price; weighted by value (player × `PlayerRaidWeight` 0.5). `ResolveRaid`: police of the sector owner in the sector help with chance min(`PoliceResponseMax` 0.8, 0.4 × patrols); attacker wins → the cargo share is gone, `FenceShare` (0.6) of its base value goes to the pirate treasury (`TreasuryIncome` "fence"), damage, rarely the NPC victim is lost (`DestroyChance` 0.015, never the player; the fleet regrows through `FleetSystem`); attacker loses → the raider flees or is destroyed (`RaiderDestroyChance` 0.35, halved without police). Threat rises, piracy is reported as a crime (`Wanted`), the raider flies home (fence/repair). Patrols in the sector intercept lurking raiders (`InterceptRatePerTick` 0.0006 per patrol, `PirateSystem.Intercept`, event `RaiderIntercepted`). At home: repair (`BaseRepairPerHull` 2 Cr) and refuel at base price from the treasury (`ServiceAtBase`, also used by police), rest `PirateRestTicks`. Every 50 ticks: upkeep `RaiderUpkeepPerTick` (0.05) per raider and a rebuild (`RaiderCost` 2000, `RaiderRebuildTicks` 3000, up to `MaxRaidersPerActor` 3) from the treasury (`TreasuryUpkeep` "pirates", `RaiderBuilt`). Outlaws never finance stations (`Investors`). Event `RaidResolved`.

**Player raids** (superseded in Phase 25: after the warning the raid is a real round combat — no hull floor, the active ship can be lost; the following describes the Phase 11 rule, which survives only with `rules.combatRounds` off). A raid on the player is announced: `PendingEncounter` (saved) + `EncounterWarning` with `PlayerWarningTicks` (60 = 15 s); docking (inside the station safety radius) or leaving the sector escapes (`EncounterEscaped`); otherwise the raid resolves with the hull floored at `PlayerMinHull` (25 %) and never a ship loss (FLOTTEN.md does not demand it). If the player wins and the raider is destroyed, the bounty (`Wanted.BountyOn` = level × `BountyPerLevel` 150) is credited as `Actor.BountyClaims` (`BountyEarned`). `OfflineCatchUp` switches player raids off and drops pending raid announcements (no reaction possible offline).

**Police (`Simulation/SecuritySystem.cs`, interval 5; faction decisions every `ManageTicks` 100).** Police ships (role `ShipRole.Police`, model `warden` of class `patrol_craft`: reactor Mk2, engine M, two `weapon_laser_s`, shield, armour; owner = faction actor; `Ship.HomeBase` = police station, else HQ, else sector HQ) — two per faction in the start world at their HQ. Per faction: upkeep `PoliceUpkeepPerTick` (0.8) per ship from the treasury (`TreasuryUpkeep` "police", unpaid reported), desired count = ⌈Σ territory (`PolicePerSector` 1 + threat × `PolicePerThreat` 0.5)⌉ within `PoliceMinPerFaction`..`PoliceMaxPerFaction` (1..4); below → `Commission` buys through the shared `ShipPurchase` rule at a reachable yard (kit + modules from stock, price to the yard, `PoliceReserve` 15000 must remain, `PoliceChangeCooldown` 2000; `PoliceCommissioned`); above for `PoliceDecommissionTicks` (6000) → decommission one. `Assign` distributes patrols over the territory by weight (base + threat + traffic ÷ 20000, largest remainder) and moves only as many as needed (`Ship.DutySector`). A patrol flies random patrol points of its sector (gates, stations, fields; `PatrolDwellTicks` 150), returns home below `RetreatHull` (50 %) or 30 % fuel. Jurisdiction: own territory only. Parked NPC ships regenerate hull slowly (`NpcHullRegenPerTick`). A player with wanted level ≥ 3 at the sector owner and a patrol in the sector is intercepted after a warning: contraband seized, the fine collected from the wallet as far as possible (`TreasuryIncome` "fine", `FinePaid`, `PlayerIntercepted`); paid in full → record deleted, otherwise level "verwarnt" with the remaining fine.

**Wanted level (`Simulation/Wanted.cs`, `WantedSystem` interval 50).** `Actor.Wanted` (sparse, per faction: `WantedRecord` heat, last crime tick, fine, crime) — POLITIK.md: actors carry the wanted level. `Wanted.Report` raises heat by `CrimeHeat` (smuggling 2.5, toll evasion 1.5, piracy 1.2, assault 3) up to `WantedMaxHeat` 5 and the fine by `CrimeFine`; levels 0–4 (sauber, auffällig, verwarnt, gesucht, Staatsfeind) from `WantedLevels` [1,2,3,4]; decay `WantedDecayPerTick` after `WantedMinTicks` (1000), record removed at 0. Crimes wired: **smuggling** at a checkpoint (Phase 10 `CargoCheck` denial now carries `AccessVerdict.Crime`, `GatePassage.Crime/Authority`; `TryPlayerJump` reports it and raises the threat), **toll evasion** (`TryPlayerJump`: if the wallet cannot pay the toll the player now jumps anyway, pays what is there and the rest becomes part of the fine — before Phase 11 the jump was refused; with security off the old refusal stays), **piracy** (every raid, for the outlaw actor at the sector owner). Assault does not exist yet (no player weapons fire at ships).

**Court and bounty office (`Services/CourtService.cs`).** `court` (`pay:<FactionId>`; fine = `Wanted.FineOf` = fine + heat × `FinePerHeat` 100; accepted for the station's faction and — legal assistance — factions whose relation to it is ≥ `ExtraditionRelation` 0; money to that faction's treasury, record deleted) and `bounty` (`claim:<FactionId>` pays the player's claim from the station faction's treasury, at most what it holds; `wanted:<ActorId>` offers are the faction's wanted list for display only and cannot be executed). Both are listed by HQ, sector HQ and police station (government stations now have services); strict keys, `ServicePerformed` only on success, `TreasuryIncome`/`TreasuryUpkeep` "bounty" keep money explainable.

**Trader/miner risk.** `TraderSystem.BestTrade` subtracts `RouteRisk(buy → sell) × load × sell price` from the profit; `MinerSystem.BestMine` multiplies the score by (1 − field-sector risk − delivery route risk). The same discount for everyone, capped per sector — it shifts routes instead of herding.

**Content.** Module `weapon_laser_s` "Laserkanone" (weapon S, grants Combat, `weaponPower` 6, power draw 3; key sorts last so module good ids stay stable; made by the weapons works automatically), classes `patrol_craft` "Patrouillenboot" (M, medium kit, hull 140, two bow weapons, shield, armour) and `raider` "Kaperschiff" (S, light kit, hull 80, `restricted` — `ShipClass.Restricted`, JSON `restricted`, dealers sell it only when their class list names it), models `warden` "Wächter" and `corsair` "Korsar" (no jobs). Yard stocks laser [2,4], shield [1,2], armour [1,2]. Government types get `security` and the services `court`, `bounty`. Rules: all knobs above (`EconomyRules`, Phase 11 block, additive in the save).

**Persistence (v14).** `SectorSave.Threat/Security/Traffic`, `ActorSave.Wanted` (`WantedSave`), `BountyClaims`, `LastForceTick`, `ShipSave.HomeBase/DutySector`, `SaveGame.Encounters` (`PendingEncounterSave`), `StationTypeSave.Security/Hideout`, `ShipClassSave.Restricted`. `UniverseHash` mixes the map values, wanted records, claims, force ticks, home base/duty sector, the hull of every ship and pending encounters. `MigrateV13ToV14`: `AddMissingDefaultContent` (laser, classes, models, `court`/`bounty`, recipes, stocks), `security` of the default government types, the hideout type; unless `SecurityActive` is off: founder police per faction at its home base (full tank and hull) and — only if the fleet can replace losses (ship kit good resolvable) — the outlaw actor with its hideout and raiders; new ships take ids in the gap behind the founder ships (as the world builder does), so a demoted fresh world loads hash-equal. `MigrateV10ToV11` no longer adds the hideout type. Verified with the real v2 save (loads, 4 police ships, no pirates because its goods have no keys, 20000 ticks, 926 trades, fleet 14 → 14).

**Display (`Simulation/SecurityInfo.cs`, pure).** `ForSector` (threat/security words, patrols present, risk %, the player's level there, summary), `ForPlayer` (wanted per faction with fine, crime, bounty claim), `HudLine`. **UI:** flight HUD line under the sector chip with the situation ("Sicherheit … · Bedrohung …") and a red/orange wanted line (toast moved to y 100); toasts for warnings, escapes, raid outcomes, raids/intercepts in your sector, police interception, crimes. Universe map security layer from the map (halo by threat/security, lock levels still shown when set, legend), detail card with situation, patrols, risk and your wanted level; sector view detail with the situation, police ships blue and raiders red (also in `SectorView`); station info "Lage" and "Fahndung hier"; "Meine Fraktion" shows the wanted level per faction with fine and bounty (placeholder `MyFactionWanted` removed); new dock area **"Behörde"** (`Ui/Screens/AuthorityScreen`, `dock.authority`) replaces the "Aufträge" placeholder tile at stations offering `court`/`bounty`: court rows with "Bezahlen", bounty claim with "Abholen", wanted list — rows only from `Offers`, execution only via `Execute`. Selftest (player raids off during the old stages): HUD situation/wanted lines, "Meine Fraktion", universe security layer, dock at Gildenhaus Gamma → "Behörde" area, station info situation, pay the fine (record deleted), strict key refused, collect a bounty; raid on the player → warning → dock → escaped without losing cargo. `--ui-shots` adds 33–40 (HUD wanted/situation, situation map, my faction, dock menu with Behörde, Behörde, station info, raid warning, sector view with police/raider).

**Diagnostics.** The balance tool prints raids (won/repelled, police help, on the player), intercepts, raiders lost/built, victim ships lost, **loss rate** (loot base value ÷ trade revenue), police count/desired per faction, purchases/decommissions, **police share of treasury income** (police upkeep + purchases + repairs ÷ (taxes + tolls + fines)), pirate treasury/costs/wanted level, the situation map per sector and the **computing time of the security module** (`TimedModule` wraps its systems; `SimulationBuilder.Registered`); `--security off` for comparison runs. The government upkeep line now counts locks only as "lock".

**Tests.** New `SecurityTests` (16): content and start world, invalid actors/owners/security, situation map (base protection, patrol coverage, threat, route risk, decay, off switch), resolver (strength, determinism with exactly five draws, replaceable resolver used by the pirates), raid on a trader (cargo share, fence, threat, wanted, safety radius), police helping and intercepting + pirate rebuild, player raid (warning, escape by docking, resolution with hull floor and no loss, off switch), bounty (claim on victory, office pays from the treasury, strict keys), wanted levels/decay/court (legal assistance, hostile factions refused), smuggling at the checkpoint and police interception of a wanted player, police upkeep/desired/purchase via the purchase rule/assignment, decommission, trader and miner risk discount, v14 roundtrip bit-identical with wanted/threat/pending, migration v13→v14 (full strip `ToV13Json(full: true)`) and hash-equal reload of a demoted fresh world, 20000-tick run (raids > 0, police reacts, loss < 5 % of trade, capacity holds, no ghost orders). `SecurityTests.ToV13Json` demotes v14 (default keeps the laser content and the map values so older migration tests stay hash-equal; `PoliticsTests.ToV12Json` calls it first) and `SecurityTests.WithoutSecurity` removes police/pirates for tests that demote a running world. Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 13→14; module/class/model counts; service registry and government service lists (`court`, `bounty`); `CountEconomic` excludes the hideout; fleet tests exclude police/pirate ships like builders (tank, speed, unit models, bought ships); the yard sells all non-restricted models; the jump test expects toll evasion instead of a refusal (old refusal checked with security off); the actor tests know the outlaw owner/hideout and the "pirates" key; money explainability includes `TreasuryIncome` and the pirates' start treasury (faction-only test filters faction actors); the v6→v7 wallet test runs without security; a latent bug in the v2 fuel chain test (default-world good id instead of the chain's propellant id) fixed. 429 tests green, selftest OK.
