**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", capacity trade 13.6/14, mine 8.5/8, 34 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys; 46 raids (35 successful, police helped 18×), 10 intercepts, 4 raiders lost/4 rebuilt, 0 victim ships lost, **loss rate 0.49 %** of trade revenue, police 2+2 ships (desired 4/3, the yard lacked modules for a purchase), **police 6.7 % of treasury income**, security module **2.8 % of runtime**. 60000: green, trade 14.6/14, mine 8.3/8, 0 emergency buys, 127 raids, loss rate 0.56 %, 1 victim lost, police 8.1 %, 1.9 % runtime. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green, trade 14.8/12.9/14.2/14.2, mine 8.0/8.3/8.0/8.3, 24–42 raids, loss rate 0.28–0.56 %, 0–1 victims lost, police bought 2–4 (decommissioned 0–2), police 8.1–9.3 % of income, 0 emergency buys. With `DestroyChance` 0.03 seed 4 warned (reactor/engine/consumer goods > 3×: replacing destroyed ships drains the scarce Mk1 modules; `--security off` was green) — halved to 0.015.

**Known weaknesses.** Encounters are abstract only (no combat visuals; the player can win only with a weapon fit, so bounties are rare; there is no active bounty hunting). Police purchases often fail for lack of yard modules (default seed: none). Pirates have no market/black market and no fleet growth beyond 3 raiders; their treasury hovers near zero. Threat is per sector (owner's view), not per faction and sector. Crimes: assault and territory violations are not wired (no player attacks); NPC smuggling does not exist. Police interception collects fines even while the player is offline/docked (it is announced and only fines/contraband are taken). Faction police never chase across borders or escort convoys; no protection contracts.

## Phase 12 (English summary): commands and executors, player fleet with automation, squadrons, hangar/berth fees, remote switch, taxi, rental

**Commands (`Model/Commands.cs`, `Simulation/Orders/`).** `ShipCommand` records (`GoToCommand(sector, pos, station)`, `HoldCommand`, `PatrolCommand(sectors)`, `GuardCommand(station)`, `EscortCommand(ship)`, `FollowCommand(ship)`, `TradeCommand(AutoLimits)`, `MineCommand(AutoLimits)`, `BuildCommand(site)`, `ReturnCommand`) live in `Ship.Command` (null = automation layer), separate from `Ship.Order` (the working order of the executing systems). `IOrderExecutor` per kind (`Kind`, `Needs` capability, `Check`, `Start`, `Step` → Running/Done/Failed, `Stop`, `Describe`) in the registry `OrderExecutors` (order goto, hold, trade, mine, build, patrol, guard, escort, follow, return; duplicate throws). `OrderSystem` (new `OrderModule` "Orders" right after Production, before Jobs, every tick; ships without a command cost nothing) implements the levels **emergency > command > automation**: emergency (hull < `EmergencyHullFraction` 0.3 or empty tank) stops the command, flies to the nearest station with `repair` (else the home base), refuels/repairs there and restarts the command (`EmergencyStarted/Ended`). `Commands.Give/Cancel/Check/Describe` is the API (the active ship never takes commands; the player's ships are limited by the **command capacity** of the owner's size class: `CommandShips` [4, 8, 16, 32, 64] individually commanded ships, `CommandSquadrons` [1, 1, 3, 6, 12] squadrons). Movement executors share `OrderMove` (fuel via `Refuel.PrepareTrip`, squadron speed, arrival tanks up at a `GoToOrder` stop, `StopHere`). Work commands only set the task label (`Role` Trader/Miner/Builder) — `TraderSystem`, `MinerSystem` and `ConstructionSystem` stay the executors (automation layer). `AutoLimitRules`: a ship with `TradeCommand`/`MineCommand` only considers stations/fields within `RadiusJumps` of `Center`, buys at most at `MaxBuyFactor` × base price and keeps `Reserve` in the wallet; NPCs have no command, so their code path is unchanged. `TraderSystem` now books via `Universe.WalletOf(ship)` (identical for NPCs). Player construction ships with `BuildCommand` are site builders (`Ship.IsSiteBuilder`): material is paid from the site escrow, the 10 % builder fee goes to the player wallet. `Travel.Plan/Start` take an optional speed override.

**Squadrons (`Squadrons`).** `Squadron` (owner, name, leader, ≤ `SquadronMaxMembers` 8 members, formation — display only —, home base, rules of engagement) in `Universe.Squadrons`, `Ship.Squadron`. Create/join/leave/disband are free but capped (capacity). A squadron command goes to the leader: movement commands are planned only by the leader with the slowest member's speed, the others get `FollowCommand(leader)`; work commands go to every capable member, the rest follow. If the leader is lost (`Universe.RemoveShip` → `Squadrons.OnShipRemoved`), the next member takes over its movement command and the followers. `Materializer` offsets members near their leader by `Squadrons.Offset(formation, index, dir)` (line, wedge, star, box) — display only, hash unaffected.

**Rules of engagement (`Engagement`: Defend default, Passive, Attack, HoldFire; squadron rules win).** `FleetGuards.Escorts`: escorts of a victim and guards of a station in the raid sector join the defenders in `PirateSystem.ResolveRaid` (Defend/Attack only); `FleetGuards.Hunters`: patrols/guards with Attack in a sector intercept lurking raiders like the police (bounty claims for the player). Non-active player ships are now raid victims without warning (`FleetRaids`, default true; never destroyed, hull floor as for the player); the active ship keeps the Phase 11 warning. Parked player ships regenerate hull like NPCs.

**Hangar, berth fee, selling (`Simulation/Hangar.cs`).** `Ship.DockedAt` (saved): set by `Universe.FinishTransit` when a trip ends at a dock point (`Universe.StationAt`, radius 30), cleared on departure/jump/station removal, set by the view on docking/undocking of the active ship, by `ShipPurchase.Create` and by the world builder/old-save loading (not for the active ship). `HangarSystem` (interval 50): non-active player ships docked at a station of another owner pay the class berth fee per day (`ShipClass.BerthFee`, JSON `berthFee`, else `BerthFeeBySize` S 12 / M 24 / L 60 / XL 120; `DayTicks` 2400 = 10 min) to the station (`BerthFeePaid`, unpaid rest reported), and commanded fleet ships pay `ShipUpkeepPerTick` like NPCs (`FleetUpkeepPaid`, sink). Service `hangar` (`switch:<id>`, `home:<id>` = return command, `sell:<id>`; selling pays material value × `ShipSellShare` 0.5 from the station, cargo must be empty, rentals and the active ship cannot be sold). Foreign docked ships are listed via `Hangar.ForeignDocked`. `PlayerFleet`: `ParkedAt` uses `DockedAt`; `SwitchRemote` (UI.md "switch where it is": a travelling target stops at its interpolated position, its command ends; the old ship stays where it is and holds), `Dock`, `Undock`, `Activate`. Player purchases set `HomeBase` to the yard.

**Taxi and rental (`Services/TaxiService.cs`, `RentalService`).** `taxi:<stationId>`: the person travels alone to a station with `hangar` where an own ship waits or a rental is available; price = `TaxiBaseFee` 40 + `TaxiPerJump` 30 × jumps + `TaxiPerUnit` 0.1 × distance + tolls of the route (empty profile); blocked routes are not bookable (reason). Execute docks the old ship at the origin, switches to the waiting ship or rents one at the destination, publishes `TaxiRide`, and returns the trip time in ticks (`ServiceResult.Units`); the view runs those ticks (`GameSession.RunTicks`). `rental`: `rent:1` creates a `RentalModel` (`pip`) player ship (fee `RentalFee` 60 to the station, deposit `RentalDeposit` 400 held on the ship, `RentalMaxPerPlayer` 1) and makes it active; `return:<id>` charges `RentalPerDay` 120 per started day from the deposit (wallet if needed), refunds the rest and switches back to an own ship docked there (`ShipRented`, `RentalReturned`). Services in content: default service list without JSON field is now `["trade", "hangar"]`; `hangar` at all stations with berths (also plating works, refinery, warehouse, HQ, sector HQ, police), `taxi` at shipyard, trade station, refinery, warehouse, HQ, sector HQ, `rental` at shipyard and trade station.

**UI.** Dock: the menu shows real docked ships (active, own in the hangar, foreign with owner) and the berth fee; the tile grid and the area rail scroll, so "Lager" and "Behörde" no longer displace "Taxi" (up to nine tiles; "Aufträge" stays a placeholder). `HangarScreen` (own parked ships with command, fee, Übernehmen/Heim/Verkaufen via `hangar`, "Befehle" → fleet; foreign ships; rental via `rental`), new `TaxiScreen` (`dock.taxi`, offers + `TaxiService.Quote`: price, duration, toll, situation, waiting ship or rental, reason; booking via `Execute`, Main fast-forwards and re-enters the active ship). Command centre: `FleetScreen` rewritten (list with command/location/squadron, capacity header, command grid from the registry disabled by capability, target choice — at most three taps —, automation limits steppers, rules of engagement, squadron actions, cancel, "Übernehmen" = remote switch); `AssetsScreen` detail (remote switch, commands, send home, sell, real berth fee/value/fuel); universe card "Taxi buchen" real when docked at a taxi stand; "Meine Fraktion" berth fee and capacity real. Main: `EnterActiveShip` rebuilds the view where the new active ship is (docked → dock menu), triggered by `ActiveShipChanged` when the ship is not at the docked station. Placeholders removed: taxi price, hangar note, fleet command grid/behaviour/capacity, ship upkeep/value/switch hint, berth fee, two locked abilities. Selftest: Taxi tile real, nine tiles at the warehouse, fleet screen gives the bought Prospector a GoTo command (patrol without weapons refused) and it docks at the trade station, taxi from the yard to it (old ship stays docked, fare paid, ticks fast-forwarded), rent and return a Pip in the hangar, invalid hangar key refused, remote switch back into the first ship at the yard, automatic trade command with limits (trades booked on the wallet, the fleet ship keeps 0 credits), asset detail. `--ui-shots` adds 41–47 (dock menu with hangar/taxi, hangar with fleet and rental, taxi, fleet commands, target choice, ship detail, warehouse with nine tiles).

**Persistence (v15).** `ShipSave.Command` (the extended `OrderSave`: kind, A/B/C plus optional `Ids` (patrol sectors) and `Values` (point, limits)), `CommandSince`, `CommandStep`, `Emergency`, `Engagement`, `Squadron`, `DockedAt`, `RentedFrom/RentedSince/RentalDeposit`; `SaveGame.Squadrons`/`NextSquadronId`; `ShipClassSave.BerthFee`; rules additive. `UniverseHash` mixes all of it. `MigrateV14ToV15`: `AddMissingDefaultContent` (services `hangar`/`taxi`/`rental` on default types) and `hangar` for every other type with `trade` except build sites; loading a pre-v15 save derives `DockedAt` from positions (not for the active ship). Verified with the real v2 save (loads, hangar on its own types, 20000 ticks, 926 trades, fleet 14 → 14, save → load hash-equal).

**Tests.** New `FleetCommandTests` (21): registry/order/capabilities, check refuses the active ship and missing capability/target without state change, GoTo across sectors docks and ends, Hold stops a trip in place, Follow + Return with refuel, automatic trade within radius/reserve booking on the wallet, automatic mining selling on the wallet, emergency (repair, command resumes), command capacity for ships and squadrons, squadron (slowest speed, followers dock, 8 members, leader succession inherits command, work command split, disband), formation display-only with unchanged hash, escort defends in a raid (Passive does not), fleet ships are victims unless `FleetRaids` off, hangar switch/home/sell with strict keys and foreign ships, berth fee per class and day (own station free), remote switch, taxi (waiting ship, fare, ticks, closed gate not bookable), rental (deposit, two started days, return), determinism + v15 roundtrip with commands/squadron/rental bit-identical incl. continuation, offline catch-up keeps the fleet working, migration v14→v15 hash-equal, NPC world with and without the order module hash-identical. `FleetCommandTests.ToV14Json` demotes v15 (`SecurityTests.ToV13Json` calls it first). Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 14→15, service lists (registry, default types with `hangar`/`taxi`/`rental`, `ForStation` of a market station now trade + hangar, a station without market keeps only `hangar`). 450 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Identical to Phase 11 apart from the hash (NPC economy untouched; the hash changes only through `DockedAt`): default seed 20000 "Preise im Zielbereich", 14/8 traders/miners (capacity 13.6/14, 8.5/8), 34 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys, 46 raids, loss rate 0.49 %, police 6.7 % of treasury income; 60000 green, 14.6/8.3, 0 emergency buys, loss rate 0.56 %, police 8.1 %; seeds 1–4 at 20000 all green (trade 14.8/12.9/14.2/14.2, mine 8.0/8.3/8.0/8.3), loss rate 0.28–0.56 %, police 8.1–9.3 %, 0 emergency buys.

**Known weaknesses.** Police and pirates still run their Phase 11 systems (patrol points are a shared rule, escorts/guards/hunters join encounters); factions never command squadrons. Rules of engagement only decide who defends or hunts (abstract encounters; Passive/HoldFire do not flee or change the victim's own strength). The squadron home base is set at creation and not editable in the UI; squadron followers re-plan only after arriving. Player fleet trading earns like an NPC trader without wealth tax (only fuel, berth fees and the small upkeep); a balance run with a large player fleet was not done. The taxi is instant in the simulation (the trip time passes in fast-forward; pending raids are not resolved for the person). Fleet raids continue during offline catch-up. Rentals are unlimited per station (no rental pool), no damage charge.

## Phase 13 (English summary): research — projects, research stations, faction-wide unlocks, research service

**Model and content.** `Model/Research.cs`: `ResearchProject` (key, name, description, `Points` (FP), `Requires`, `Unlocks` as `ResearchUnlock(UnlockKind Module|Model|StationType, key)`, text form `module:<key>`/`model:<key>`/`stationType:<key>`, and extra per-cycle `Inputs`), `ResearchCatalog` on `Universe.Research` (projects in content order plus the lock index — content no project names is always free), `ResearchConfig` on `StationType.Research` (cycle `Ticks`, `Points`, staff `Credits` per cycle, base `Inputs`, `FundTarget`, `FundPerTick`) and `ResearchState` on `Faction.Research` (`Current`, `Commissioned`, `Progress` per project, `Completed` key → tick, `PointsTotal`, `Spent`, `PlayerFunded`). World JSON: top-level `research[]` (`key`, `name`, `description`, `points`, `requires` — only earlier projects, so cycle-free —, `unlocks`, `inputs`) and `stationTypes[].research` (only with `government: "research"`, new `GovernmentKind.Research`: official station → upkeep from the owner's treasury, bankruptcy-protected, not counted against the build caps, no police home base, influence 0 in the default world). `WorldBuilder` validates (unknown/duplicate keys, unreadable or unknown unlocks, an unlock belonging to two projects, requires order, points > 0, unknown input goods, research config without research government and vice versa) and the "Totlauf" rules: a locked module may not be a recipe input, a free model may not carry a locked module, a locked model only modules of its project or its requirements, and no start ship, start package or start station may use locked content. Default content: modules `reactor_mk3` (power 18; steel 4, electronics 4, platinum 2; drive works, `maxTier` 3) and `mining_laser_3` (chance ×1.6, size ×1.5; electronics 4, copper 2, platinum 2; systems works, `maxTier` 3) with upgrade paths Mk2 → Mk3, small stocks (factory [1,1], yard [0,1]), model `excavator` (heavy miner: reactor Mk3, engine M, laser Mk3, 160 cargo; job `mine`); station type `research_station` "Forschungsstation" (services `trade`, `research`, `hangar`; stock-only entries energy [20,30], electronics [6,10], platinum [2,4], wafers [4,8], titanium [3,6]; cycle 10 ticks, 5 FP, 10 Cr staff, base input 2 energy; budget up to 5000 Cr at 8 Cr/tick; upkeep 1) with one instance per faction: "Forschungsinstitut der Union" (id 37, Alpha (−40,−140)) and "Gildenlabor Gamma" (id 38, Gamma (120,120)) — listed before the HQs so migration chains keep the sector station order. Projects: `fusion_mk3` "Fusionstechnik Mk3" (3000 FP, +0.05 platinum per cycle, unlocks the reactor), `precision_lasers` "Präzisionslaser" (3000 FP, +0.2 wafers, the laser), `deep_mining` "Tiefbau-Schürfer" (5000 FP, requires both, +0.2 titanium, the Excavator). Everything the start fleet, the AI fleet, the police and the lifecycle use stays unlocked.

**Rules (`Simulation/Research.cs`).** `Research.IsUnlocked(u, faction, kind, key)` (free without a locking project or with `rules.researchActive` false; otherwise the faction must have completed the project — faction-wide, FORSCHUNG.md "Besitz"), `TechOf(station)` = faction of the owner, else of the sector, `Available(u, station, kind, key)`, `GoodAvailable` (module goods), `UnlockedAnywhere`, `LockText` ("nicht erforscht (Projekt)"), `Peek` (pure: running project or the heuristic's next one — most progressed available project, ties by content order), `CurrentOf` (sets it), `AddPoints` (shared by stations and player; completes → `ResearchCompleted`, overflow dropped), `CycleNeeds`, `StallReason`, `StationsOf`, `UnlockName`. Gates: `ProductionSystem` skips recipes whose output is a module the station's faction has not researched; `ShipPurchase.SellsModel` (player dealer, `FleetSystem.ChooseModel`, clone path, police commissioning); `ShipDealerService.Quote.Locked` (locked models stay listed, `Buyable` false, `Execute` refuses with the reason); `OutfitterService` lists locked modules as "nicht erforscht" offers (Amount 0) and refuses fitting them from station stock (from the own hold is allowed); `ShipUpgrade.Quotes` gives locked targets an issue; `ShipQualityRoll` only upgrades into modules the yard's faction has (founders without a yard: only free modules); `StationLifecycleSystem` only picks types some faction has unlocked and builds only if the investor's faction has.

**`ResearchSystem`** (new `ResearchModule` "Research" right after Production/Market, every tick; station list cached per `StructureVersion`): per research station (id order) (1) caps its bid for the cycle goods at `BasePrice × ResearchMaxBidFactor` (1.25; a stock-only entry would otherwise bid 4× and outbid the factories), (2) tops its credits up to `FundTarget` from the owner's treasury at most `FundPerTick × dt` while there is something to research (`TreasuryUpkeep` "research", so treasury money stays explainable), (3) every `Ticks` ticks runs a cycle: base + project inputs out of the stock, staff credits out of the station (sink), `Points` FP on the faction's current project (`ResearchCycle` with points/credits/goods base value, or the stall reason — missing goods or budget; research stalls like production). Several stations of one faction add up.

**Service `research` (`Services/ResearchService`, registered last).** Available at finished research stations of a faction when projects exist. Offers: `commission:<project>` per unresearched project (fee `ResearchCommissionFee` 500 Cr to the station; Amount 1 only if requirements are met, it is not already running, the wallet pays and the faction is not hostile — relation ≤ `HostileRelation` refuses everything) and `fund:<amount>` for the running project per `ResearchFundAmounts` [500, 2000, 10000] (credits to the station, amount ÷ `ResearchCreditsPerPoint` (20) FP, may complete the project = earlier access for the player). Strict keys; failure changes nothing and publishes nothing; success publishes `ResearchCommissioned`/`ResearchFunded` and `ServicePerformed`. Goods can additionally be delivered through normal trade (stock-only entries).

**Display (`Simulation/ResearchInfo.cs`, pure).** `ForFaction`/`ForStation` → `ResearchFactionView` (completed/total, running project, FP totals, staff spending, player funding, project views with status Done/Running/Available/Blocked, progress, completion tick, missing requirements, unlock names, extra inputs; station views with FP per cycle, needs, stall reason, credits; ETA) and `Summary`. **UI:** new dock area "Forschung" (`Ui/Screens/ResearchScreen`, `dock.research`, tile from `DockAreas` when `research` is offered): projects with status bar, unlocks and "Beauftragen", station operation (running/stalled, consumption, cash, ETA) and "Mitfinanzieren" buttons — rows from `ResearchInfo`, buttons only from `Offers`, execution only via `Execute`. Command centre: faction detail shows the research state instead of the event placeholder; "Meine Fraktion" shows the own faction's research line. Shipyard rows of locked models say "Nicht erforscht: <project>" (buy disabled); outfitter rows of locked modules say "Nicht erforscht (…)". Selftest: dock at the Union institute → "Forschung" area, three project rows and three funding buttons, commission (fee booked), fund 2000 Cr (+100 FP), strict keys refused, factions/my faction open; at the guild's yard the Excavator row and the outfitter's reactor Mk3 are "nicht erforscht", after completing the guild's projects nothing is locked. `--ui-shots` adds 48 (dock menu at the institute), 49 (research area), 50 (factions with research), 51 (yard with a locked model), 52 (outfitter with a locked module).

**Persistence (v16).** `SaveGame.Research` (`ResearchProjectSave`, unlocks as text, inputs as good ids), `StationTypeSave.Research` (`ResearchConfigSave`), `FactionSave.Research` (`ResearchStateSave`, omitted while empty); new rules additive. `UniverseHash` mixes each faction's research state. `MigrateV15ToV16`: `AddMissingDefaultContent` (Mk3 modules, goods, factory recipes, stocks, Excavator), upgrade paths Mk2 → Mk3 on default modules without one, the research station type with its default stations (only if its inputs and stocks resolve; official, bankruptcy-protected) and — only if the world then has a research station and the unlocks/inputs resolve and no existing ship already uses the content — the projects; otherwise nothing is locked ("im Zweifel alles frei"). `MigrateV12ToV13` no longer adds the research type as a plain government type. Verified with the real v2 save: loads, no research station/projects (goods without keys), everything unlocked, 20000 ticks, 926 trades, fleet 14 → 14, save → load hash-equal.

**Fixes on the way.** (1) Save/load determinism: `Universe.RemoveShip/RemoveStation` compact the dictionaries (`TrimExcess`), so ships/stations added later are appended exactly like after loading — before, a new ship could take a freed slot and systems iterating `Ships.Values` saw a different order after save/load (surfaced as a diverging roundtrip in `ActorTests`); `TradeActions.SellAll/Quote`, `Ship.UsedVolume` and the threat map's traffic sum iterate cargo in good-id order. (2) Pirate restart (correction to Phase 11): an outlaw without raiders gets `PirateIdleIncomePerTick` (0.1) from its hideout (`TreasuryIncome` "hideout") and the first rebuild costs `RaiderCost × RaiderRestartCostFactor` (0.5) — before, a wiped-out pirate fleet with a near-empty treasury never came back (loss rate fell to ≈0.1 % in long runs, also in Phase 12 on seeds 5 and 7).

**Tests.** New `ResearchTests` (17): content and locks, validation failures, research cycle (consumption, staff, FP, budget from the treasury, bid cap, stall), completion unlocks production faction-wide only, shipyard/outfitter/upgrade/quality roll/AI fleet/lifecycle gates, the `research` service (commission, fund to completion, strict keys, hostile refusal), display, 20000-tick run (projects complete, nothing locked is produced), v16 roundtrip bit-identical, migration v15→v16 hash-equal, realistic v15 without research content, world without resolvable research goods stays fully unlocked; `ResearchTests.ToV15Json` (default keeps the research stations/Mk3 content and faction states like the Phase 11 demoter keeps the map; `full: true` strips the states) is called first by `FleetCommandTests.ToV14Json`. Plus `SecurityTests.Piraten_Neuanfang…`. Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 15→16, registry/service lists (`research`), module/model counts, government station lists and upkeep sums (research stations are official), `PoliticsTests.ToV12Json` keeps types with a research config, the tax-flow comparison runs without research (treasury-funded), `SecurityTests` long run checks raiders in the last quarter (the default seed's fleet is wiped out at tick ≈18000 and restarts), `MultiroleTests` capacity margin +3 instead of +2 (default seed 16.9/14 through four trading Tinkers after the RNG shift; seeds 1–4 13.2–15.2). 469 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", capacity trade 16.9/14, mine 7.6/8 (18 traders/7 miners incl. 4 Tinkers trading), 47 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys, loss rate 0.25 %, police 6.7 % of treasury income; research 3820 cycles = 19100 FP, 4 unlocks (both factions: Fusionstechnik Mk3 at ≈6700/7100, Präzisionslaser at ≈12700/13100), budget from treasuries 181k Cr (15.8 % of treasury income), staff 38k Cr sink, goods 103k Cr base value. 60000: green, trade 14.2/14, mine 8.5/8, 0 emergency buys, loss rate 0.25 %, police 9.6 %, all 6 unlocks (Tiefbau-Schürfer at ≈22700/23200), treasuries 1.10M instead of 1.57M (budget 209k = 7.4 % of income; government upkeep incl. research stations 43 %). Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green (trade 13.2/13.9/15.2/14.2, mine 8.0/8.3/7.6/8.3, loss rate 0.30–0.57 %, 0 emergency buys, 2–4 unlocks). Seeds 5–8 at 20000: 7 of 8 green over seeds 1–8 (seed 6: consumer goods 3.12×; Phase 12 had 8/8); 60000 ticks seeds 1–6: 6/6 green (Phase 12: 4/6, with 0.30× warnings for unused modules), loss rate 0.13–0.61 %. Simulation ≈25–30 % slower (more trade routes/markets; the research system itself ≈7 µs/tick).

**Known weaknesses.** Electronics was dropped from the base research consumption: with 0.2–0.5 electronics per cycle the already tight electronics chain tipped Mk1 reactors/engines or consumer goods over 3× on 3 of 8 seeds — research now uses energy plus small project inputs (platinum, wafers, titanium), deviating from FORSCHUNG.md ("Elektronik Hauptverbrauch"). The Mk1 module band (reactor/engine 2–3×) stays the economy's fragile spot; single seeds flip either way. No recipe or goods/ore unlocks, no "improvements" (reload, chunk chance) and no research missions; the mechanism supports station-type unlocks (tested with a test world) but the default content locks only modules and a model. The AI rarely buys the Excavator (worse value per credit than unit miners); Mk3 modules mainly reach NPCs through quality rolls and upgrades. Funding gives the player acceleration, not exclusive early access. Destroying a research station keeps the faction's progress (no regression). Pirates still die out temporarily on some seeds; the restart brings them back after a few thousand ticks.

## Phase 14 (English summary): export sink, planets, switches for fuel and research

**Switches (code and tests kept, only switched off).** `EconomyRules.FuelActive` (JSON `rules.fuelActive`, code default true for test/custom worlds, **default world false**) and the existing `ResearchActive` (**default world false**). Fuel off: `Refuel.Consume(u, …)` (used by `Travel.Start`, `AccessEnforcement`, `PlayerFuel.ConsumeFlown(u, …)`) books nothing, `PlayerFuel.ThrottleCap(u, …)` = 1, `Refuel.PrepareTrip` always returns `Depart`, `BuyFuel`/`TankUp`/`EmergencyBuy`/`TripFuelCost` do nothing, `TraderSystem` scores without fuel cost, `OrderSystem.EmergencyReason` never reports "Tank leer", police never return for fuel, `RefuelService.IsAvailable` is false, `Refuel.PlayerEmergency` refuses. Research off: everything unlocked (as before), `ResearchService` unavailable, no budget leaves the treasuries. `Simulation/FeatureSwitches.OmitFromWorld` lets `WorldBuilder` skip stations whose type belongs to a switched-off system — research stations (type with `research` config) and pure fuel makers (`IsFuelSupply`: every recipe output is a good some drive module lists as fuel) — keeping the JSON ids of all other stations; `MaxStations` drops by the number of skipped economic stations (the two refineries: 36 → 34) so the build headroom stays the same; the lifecycle never builds such types. Fuel/research tests build their world with the switch on (`tests/.../TestWorlds.cs`: `Default(fuel, research, founderQuality)`, `WithFuel()`, `WithResearch()`, `Def()` — before building, so refineries and research stations exist). Balance tool: `--fuel on|off`, `--research on|off` (override the world's rule before building), fuel/research lines only when active, `--dump on` lists every station's market. UI: fuel gauge hidden in the flight HUD (`FlightHudData.FuelActive`), dock ship column, hangar, assets, fleet, shipyard and outfitter texts; "Notruf" hidden in the hamburger menu (`CommandMenu.SetFuelActive`); the dock area is called "Reparatur" and shows no refuel section; research rows in factions/my faction hidden (the research dock area only exists at research stations).

**Planets.** `sectors[].planet { name, color, radius }` → `Model/Export.cs` `Planet` on `Sector.Planet` (validated: name not empty, radius > 0; saved in `SectorSave.Planet`, hashed by name). Default world: Beta "Korvan", Gamma "Thalassa" (the colony's planet). Display: station info row "Planet", universe/sector detail line "Planet <name> · …", 3D sector view a large sphere far below the play plane (`SectorView.PlanetNode`, no collision, not an obstacle, picking still uses the plane).

**Goods categories.** `goods[].category` → `Good.Category` (saved in `GoodSave.Category`): ore, refined, component, end (food, consumer goods, luxury goods), kit, fuel; module goods get "module" automatically (`ModuleGoods.ModuleCategory`).

**Export sink.** Station types get an optional `export { categories, goods, targetValue | target, delayTicks?, markup? }` → `ExportConfig` (validated: known categories/goods, not empty, an export good may not be in the type's own recipes; saved in `StationTypeSave.Export`). Export goods become stock-only entries (start 0) with target `targetValue ÷ base price` (3000 Cr: food 214, consumer goods 21, luxury 6), so AI traders deliver but never buy there and `TradeActions.Buy/MaxBuyable` refuse (the trade offer reads "Nur Ankauf (Export)"). A station of an export type is only allowed in a sector with a planet (`WorldFormatException`), and the lifecycle never builds export types. Flow: the station pays at purchase through the normal market (traders via `TraderSystem.Deliver`, player via `TradeActions.Sell/SellAll`) and `ExportRules.OnDelivered` turns each delivery into an `ExportBatch { Good, Amount, Paid, DueTick }` on `Station.Exports` (saved in `StationSave.Exports`, hashed, list order) plus `ExportQueued`. Waiting batches stay in the stock, so the price falls with heavy supply. `ExportSystem` (own `ExportModule` right after Production/Research, every tick, station list cached per `StructureVersion`) caps the export bids at `BasePrice × ExportMaxBidFactor` (0.9; since Phase 17 per good via `export.bids`, default world consumer/luxury goods 1.25×, food 0.9×) and sends due batches to the planet: goods leave the world, the station receives `Paid × (1 + markup)` (`ExportMarkup` 0.18, `ExportDelayTicks` 1200 = 5 min; type values override), `GoodsExported` (new money = revenue − paid). `ExportInfo.ForStation` (pure) gives the station-info line "Export an Thalassa: n Chargen unterwegs, nächste in m:ss". Content: `trade_station` (Handelsstation Beta, still sells skiffs/modules, start credits 30000) got the export config; new type `trade_post` "Planetenhandel" (trade, hangar, taxi, export only) with "Handelsstation Gamma" (id 39, (140,−90), protected, 30000 Cr); `maxStations` 35 → 36, `maxStationsPerSector` 9 → 10.

**Trader rules (found while making end goods flow).** (1) Scoring and buying are capped by what the buyer can pay (`floor(buyer credits / price)`); before, a full load was scored with an unpaid rest, so expensive goods (luxury) never had a profitable round and were never hauled (0 luxury trips in long runs). (2) Traders buy only at producers (`Station.Produces`); before, they bought food back from the colony and water from the life-support works and sold it to the farm — the consumer bought high and sold low and bled out (colony broke, permanently at 4×). (3) On arrival a station takes only what it accepts and can pay; the rest goes to the next buyer (`TradeActions.BestBuyerFor`, never a build site), unpaid dumping only if there is none (Phase 19: no dumping any more — the rest stays aboard, see there). (4) `JobAssignmentSystem` never switches a multirole ship into a job without room, even when its current job earns nothing (a 60000-tick run bought 24 Tinkers for mining that all fled into the full trade job: trade 28/14). **Final consumers:** `EconomyRules.ConsumerMaxBidFactor` (code default 4 = off, default world 2) caps the bid of stations whose recipes produce nothing (`MarketSystem.IsFinalConsumer`) — without it the colony was bistable (empty stock → 4× → its income buys too little → stock stays empty). **Content tuning:** colony income 8 → 20, recipes 10 → 30 ticks (food/CG/LS) and 20 → 80 ticks (luxury), luxury stock target 100 → 4; `maxMiners` 8 → 10 (the export's end demand pulls electronics → copper/silicon ore; with 8 or 9 miners copper ore tipped over 3× on several runs); shipyard stocks of shield/armour [1,2] → [1,3] (armour sat exactly at the 0.30× floor).

**Persistence (v17).** Additive fields only (`GoodSave.Category`, `SectorSave.Planet`, `StationTypeSave.Export`, `StationSave.Exports`); no migration (old saves are irrelevant by the user's decision; `Migrate` only lifts the version). The old migration chain and its tests still pass; old saves load without planets/export sinks.

**Tests.** New `ExportPlanetTests` (12): content (planets, export goods = end goods only, categories, value-based targets), validation (export station without planet, empty planet name, unknown category/good, export good in the type's recipe), purchase pays at once and the batch exports after exactly 1200 ticks with paid × 1.18, no kits/modules/intermediates, export line, 20000-tick run (luxury and CG trips > 0, batches exported with exactly 1.18×, no trader buys from a non-producer, colony luxury bid ≤ 2×, trade stations solvent), v17 roundtrip with open batches bit-identical incl. continuation, buy cap by buyer payment, fuel off/on (no refineries, no consumption, no refuel service, emergency refused, `MaxStations` 34/36), research off/on (no research stations, everything unlocked, no budget/cycles, service gone). Adjusted on purpose: version asserts 16 → 17; fuel and research test files (`FuelEconomyTests`, `FuelModelTests`, `RefuelRepairTests`, `ResearchTests`) build `TestWorlds` with the switches on, `ResearchTests` gate tests on `QualityUpgradeTests.AtYard` set `ResearchActive`; `WalletTests` refuel test, `FleetCommandTests` follow/return test and the v3→v4 migration test use a fuel(/research) world; `PoliticsTests` government upkeep and `CountEconomic` without research stations; `StationServiceConfigTests` knows `trade_post`; `CapabilityTests` save test ignores multirole ships (a Tinker bought in the first 500 ticks has its fit's capabilities); three `ConstructionTests` keep the lifecycle from starting its own sites (the export sinks report end-good demand at tick 0, `NoLifecycleSites`); the pirate restart test checks that the restart brings a raider into the world (it may be destroyed again before tick 12000). 481 tests green, selftest OK (new final stage on the default world), `--ui-shots … --ui-shots-phase14` adds 53–56 (flight in Gamma with planet and no fuel gauge, station info with export line, trade area at the export sink, universe detail with planet).

**Balance (fresh Release build, default world: fuel and research off).** Default seed 20000: "Preise im Zielbereich", capacity trade 13.6/14, mine 10.3/10, export 68 batches (paid 47,829 Cr, revenue 56,438, new money 8,609; food 3,641, consumer goods 22), end-good trips food 265 / consumer goods 254 / luxury 131 (156 units), colony tail prices food 0.77×, life support 1.40×, consumer goods 1.68×, luxury 2.00× (its cap; never at 4×), money stations 65k + ships 124k + treasuries 780k. 60000: green, trade 14.3/14, mine 10.3/10, export 359 batches (paid 227,600, revenue 268,568, new 40,968; food 18,827, CG 40), luxury 381 trips (448 units), consumer goods 766, colony tail food 0.58×, LS 1.20×, CG 1.38×, luxury 2.00×, money stations 84k + ships 108k + treasuries 2.09M. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: all green, trade 13.7/14.3/13.7/15.4, mine 10.0/10.3/10.0/10.1, luxury trips 103/138/113/104, colony luxury 0.38–2.0×. With `--fuel on --research on` (default seed, 20000): green, 14.0/10.3, 212 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys, 12,905 FP. The simulation is ≈2.5× faster than Phase 13 (no fuel planning, no research). Phase 13 had at 20000 ticks trade 16.9/14, mine 7.6/8, treasuries 0.46M.

**Known weaknesses.** The export mainly absorbs surplus **food**; consumer goods and luxury go to the colony first (it bids up to 2×, the sink 0.9×; Phase 17 raised the sink bid for both to 1.25× via `export.bids`) — with the sink at 1.0× more CG/luxury was exported but the electronics chain then pulled copper ore above 3× at 60000 ticks. The trade stations' cash settles at a few thousand credits (18 % markup on mostly cheap food vs the 0.03 %/tick wealth tax and, at Beta, module stock purchases): they stay solvent (protected anyway) but this limits the export volume. Treasuries grow faster than in Phase 13 (no research budget sink, colony income +12/tick): 2.1M vs 1.1M at 60000 ticks — reported, no other incomes were cut. The colony's luxury price often sits at its 2× cap (luxury is platinum-limited). Fuel and research stay switched off in the default world; their tuning from Phases 4/13 was not revisited with the new trader rules (one 20000 run green). Planet visuals are a plain sphere.

## Phase 15 (English summary): economy corrections — honest verdict, production by demand, money returns from the treasuries

**Balance verdict (`Diagnostics/MarketWatch`, `tools/SpaceSim.Balance`).** The per-good price average hid permanent states (Werft Delta had the Mk1 reactor and engine at the 4× cap 100 % of the time while the average read ~2×). `MarketWatch` samples every 20 ticks (after the first 10 % of the run) every market entry and station cash and counts trips per good (`TradeCompleted` + `MineDelivered`); the tool prints a **HINWEIS block**: entries > 50 % of the time at the upper cap (stock empty — factor ≥ 3.9 — *and* price at the entry's fixed highest bid: 4×, export goods `ExportMaxBidFactor`, final-consumer inputs `ConsumerMaxBidFactor`, research shelves `ResearchMaxBidFactor`; a factory's dynamic willingness ceiling is not a cap) or at the lower cap (price ≤ 0.27× or stock ≥ 3.7 × target), traded goods with 0 trips (a producer or an ore source plus a non-producing holder), and stations (not official ones) whose cash is < 100 Cr more than 25 % of the time. It also prints the cap share of the Mk1 reactor/engine at every ship dealer, the treasury share of the money over time, export-sink cash minimum, max capacity per job (sampled every 200 ticks), maintenance/subsidy totals and retired ships. `--strict on` returns **exit code 3** when the block is not empty; the price verdict (exit 1) and map split (exit 2) are unchanged.

**Production by demand (`Simulation/ProductionDemand`).** Cause of the permanent 4× modules: the module works and the yard produced every recipe up to 3 × target regardless of buyers (power banks, shields, extra cargo modules at 0.33×, 77 light kits at the yard) and bought inputs for it; the yard and the drive works were broke and could not pay for the scarce Mk1 modules (the drive works even had them in stock, the broke yard could not buy them, the rich trade station Beta hoarded up to 9 reactors). Rules: `OpenDemand` = Σ (target − stock) over all other stations that hold a good without producing it (consumers, shelves, build sites, export sinks); a recipe whose products nobody is short of pauses at `Target × NoDemandStockFactor` (1) instead of `× OverstockFactor` (3); demanded recipes run first (`ProductionSystem`); inputs needed only by paused recipes are accepted only up to `Target` (`StockEntry.Overstock`, a transient per-tick field set by `MarketSystem`, never saved — a floor price was tried and rejected because it made inputs look like a glut in the verdict). **Shelves** (stock-only entries: yard/trade-station modules, warehouse) accept only up to `Target × ShelfOverstockFactor` (1) — a full shelf sits at the base price instead of 0.33×; export goods keep the overstock (`ExportRules.CapBids` resets it), research stations keep it (their stock is consumed). **Pure products** (`StockEntry.NoIntake`): a station never takes deliveries of a good it produces and does not consume — traders had shuttled energy between solar plants and power plants (and water between water works), the producer with the lower stock bought its own product at the scarcity price and went broke; this one rule removed most empty-cash hints. Content: yard stocks `ship_kit_light` [10,10], `hull_plates` [20,20], `electronics` [10,10], `fuel_cells` [10,10] (target 100 before), medium/heavy kits [5,5] (were [16,5], 0.31× — now the demand rule keeps them from drawing plates).

**Working capital and fiscal brake (`UpkeepSystem`, `Simulation/FactionBudget.WorkingCapital`).** `WorkingCapital.Of(u, st)` = Σ target × base price over every entry the station takes in (inputs, shelves, export and maintenance entries; not pure products) × `StationWorkingCapitalShare` (1). The station wealth tax applies only to money above it — the export sinks had lost their 30 000 Cr start money to the tax (≈ 9 Cr/tick against an 18 % markup on cheap food). **Fiscal brake** `TreasuryTargetShare` (0.4): `UpkeepSystem.FiscalFactors` gives each faction the share `1 − treasury ÷ (0.4 × money ÷ factions)` (clamped 0..1) of the taxes it collects; payers pay the same as before, the part a full treasury does not take leaves the world (`TaxSunk`, the pre-Phase-9 sink). Without it the faucets (station incomes ≈ 48 Cr/tick, export markup) exceeded all sinks and the money grew linearly into the treasuries (2.09 M = 92 % after 60 000 ticks); with it the money is stable (≈ 0.54 M at 20 000, 0.62 M at 60 000) and the treasury share settles at 16–25 %.

**Treasury spending (ActorModule, interval 50).** `MaintenanceSystem`: every finished faction (or member) station except hideouts consumes per day `BuildCost × MaintenanceShare` (0.03) at base prices, split by `MaintenanceMaterials` (steel 0.55, hull plates 0.3, electronics 0.15). Stations that do not hold a material get a **pure maintenance entry** (stock-only, target `MaintenanceStockDays` (2) days of need, bid capped at `MaintenanceMaxBidFactor` × base (1.25) in `MarketSystem`); traders deliver through normal trade, the station pays, and on consumption the owner faction's treasury refunds the station the consumed material at its market price (`TreasuryUpkeep` "maintenance"). Missing material only grows `Station.MaintenanceBacklog` (≤ one day of need, saved, hashed; status line "Instandhaltung: gedeckt / offen: …" in the station info, `Maintenance.StatusText`) — nothing decays. This is the permanent material demand Phase 8 lacked (≈ 75 000 Cr refunded per 20 000 ticks). `SubsidySystem`: a faction tops up its eligible stations (official, bankruptcy-protected, critical, final consumer or export sink; never hideouts) whose cash is below `SubsidyFloorShare` (0.5) × working capital, at most `SubsidyPerDay` (2400) per station and day, never below `SubsidyReserve` (20 000) (`StationSubsidized`, `TreasuryUpkeep` "subsidy"; ≈ 110 000 Cr per 20 000 ticks, mostly copper smelter, wafer fab, drive works, yard, colony). Official stations are eligible because they now have markets (maintenance) and must pay their deliveries.

**Raw chain and lifecycle.** Copper ore tipped over 3× (seed 5 at 20 000, seed 1 at 60 000) and silicon hit 4× because the raw supply cannot carry the electronics demand (silicate belt depleted to 1/14 rocks; copper ore 0.25 units/tick against a smelter capacity of 0.6). Changes: `metal_rock` yields iron 4 → 3, copper 4 → 5 (iron sat at 0.4×); `maxMiners` 10 → 12; and the lifecycle no longer builds a producer whose **raw-ore** input is itself scarce (`StationLifecycleSystem.UpstreamProducer`: stock ratio of the ore > `BuildPriceRatio` and no buildable producer → build nothing) — extra copper smelters and wafer fabs without ore only competed for the same ore with empty stocks at 4×. Tried and kept out: larger/faster silicate belts (no clear gain), 11 miners (seed 8 still 3.15×).

**Fleet capacity and idle ships.** Trade capacity reached 14.8/14 at 60 000 ticks (seed 4) although `HasRoom`/`ChooseModel` held: quality rolls (Masterwork bonus cargo slot) and NPC cargo upgrades raised units after the check. Now `ShipQualityRoll.Roll(…, accept)` falls back a grade when the rolled fit would exceed limit + overshoot (`FleetSystem.Fits`, both the model-choice and the clone path), and `UpgradeSystem.Best` skips upgrades that would. A trader/miner idle for `IdleRetireTicks` (6000) is retired even with money (`ShipRetired`; credits go to its owner's treasury, a free single ship's leave with it) — e.g. a multirole ship whose job earns nothing and whose other job is full; 0 retirements in the balance runs.

**Small fixes.** `PirateSystem.ServiceAtBase` refuels only with `fuelActive` (it paid for police/raider fuel from the treasury with fuel off); `TradeActions.BestBuyerFor` no longer sorts per call (tie-break by id, same result).

**Persistence.** Additive only, save stays v17: `StationSave.MaintenanceBacklog`; new rules in the saved `EconomyRules`. `UniverseHash` mixes the backlog. Pure maintenance entries are normal market entries (saved).

**Tests.** New `EconomyFixTests` (14): MarketWatch hints (cap, empty cash, idle goods, caps by entry kind), production pause without/with demand and the old rule, inputs of paused recipes only up to target, shelf vs export acceptance and shelf price, producers do not take their pure product (3000 ticks without an energy delivery to a producer), working-capital tax and fiscal brake (payers unchanged), maintenance (entries, consumption, refund, backlog cap, bid cap), subsidy (cap per day, floor, reserve, eligibility), lifecycle raw-ore rule, idle retirement (free and promoted owner, builders stay), quality fallback by capacity, `ServiceAtBase` without fuel, roundtrip with backlog bit-identical, and a **20 000-tick run of the unchanged default world** (switches off): capacity max ≤ limit + overshoot, no ghost orders, food/CG/luxury trips > 20, food and CG exported, export-sink cash < 25 % of the time empty, yard Mk1 reactor/engine < 50 % at the cap, treasuries < 50 % of the money. Adjusted on purpose: `ExportPlanetTests` (Gamma holds steel/electronics as maintenance entries — checked that they never become export batches), `ResearchTests` lifecycle test fills iron ore/energy (raw-ore rule), `ActorTests` tax comparison disables maintenance/subsidy/brake (they depend on the treasury), `ModuleGoodsTests` willingness test uses the new yard targets (light kit 10, hull plates 20, medium kits no longer on stock), `OutfitterShipyardTests` v7→v8 compares the trade station's market without pure maintenance entries, `WalletTests` v6→v7 runs without maintenance/subsidy (no government stations before v13), `FuelEconomyTests` long run allows police/pirates a 5-tick arrival slack (their systems run every 5 ticks — a latent assumption); **sharpened back**: the pirate restart test again requires raiders at the end (default seed: they exist), the construction tests use `OnlyTestSites` (`MaxActiveSites` = their sites) instead of the `BuildCooldown = int.MaxValue` crutch (the builder test needs nothing). 495 tests green, selftest OK (station info shows the maintenance line).

**Balance (fresh Release build, default world: fuel and research off; Phase 14 → Phase 15).** All 15 runs "Preise im Zielbereich" (Phase 14: seed 5 at 20 000 and seed 1 at 60 000 exit 1). Yard Delta Mk1 reactor/engine at the cap: 100 %/100 % → 0–15 %/0 % (20 000, seeds 1–8 and default), 45–52 %/30–38 % → 0 %/0 % (60 000). Traders/miners 14/12 (limits 14/12; capacity max ≤ 14.5 and ≤ 12.5 on every run). Export default seed 20 000: food 3 641 / CG 22 / luxury 0 → 7 264 / 109 / 7; 60 000: 18 827 / 40 / 0 → 23 607 / 670 / 295; seeds 1–4 at 60 000: CG 1 050–2 318, luxury 143–459; seeds 1–8 at 20 000: CG 109–940, luxury 7–183. Export-sink cash min/end (default 20 000): Beta 7/4 284 → 11 240/21 203, Gamma 475/2 117 → 11 623/15 211; minimum over all runs 1 545. Treasury share 80 % (20 000) / 92 % (60 000) → 16 % / 20 % (seeds 16–25 %, flat after ≈ 30 000 ticks); money 0.97 M / 2.28 M → 0.54 M / 0.62 M. Police purchases now succeed (default 20 000: 0 → 4; 60 000: 5 → 12, with 10 decommissions — the desired count follows the threat). HINWEIS lines per run 23–32 → 1–11 (remaining: export sinks often without CG/luxury in stock, the copper smelter Alpha's cash often near 0 — it runs at its ore limit on a thin margin —, a few lower-cap hull-plate entries from dumped rests). `--fuel on --research on` (20 000): green, 104 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys, 17 275 FP, 4 unlocks (Phase 14: 12 905 FP). Simulation ≈ 35 % slower than Phase 14 (more trading, per-entry market rules; still faster than Phase 13).

**Known weaknesses.** The fiscal brake is a sink by design (money above the treasury target leaves the world); total money is now stable but the treasuries rarely fund stations any more (0 treasury investments in the runs; stations and ship accounts invest). Consumer and luxury exports are still small at 20 000 ticks on the default seed (the colony and the electronics/platinum chains take them first); luxury exports vary 7–183 per seed (fixed in Phase 17: per-good export bids, cause was the 0.9× sink bid below the factories' ask). Copper ore remains the tightest raw good (single snapshots up to 4× on some seeds; tail averages ≤ 3) — fixed in Phase 16 (cause: willingness from the smelter's own scarcity price). Unpaid rest dumping (Phase 14 last resort) still pushes tiny maintenance entries (hull plates, target 1) to the lower cap. Police buy/decommission churn (≈ 1 per 5000 ticks). Pirates often end a run with 0–1 raiders (restart works).

## Phase 16 (English summary): copper chain, grid-independent verdict, intake reasons in trade

**Cause of the copper shortage (measured).** Not supply: on seed 11 (20 000 ticks) miners mined 11 644 copper ore but sold only 2 989 to the smelter; 6 680 were dumped *at the smelter* because it was broke (cash 0 at 300 of 301 dumps), 1 444 more at the steelworks. The smelter was broke because its input ceiling was derived from its *own* copper price, which sits at 3–4× while its own copper stock is empty; it paid on average 56.4 Cr per ore (3.5× base) and then sold the copper at what the electronics fab bid (83.6 Cr = 2.4×): ≈ 177 Cr cost vs 167 Cr revenue per cycle — broke → ore dumped → no copper → own copper price 4× → ore bid 4× → broke. `MinPriceFactor`, crowding, risk, belt size and miner count were not the cause.

**Fix (`MarketSystem`, rule `WillingnessFromBuyers`, default true).** The product value behind a factory's willingness ceiling is min(own price, best buyer bid). `MarketSystem.BuyerBids` = highest price per good over finished stations that hold it and are not pure producers of it (`Produces && !Consumes`), from the previous tick's (saved) prices and deliberately without the derived per-tick fields (`Overstock`, `NoIntake`), so save → load → continue stays bit-identical. Applies to every factory. New rule `BuildChecksRawOre` (default true) switches the Phase 15 raw-ore filter of the lifecycle (tests only).

**Verdict independent of `--every` (`MarketWatch`, balance tool).** `MarketWatch.Sample` also stores the per-good price/base average (same definition as `EconomyStats`) per sample; `AverageRatio(good, fromTick)` / `SamplesSince`. The balance verdict (exit 1), the bottleneck-module line and the propellant line use the dense sample (every 20 ticks from ¾ of the run), not the `--every` snapshots. The tool steps to the next multiple of 20 so samples always land on the same ticks, and the last chunk runs to exactly `--ticks` (a non-dividing `--every` used to end the run early). New lines "Urteilsprobe" and "Urteilswerte" (three highest/lowest goods). Same run → same hash and verdict with `--every 5000` and `10000` (checked on 13 seeds).

**Intake reasons (`Trading/MarketInfo.Acceptance`, pure).** Returns `Intake(Units, IntakeLimit, Reason)` with the same amount a sale would move (min(`AcceptableUnits`, credits ÷ price)): "Stellt die Ware selbst her" (`NoIntake`), "Lager voll", "Kein Bedarf: Produktion pausiert" (input of a paused recipe), "Nimmt nur Endwaren" (export sink, good not in its market) / "Handelt diese Ware nicht", "Baustelle: nur noch n" / "Baustelle: Material vollständig" / "Verlassene Baustelle", "Station kann nicht zahlen" / "kann nur n bezahlen"; `NoteFor(held)` gives the row note ("nimmt noch n"). `ServiceOffer` gained an optional `Note` (additive); `TradeService` `sell:` offers carry it and `Execute` refuses with the same text. `MarketInfo.BestBuyerForGood` only names stations that accept. `TradeActions.BestBuyer` (mining autopilot, NPC miner leftovers) counts per good only what a station takes in at least one whole unit (a sub-unit rest counts if taken completely), so nobody drives to a full station for fractions (since Phase 17 the same rule also governs the sale itself, `TradeActions.SaleUnits`). Player fleet automation needed no change (traders use `AcceptableUnits`, miners require 5 units).

**UI.** `TradeScreen`: the sell note is shown in the row, tile and editor (orange when cargo is aboard and nothing is accepted, else dimmed); "Verkaufen" is disabled when the station accepts nothing; the price difference to the best buyer has a proper sign. Selftest: steel aboard at the steelworks → reason visible, sell disabled, refusal with the same reason, best buyer accepts steel. `--ui-shots` adds `57-dock-handel-annahmegrund.png` (also with `--ui-shots-phase14`).

**Tests.** New `CopperIntakeTests` (12): willingness from buyer bids vs the old rule, buyer bids independent of derived fields, dense `AverageRatio`, every intake case, trade offer note = refusal message without state change, best buyer only among accepting stations, `BestBuyer` ignores sub-unit acceptance. The Phase 15 20 000-tick default-world test additionally asserts copper smelter cash empty < 25 %, smelter subsidy < 3000 Cr, copper ore < 3×. Sharpened: the research lifecycle test switches the raw-ore filter off (`BuildChecksRawOre`) instead of filling ore/energy and now also asserts the allowed type is built in the first half; the fuel long run uses the exact rule "a transit may be overdue only until the next tick of its system" (police/pirates interval 5, else 1) instead of a 5-tick slack. Adjusted on purpose: `ActorTests` tax comparison runs without security (police purchases/upkeep depend on the treasury and feed back through raids), `ServiceTests` expect the new refusal texts. 507 tests green, selftest OK. Save stays v17.

**Balance (fresh Release build, default world).** All runs "Preise im Zielbereich": default 20 000/60 000, seeds 1–12 at 20 000 with `--every 5000` and `10000` (identical hash and verdict), seeds 1–4 at 60 000, `--fuel on --research on` (97 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys). Before: seed 11 red on both grids, seed 2 red with `--every 10000`. Copper ore (dense tail) 0.79–2.54× → 0.84–0.97× (60 000: 0.71–0.76×). Copper smelter Alpha: empty cash 49–64 % of the time on 8 of 13 seeds with 9 000–17 400 Cr subsidy (default 60 000: 59 %, 51 055 Cr) → 0 % and 0 Cr everywhere; no copper HINWEIS lines left. Lowest good 0.35–0.48× → 0.34–0.46× (ice, hydrogen, titanium ore). Traders/miners 10–16 / 11–12 ships (capacity 13.9–14.5 / 11.8–12.5), 0 emergency buys. Subsidies default 20 000: 80 932 → 57 308 Cr; export CG 109 → 264, luxury 7 → 84.

**Known weaknesses.** The new willingness applies to all factories; prices are compressed (highest good mostly iron ore/steel 1.2–2.0×; iron ore is now the priciest raw good, seed 10 1.96×). Remaining HINWEIS lines (2–7 per run) are the export sinks without CG/luxury stock, occasional modules/intermediates at the lower cap and briefly broke new module works. Miners still dump what nobody can buy or pay (Phase 14 last resort), now mostly titanium-ore leftovers.

## Phase 17 (English summary): open points from Phase 16 — one sale rule, performance, export sinks get consumer and luxury goods, two sharper tests

**One quantity rule for every sale (`TradeActions.SaleUnits(have, want, cap)`).** Before, `MarketInfo.Acceptance` reported 0 units ("Lager voll", "Station kann nicht zahlen") when a station took or could pay for less than one unit, while `TradeActions.Sell` still sold the fraction. Now: if the station takes everything aboard (`cap` = min(`AcceptableUnits`, credits ÷ price) ≥ `have`) and everything is wanted, everything goes — also a sub-unit ore rest from mining, so no unsellable remainder stays in the hold; otherwise only whole units move (floored), below one unit nothing. Used by `Sell`, `SellAll` (hence the mining autopilot and NPC miners), `Quote`/new `Sellable(u, ship, station)` (units and revenue `SellAll` would move, with the cash left after previous goods — so `BestBuyer` scores exactly what a sale would do), the `sell:` offer amount (`Intake.SellableFrom(held)`; `Intake` gained the unrounded `Cap`) and the note (`Intake.NoteFor(held)` follows the same rule; a fraction that fits completely has no note). The `sell_all` offer now carries the units `SellAll` would move (not the cargo total) and a note "Station nimmt nichts von der Fracht an" when that is 0; `Execute` succeeds iff units > 0 and returns that note. `TradeScreen` enables "Verkaufen"/"Alles verkaufen" iff the offer amount > 0 (before: held ≥ 1 and amount ≥ 1, so a 0.4 ore rest could not be sold by hand). NPC behaviour changes slightly (miners sell whole units into nearly full stations; hashes differ from Phase 16). Tests: offer amount > 0 ⇔ `Execute` succeeds and moves exactly that amount over 120 combinations of hold/acceptance/cash/quantity, the same for `sell_all`, mining fractions (partial sale leaves whole units + fraction, the next station that takes everything clears the hold, a station taking less than the fraction refuses), the note rule. Selftest: a 0.4 iron-ore rest at the steelworks is sellable and sold completely; with acceptance 0.5 and 3 units aboard both buttons are off and both executions are refused with the row's reason.

**Performance (no behaviour change — `UniverseHash` and the whole balance output except timing lines identical before/after on the default seed and seeds 1–3 at 20 000 and 60 000 ticks).** Measured per system (the balance tool now wraps every system in its own stopwatch: line "Rechenzeit je System (Top 5)", `--systemtime off` for the old single security stopwatch; the summary line also prints ms/tick) and with `dotnet-trace`: MarketSystem 36 %, TraderSystem 26 %, JobAssignment 8 %, MinerSystem 8 %, Production 6 %, GC ≈ 17 % of the trace. Changes: `Station.RoleOf/Roles()` — per-good recipe role (input/output) cached in a byte array, valid while the recipe list is the same list with the same length and first/last recipe (recipes are only appended, removed or replaced as a whole at runtime); `Produces`/`Consumes`/`IsStockOnly` use it. `Universe.GoodOf(id)`/`GoodIdBound` (goods as an array, rebuilt per tick). `MarketSystem`: roles hoisted per station, buyer bids/ceilings/open demand as good-indexed arrays (`ProductionDemand.OpenDemand(u, float[])`, `Demanded`/`Saturated` overloads; ceiling goods kept in insertion order = former dictionary order), `Maintenance.IsPureTypeEntry` (the type part of `IsPureEntry`). `Universe.StationsTrading` keeps its index across ticks while the station set and the total number of market entries are unchanged (entries are only ever added), plus `StationsTradingEntries(good)` (parallel entry list); route cache kept across ticks while nothing is restricted and the gate/sector counts are unchanged. `Universe.ShipsById()/StationsById()` — cached id-ordered snapshots (exactly validated on every call; a rebuild allocates a new list so running loops keep their snapshot) replace per-tick `OrderBy(...).ToList()` in JobAssignment, Upgrade, ShipJobs.UsedUnits, ThreatMap, Construction, Security, Pirates, Actors, FactionBudget, Hangar, PlayerFleet, OrderSystem, Refuel (filtered loops whose body can change the filter's inputs are still materialised). `TraderSystem.BestTrade`: reused buffers, travel time/fuel/risk memoised per buy station and per (buy, sell) pair within a call, entries from the index instead of dictionary lookups, one `AcceptableUnits` per pair. `MinerSystem.BestMine`: mix value and class base price memoised per call; `Belt.HasOre(s, f, threshold)` = exactly `OreIn ≥ threshold` with early exit (double accumulation as LINQ `Sum`). `Refuel.TripFuelCost` checks the fuel switch first. Result (same machine, runs one after another): default seed 60 000 ticks 0.163 → 0.106 ms/tick (−35 %; Phase 15 was 0.137, Phase 14 ≈ 0.10), 20 000 ticks 0.238 → 0.190; seeds 1–3 at 60 000: 0.179/0.174/0.159 → 0.103/0.097/0.096. After all Phase 17 changes (more trade through the export sinks): default 60 000 0.115 ms/tick, seeds 1–4 0.098–0.122. Top 5 now (default 60 000): MarketSystem 28 %, TraderSystem 23 %, MinerSystem 13 %, ProductionSystem 6 %, UpgradeSystem 5 %.

**Export sinks got too few consumer and luxury goods — cause (measured with a throwaway flow harness: stock changes per system × station type × good).** Not production capacity and not the colony: on the default seed (20 000 ticks) the goods factories and the luxury workshop held 86–92 units on average (0 % empty) at 1.1–1.2× base, the colony was fully supplied (667/667 consumer-goods cycles, 216/250 luxury), but the sinks bid at most 0.9× base — below the factories' ask, so a trip to a sink paid only when a factory was overstocked. Through the Phase 16 willingness rule (product value = min(own price, best buyer bid)) the low sink bid also capped what the luxury workshop bid for platinum (≈1.06× vs the refinery's 1.21× ask → platinum 62 % empty at the workshop). Raw ore (iron ore → steel for consumer goods, platinum ore) is the next limit, not the first. **Fix (data-driven):** `export.bids` in the world file (`ExportConfig.Bids`/`BidOf`, key = good key or category of the sink, good wins, factor in (0, 4], validated; saved additively in `ExportConfigSave.Bids`, no version bump): default world consumer goods and luxury goods 1.25×, food stays 0.9×; markup (18 %), delay and flow of the sink unchanged. `ExportRules.CapBids` and `MarketWatch.UpperCap` use the per-good bid. With the higher bid the luxury workshop outbids for platinum and the lifecycle answers the new steel/platinum scarcity by building a steelworks/platinum refinery in its normal slots. Tried: 1.1× (luxury still 39–65 % empty on half the seeds), 1.5× (slightly more export, more money creation), `maxStations` 38/40 (fixed the seed-3 water case below but built extra module works whose titanium sits at the lower cap). **Balance tool:** a line per sink "Exportsenke …: leer am Gebotsdeckel Nahrung x %, Konsumgüter y %, Luxusgüter z % | Gebot …", and sink entries empty at their bid cap are listed as `EXPORTNACHFRAGE:` (unsatisfied export demand, not an error; `MarketWatch.IsExportDemand`) instead of `HINWEIS:` and do not count for `--strict`.

**Numbers (fresh Release build; before = Phase 16 rules + the Phase 17 sale rule, i.e. only the bids differ).** Default 20 000: export food/CG/luxury 7 274/340/46 → 6 593/964/248 (Phase 16 itself: 7 292/264/84); sink empty-at-cap share CG/luxury Beta 69/93 % → 6/22 %, Gamma 68/59 % → 5/10 %. Default 60 000: CG 1 352 → 3 516, luxury 178 → 1 014; shares ≤ 31 %. Seeds 1–12 at 20 000: CG 139–772 → 907–1 140, luxury 46–120 → 240–332, every sink below 50 % for CG and luxury on every seed (max 31 %; before 35–93 %, mostly > 50 %); seeds 1–4 at 60 000: CG 696–1 733 → 3 021–3 683, luxury 180–388 → 860–1 032, max 30 %. Colony unchanged (all consumer-goods cycles, luxury ≈ 213/250). All runs "Preise im Zielbereich" (default 20 000/60 000, seeds 1–12 at 20 000, seeds 1–4 at 60 000, `--fuel on --research on`), traders/miners 14–15/11–13 ships (seed 9: 10 traders with large hulls; capacity ≤ 14.5/12.5), 0 emergency fuel buys; highest good 1.02–1.62× (mostly iron ore/steel; food on seeds 3/7), lowest 0.35–0.44× (ice, titanium, titanium ore; iron ore at 60 000). EXPORTNACHFRAGE lines 2–4 → 0–1 per run (remaining: food on seeds 3 and 7, see below). Money: default 20 000 0.57 M → 0.69 M (treasury share 20 → 23 %), 60 000 0.61 M → 0.76 M (flat at 27 % from 30 000 ticks; more new money from the markup on more valuable exports).

**Tests that had lost coverage.** New `Kassen_JeAkteurAusEreignissenErklaerbar_MitSicherheitInstandhaltungSubventionUndBremse_20000Ticks`: default world with security, maintenance, subsidy and fiscal brake on; for every non-player actor end treasury = start + taxes + incomes + tolls + refunds + retired-ship money − upkeeps (police, pirates, maintenance, subsidy, government, locks, research) − treasury investments, and the run actually exercised police, pirates, maintenance, subsidy, government upkeep, fencing and the brake (the existing 20 000-tick test only checked the sum over all treasuries). New `Stationsbau_RohstoffFilter_KeineHuetteBeiKnappemErz` (theory, through `StationLifecycleSystem`): steel scarce everywhere; iron ore scarce + filter on → nothing is built, ore available → a steelworks site, filter off → steelworks site (the unit-level `UpstreamProducer` test in `EconomyFixTests` stays). New file `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/OpenPointsTests.cs` (14 tests incl. the cache tests: roles follow recipe changes, market index/snapshots follow added entries and direct dictionary removals, route cache across ticks with a closed gate, export bids content/validation/cap/save, 20 000-tick sink acceptance). Adjusted on purpose: `ServiceTests` (sell offer limited by cash now 2 whole units instead of 2.5; offer amounts via `SaleUnits`/`Sellable`), `ExportPlanetTests` (luxury bid cap from `BidOf`). 521 tests green, selftest OK. Save stays v17.

**Known weaknesses.** On seeds 3 and 7 (and seed 3 at 60 000) food export drops to ≈ 2 800 (60 000: 9 100) because the farms run short of water: the lifecycle's last free slots went to a steelworks/platinum refinery instead of a second water plant (station cap 36); the colony is still fully supplied and food stays < 1.9×; it happened on seed 7 before Phase 17 as well (fixed in Phase 18: the cause was the lifecycle's build choice plus a start world short of water and farm capacity). Remaining HINWEIS lines (0–5 per run) are inputs at the lower cap in module works and yards (titanium, fuel cells, Mk3 modules). Money in circulation rises ≈ 25 % (export markup on CG/luxury) and settles. The CG chain is now steel-limited (iron ore mining); luxury is platinum-limited. (Phase 18 measured it more precisely: the CG limit was the single goods factory's capacity — inputs short only 1–9 % of the time —, luxury was platinum-ore-limited.)

## Phase 18 (English summary): cargo rests, water supply of the farms, raw material base

**Cargo rests.** `Trading/CargoJettison` (`Issue`, `Jettison(u, ship, good, units)`; units ≤ 0 = all): player ships only, the good leaves the world without revenue, event `CargoJettisoned(ship, sector, good, units)`; works docked and in flight. The `trade` service gained the strict key `drop:<GoodId>` (one offer per good aboard, price 0, amount = held, note = why the station does not take all of it, null if it does; player ships only; success publishes `ServicePerformed` + `CargoJettisoned`). **Rest tolerance** (`EconomyRules.SaleRestTolerance`, default 1, 0 = Phase 17 rule): `TradeActions.SaleUnits(have, want, cap, allCap)` sells everything when the overshoot over the station's acceptance is below one unit, the station accepts the good at all (acceptance > 0, not a build site) and can pay the whole amount (`TradeActions.SaleCaps` → `(Cap, AllCap)`; `Intake.AllCap`); otherwise whole units as before. One rule for offer amount, note, `Sell`, `SellAll`, `Sellable`/`Quote`/`BestBuyer` (player and NPC miners — NPC behaviour changes slightly). The mining autopilot treats cargo below `MiningAutopilot.RestUnits` (1) as a rest (`HasLoad`): it does not fly to a buyer for it and does not stop when a station does not take it (before: with an unsellable 0.4 rest and an empty sector it stopped with "Kein Käufer erreichbar", after docking with "Station kauft nicht"); it never jettisons by itself. UI: `Ui/Kit/KitConfirm` (two-tap confirmation: the first tap arms "Wirklich? nochmal tippen", a second within 3 s executes; the state lives in the screen so it survives rebuilds). Trade area: rows also for cargo the station does not trade (price "—", note from the drop offer); when the station does not take all of a good aboard, the expanded editor shows "Abwerfen" (200×56) next to the reason (chosen quantity, "Max" = all), executed only via `Execute("drop:<id>")`. Command centre "Besitz" ship detail: cargo list with "Abwerfen" per good (any own ship, also in flight), executed through `CargoJettison`. Selftest: 0.4 iron ore at acceptance 0.2 sold completely; steel at the steelworks shows "Abwerfen", the first tap changes nothing, the second drops it without revenue; the ship detail drops a 0.3 food rest after the second tap.

**Water supply — cause (measured, seed 3, 20 000 ticks).** One water plant made 0.2 water/tick (ice never short, its output always hauled: 4 087 ice in, ≈ 3 856 water out) for two farms (0.2 each) and the life-support works (≈ 0.06): Farm Gamma had no water 87 % of the time, the lifecycle's farm 41 %; food export 2 828 (default seed with two water plants: farms dry 14 %/4 %, 6 593). The lifecycle had 4 free slots; the first went to a farm at tick 0 on every seed (food ratio 6.4 from the empty export sinks; at start every stock sits at half its target, ratio 2 everywhere); a water plant came only if it won the race against ship kits (ratio 4.3 → a second shipyard on seeds 3/7), Mk2 module shelves (a second drive works on seed 7), steel and electronics; seed 14 built a second farm twice (also ≈ 2 700 food). **Changes.** (1) `StationLifecycleSystem.UpstreamProducer(…, chainThreshold)` / rule `BuildFollowsChain` (default true): if a manufactured input of the chosen producer is itself scarce (> `BuildPriceRatio`), the producer of the scarcest such input is built instead (recursive, `MaxChainDepth` 3; an unbuildable scarce raw-ore input still means "build nothing", Phase 15). (2) `FleetGoodWithProducer`: goods no recipe consumes and no export sink takes (kits, modules) never trigger a build while a finished producer exists (a yard/module works makes ≈ 1 000 per recipe and 20 000 ticks, the fleet needs ≈ 10–20 kits and ≈ 80 shelf modules). (3) Content: start station "Farm Beta" (id 40, Beta (100, 90), protected; `maxStations` 36 → 37 so the lifecycle keeps its 4 slots) and the water plant at 2 ice + 2 energy → 2 water per 5 ticks (it was the only refinery at 1 unit per cycle; ice is the cheapest raw good at ≈ 0.35×). With (1)+(2) alone food converged to ≈ 4 450 on all seeds (one farm's capacity) — the start world lacked a farm since the Phase 14 export sinks, which is why the lifecycle spent its first slot on one on every seed.

**Raw material base — measured (chain probe, 60 000 ticks, default seed and seeds 1/2, Phase 17).** Belts are never the limit (0.6–1.2 k ore per belt, rocks near target). 11–12 miners: ≈ 1.4–1.5 on metal belts, 0.9–1.1 on the rare belt. **Consumer goods:** the single goods factory had its inputs short only 1–9 % of the time and shipped 5 146–5 647 of its 6 000 capacity, CG at the factory 1.53–1.61× → factory capacity, not steel (steelworks were ore-short 64–73 %, the next limit). **Luxury:** workshop platinum short 38–47 % (1 700–1 766 of 5 000 capacity), refinery ore short 75–87 %, platinum ore 3 519–3 888 mined (rare rock: 1 of 5 chunks platinum at chunk chance 0.3). **Changes (content):** goods factory 2 steel + 2 electronics → 2 CG per 10 ticks; `rare_rock` titanium 4 → 3, platinum 1 → 2; `metal_rock` iron 3 → 3.5, titanium 2 → 1.5 (titanium ore was the lowest good at 0.35×). Rejected: iron 4/titanium 1 (iron ore fell to 0.40×, CG not higher), iron unchanged (a lifecycle steelworks starved of ore on seed 5), counting queued export batches out of the sinks' stock in the lifecycle's scarcity (built third farms instead of goods factories). **After:** CG shipped 6 351–6 494 (factory inputs short 0–15 %), luxury workshop never short, platinum ore +31–39 %; the next limit for CG/luxury export is the sinks' intake (targets 21/6 units, batches wait 1 200 ticks in the stock).

**Persistence.** Additive only, save stays v17 (new rules in the saved `EconomyRules`; the new start station is content).

**Tests.** New `CargoWaterOreTests` (8): jettison rule (player in flight, NPC refused, no event on failure), `drop:` offers/strict keys/notes/events, mining autopilot with a 0.4 rest (keeps searching, does not stop when docked; a whole unaccepted unit still stops), chain rule (farm → water plant → powerplant, raw ore → nothing, rule off → farm, two links CG → electronics → copper), fleet goods (kit/module vs steel/food; the lifecycle builds the farm instead of a yard, rule off → yard), content, 20 000-tick default world (farms dry < 15 %, food > 5 000, CG > 900, luxury > 200, no yard/module works built). New `OpenPointsTests.Resttoleranz_NimmtRestUnterEinerEinheit`. Adjusted on purpose: `OpenPointsTests` Phase 17 rest assertions run with `SaleRestTolerance = 0` (the old rule stays tested), `ExportPlanetTests` `MaxStations` 34/36 → 35/37, `LifecycleTests.UnknownGood…` replaces the new platinum weight, `ActorTests` treasury build sets every stock to target except food (start stocks sit at ratio 2, the chain would go to ice), `WalletTests` v6→v7 runs one sector-control pass before demoting (a station finished in the last 50 ticks changed the influence that loading recomputes). 529 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build, default world; Phase 17 → Phase 18).** Every run "Preise im Zielbereich" (exit 0): default 20 000/60 000, seeds 1–20 at 20 000, seeds 1–6 at 60 000, 200 000 `--every 50000`, `--fuel on --research on` (78 tank-ups, 0 emergency buys, 19 250 FP). **Food export at 20 000, seeds 1–20:** 2 682–7 181 (seeds 3/7/14: 2 828/2 772/2 682) → 6 726–8 674 (minimum 88 % of the median ≈ 7 600; default 6 593 → 7 953); sink food empty at the bid cap ≤ 5 % (before up to 73 %), 0 EXPORTNACHFRAGE lines. **CG/luxury at 20 000:** 907–1 140 / 240–332 → 1 147–1 505 / 280–433. **60 000:** CG default 3 516 → 4 178 (+19 %), seeds 1–6 3 021–3 710 → 4 247–4 530 (mean of the 7 runs 3 428 → 4 344, +27 %); luxury 1 014 → 1 187 (+17 %), mean 1 013 → 1 206 (+19 %); food ≈ 24 000 unchanged (seed 3 9 096 → 24 277). **200 000:** CG 12 214 → 15 413, luxury 3 523 → 4 531, food 85 890 → 82 472. **Money:** 20 000 0.69 M → 0.75 M, 60 000 0.76 M → 0.83 M, 200 000 0.75 M → 0.83 M, flat from 50 000 ticks (treasury share 27–29 %); new export money 60 000 268 k → 298 k, 200 000 946 k → 1 130 k. Lowest good 0.35× (titanium ore, ice), highest 1.29–1.30× (iron ore/steel; Phase 17: food 1.57–1.62×). Traders/miners 13–16 / 10–12 ships (capacity ≤ 14.5/12.5). HINWEIS lines 0–4 per run (sums 29 → 29 at 20 000, 12 → 14 at 60 000; mostly maintenance entries of electronics/hull plates at the lower cap). Time per tick (single runs): 60 000 0.115 → 0.105 ms, 20 000 0.183 → 0.195 ms (±5 % noise).

**Known weaknesses.** CG export rose +19 % on the default seed at 60 000 (guide +30 %); the factory is no longer the limit, the export sinks' small value-based targets (21 CG, 6 luxury units, batches wait 1 200 ticks in the stock) are — raising them would mainly create more export money. The lifecycle still decides all 4 free slots in the first ≈ 6 000 ticks (transient stocks) and never revisits them; a steelworks built on scarce steel can still sit ore-starved for a while (Phase 19: the station review retires such a station for a persistent scarcity). Single entries sit at 0.26–0.28× (titanium at module works, electronics in maintenance entries; Phase 19: caused by traders dumping loads above the acceptance — fixed). Jettison is the player's decision; the autopilot ignores rests but never drops them.

## Phase 19 (English summary): station review (retirement/conversion), price floors, jettison in the fleet, confirm button

**Measured first (200 000 ticks, default seed and seeds 1–3, rule off).** All 4 lifecycle slots are taken 100 % of the time after ≈ 6 000 ticks. Built stations: some are ore-starved most of the time (steelworks 4/96 % ran/inputs missing, plating works 6/94 %), one redundant titanium works ran 5 % with titanium oversupplied; at the same time the only persistent scarcity without a free slot was copper (seeds 2/3: 21 %/13 % of the time, answer: a copper smelter); default seed and seed 1: none. Construction ships: idle builders earn only the 10 % fee and are abandoned as broke (`ShipBankruptTicks`); the fleet's `build` job needs a builder as buyer, so after the first wave none ever came back (0 at 200 000).

**Utilization (persistent).** `Station.BuiltTick` (set by `StationFactory.Create`, start world 0), `Station.Activity` and `Station.Starved`: exponential moving averages (time constant `EconomyRules.UtilizationWindowTicks` 6 000; 15 000 idle ticks take 1 → 0.08) of the share of due production cycles in which at least one recipe ran / nothing ran because inputs were missing (the rest paused without demand), updated in `ProductionSystem`; saved (`StationSave.BuiltTick/Activity/Starved`, additive) and hashed.

**Retirement/conversion (`Simulation/StationReview`, called from `StationLifecycleSystem`).** The lifecycle now measures scarcity every step (`StationLifecycleSystem.Scan` → `LifecycleScan(Ratios, Scarce, Glut, ScarceGoods)`, `ProducerFor` = the Phase 15/16/18 producer choice) and tracks `Universe.ScarceSince` (good → first tick of the current uninterrupted scarcity, saved and hashed). `TryRetire` fires only when all of these hold: rule `StationRetireActive`; no retirement within `RetireCooldownTicks` (15 000, `Universe.LastStationRetireTick`, saved); all economic slots taken (`MaxStations`); build cooldown passed and a site slot free; a good scarce for ≥ `RetireScarceTicks` (5 000) whose producer type (`ProducerFor`) is not already being built. Candidate stations (`StationReview.Useless`): finished, with recipes, not `BankruptcyProtected`/immortal, not official, hideout, export sink or final consumer, grace `RetireGraceTicks` (20 000) since `BuiltTick` passed, `Activity` < `RetireMaxActivity` (0.1) and none of its products scarce (stock ratio ≤ `RetireMaxOutputRatio` 1 = hysteresis); never critical (last producer), never the wanted type, never with player storage or a docked player ship.
