The lowest activity is retired (`Retire`): the owner's treasury receives the station's cash plus its stock at `RetireSalvageShare` (0.25) × base price (`TreasuryIncome` "salvage"), `Universe.RemoveStation(id, "retired")` cancels orders as for bankruptcy, `StationRetired(station, sector, type, name, owner, payout, activity, forGood, forType)`, and in the same step the lifecycle starts the site of the wanted type through the normal path (escrow, material, construction ship). **Decision:** the rule reviews every unprotected station, not only lifecycle builds — the start world marks all its stations as protected (`essential`), so in the default world it only ever touches built stations; a custom world can leave start stations unprotected on purpose. **Subsidies:** `SubsidySystem.Eligible` no longer supports a station that is only eligible because it is critical when it is `Useless` (long idle, products not scarce).

**Construction ships come back (`ConstructionRules.CommissionBuilder`, rule `CommissionBuilders`).** A blocked site without an assigned or reachable builder checks every `CommissionInterval` (50) ticks: the treasury of the site's faction buys a model with job `build` through the shared `ShipPurchase` rule (kit + modules from a reachable yard's stock, price to the yard, + `NewShipStartCredits`), keeping `SubsidyReserve`; the ship belongs to the faction actor; `TreasuryUpkeep` "builder" and `BuilderCommissioned`. The emergency build stays the fallback.

**Price floors — cause (measured with the new `CargoDumped` event: units delivered above the station's acceptance).** Not the maintenance targets and not a titanium oversupply: Phase 14's last resort ("unpaid dumping of a rest when no other buyer exists") put whole loads above the acceptance — default seed 20 000 ticks: power plants 392 + 202 hydrogen, yard 20 titanium (target 10), electronics/wafers into small entries; 200 000 ticks: up to 2 100 units per entry. Several traders planned the same buyer (only build sites reserved incoming deliveries), the second one arrived at a full station. The remaining 0.35× goods (ice, titanium ore, energy) sat at the full-stock price 1/`OverstockFactor` = 0.33×. **Changes:** (1) `TraderSystem` sells on arrival exactly by the shared sale rule (`TradeActions.SaleCaps/SaleUnits`: whole units up to the acceptance, everything within the rest tolerance; build sites the missing amount); a rest goes to the next buyer (`BestBuyerFor`), otherwise it **stays aboard**: the idle trader retries every think (`SellStranded`, now also for the second good aboard and within automation limits; its order has no buy station, `TradeOrder.Buy` = 0) and an AI trader jettisons it in space only after `StrandedCargoTicks` (3 000; `Ship.StrandedSince`, saved additively and hashed; `CargoDiscarded`); player ships keep it. (2) `ReserveIncoming` (default true): planning subtracts what other traders already bring to any buyer (cargo of those selling, capacity of those buying; before: build sites only), buying subtracts the cargo already on its way. (3) `BestTrade` plans only what the ship's wallet can pay also without automation limits — before, a broke trader drove to the seller, could not buy, re-planned at once and was never abandoned (0-credit "zombie" traders: 3–5 per 20 000-tick run in Phase 18; with the first draft of (1) without (2)/(3) half the trade fleet went broke on seeds 8/9). (4) Default world `overstockFactor` 2.5 (code default 3): the full-stock price is 0.4× instead of 0.33×; the export sinks keep 3 through the new `exportOverstockFactor` (0 = `OverstockFactor`). Miners already jettisoned unsellable leftovers in space and never dumped.

**Jettison with fleet orders (`CargoJettison.Issue(u, ship, good)`).** Trade command in transit to the buyer: allowed, the ship arrives without the good, sells nothing, the command continues with the next round. Mining command: allowed, mining continues. Squadron leader: allowed, the movement command is unaffected. **Construction ship (AI or own with `BuildCommand`) assigned to a site: material that site still needs is refused** with the reason "Baumaterial für … aus dem Treuhandgeld der Baustelle bezahlt — erst abliefern oder den Bauauftrag beenden" (it was paid from the escrow and the builder would fetch it again on the escrow's money); goods the site does not need are free. The `drop:` offer of the trade service carries amount 0 and the reason as note; `TradeScreen` shows no "Abwerfen" button for it; the asset detail shows the reason as message.

**Confirm button (`Ui/Kit/KitConfirm`, new `Ui/Kit/KitConfirmTicker`).** `Wire` adds a `KitConfirmTicker` node to the button that checks the armed state every frame and relabels when the 3-second window expires ("Wirklich?" → "Abwerfen") — no screen has to handle it; the state still lives in the screen's `KitConfirm`. `KitConfirm.Now` (clock, default `Time.GetTicksMsec`) lets the selftest advance time; `KitConfirm.Find(root, key)` finds a wired button (meta key). Selftest: armed button reads "Wirklich?", after the window "Abwerfen", a single tap does not jettison.

**Balance tool.** New lines "Stilllegungen/Umwidmungen" (tick, station, activity → type for good, salvage), "Plätze voll: x % der Messzeit; anhaltende Knappheit ohne freien Platz: good (type) y %", "Auslastung je Stationstyp (lief/Zutaten fehlten)" plus every built station (marked "stillgelegt"), "Über die Annahme abgeladen" (`CargoDumped`, only rest-tolerance fractions remain), "Im All abgeworfen (KI-Händler)" (`CargoDiscarded`); the construction line counts builders bought by factions. `--retire on|off`.

**Persistence.** Additive only, save stays v17: `StationSave.BuiltTick/Activity/Starved`, `SaveGame.LastStationRetireTick/ScarceSince`, `ShipSave.StrandedSince`, new rules in the saved `EconomyRules`. `UniverseHash` mixes all of them.

**Tests.** New `StationReviewTests` (20): utilization (no inputs → activity falls and `Starved` rises, running again → back up, `BuiltTick`), retirement (station retired for the persistent scarcity, water-plant site started, salvage = event), exceptions as a theory (protected, grace, active, product scarce, scarcity too short, free slot → normal build, cooldown, critical, rule off), subsidy for a useless critical station refused, **long run** (constructed: the start water plant removed and a redundant titanium works added with all slots taken → after grace and window the titanium works is retired, a water plant is built through site and builder, water is no longer scarce, no further retirement within the cooldown, the new plant is never retired), builder commissioning (faction pays, ship takes the site; no purchase below the reserve), save roundtrip bit-identical, 20 000 ticks without a delivery above the acceptance (rest tolerance only), a rest without buyer stays aboard and is jettisoned only after the window, jettison with trade command in transit (no delivery, command continues and trades again), with build command (escrow material refused incl. the `drop:` offer and `Execute`, other goods free, the site still gets its material), miner and squadron leader (no crash, no ghost orders). Adjusted on purpose: `FleetCommandTests` automatic trade (rest sales have buy station 0), `EconomyFixTests` long run (trade counted in capacity units ≥ 12 instead of ≥ 12 ships — the fleet now also buys a Behemoth; trade orders with buy station 0 allowed while selling) and shelf/export acceptance (export factor), `ConstructionTests` trader-delivery and emergency-build tests switch builder commissioning off (they test building without a builder), `CapabilityTests` gives the control trader money (planning needs cash), `SecurityTests.Piraten_Neuanfang…` accepts a running second restart when the first rebuilt raider is lost again before tick 12 000 (random path shifted by the trader changes). 549 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build, default world; Phase 18 → Phase 19).** Every run "Preise im Zielbereich" (exit 0): default 20 000/60 000, seeds 1–20 at 20 000, seeds 1–6 at 60 000, default and seeds 1–2 at 200 000 (`--every 50000`), `--fuel on --research on`. **Lower cap:** HINWEIS lines at the lower cap 0 in every run (Phase 18: up to 7 per long run, e.g. yard titanium 95 % at 0.26×, Sektor-HQ Delta electronics 100 % at 0.25×); HINWEIS lines in total 0–1 per run (only a newly built electronics fab with empty cash 26–33 % of the time on 5 of 33 runs). Lowest verdict value 0.35× → 0.41–0.51× (ice, titanium ore, energy). Highest 1.06–1.46× (copper on seed 10). Traders/miners: capacity 13.4–14.5 / 11.9–12.5 at the end (max ≤ 14.5/12.5). Over-acceptance deliveries: hydrogen 392 units → 0 (only rest-tolerance fractions remain, ≤ 21 units per entry over 200 000 ticks); jettisoned in space by AI traders 3–16 times per 20 000 ticks, 66–98 per 200 000. **Retirements:** 0 at 20 000; at 60 000 seeds 1/4/5 one each, at 200 000 default and seed 1 one each — always an ore-starved built steelworks (activity 8–9 %, 64–87 % of the time without inputs) converted into a copper smelter for persistent copper scarcity (salvage 6 600–7 800 Cr), never more than one per run; slots full 100 % of the time from ≈ 30 000 ticks; persistent unanswered scarcity 0–27 % of the time (copper). Construction ships: 2 at 20 000 (start fleet), 1–2 at 60 000, 0 at 200 000 — the default 200 000 run bought one through `CommissionBuilder` for its conversion (5 sites, 0 emergency builds). Exports: 20 000 seeds 1–20 food 7 460–8 057, CG 1 215–1 534, luxury 312–408 (Phase 18: 6 726–8 674 / 1 147–1 505 / 280–433); 60 000 default CG 4 178 → 3 614, luxury 1 187 → 1 140, seeds 1–6 CG 3 673–4 025, luxury 1 107–1 207; 200 000 CG 15 413 → 12 353, luxury 4 531 → 4 108, food ≈ 85 900 unchanged. Money 0.80 M at 60 000/200 000 (treasury share 27–29 %). Time per tick (single runs): 60 000 0.105 → 0.124 ms, 20 000 0.195 → 0.220 ms.

**Known weaknesses.** In the default content the review rarely has anything to do (1 conversion per long run): stations go idle mostly because their raw ore is short, and the only persistent unanswered scarcity is copper, whose new smelter is itself 70–80 % ore-starved — the conversion moves ore demand rather than creating supply. The utilization measure does not distinguish a station whose fast recipe simply meets all demand at 5 % from a useless one; the output-ratio check (products not scarce) and the "wanted type differs" rule keep that from retiring needed capacity, and start stations are protected anyway. Consumer-goods/luxury exports fell ≈ 13 % at 60 000 and ≈ 20 % at 200 000: the goods factory now pauses for lack of open demand (the sinks' value-based targets and waiting batches limit intake, no more deliveries above it); `exportOverstockFactor` 3 recovered part of it. AI traders now lose goods they cannot sell (jettisoned after 3 000 ticks) instead of being paid for dumped loads; trader cash is lower (≈ 60–90 k vs ≈ 150 k at 200 000) while station cash is higher. Builders bought by `CommissionBuilder` are again abandoned when idle and broke (only re-bought when a site needs one). Electronics fabs built by the lifecycle often run with empty cash for a while (HINWEIS on 5 of 33 runs).

## Phase 20 (English summary): weaknesses from the long runs — supply panic behind closed gates, fleet per map part, police hysteresis, fleet throughput, multirole flapping, faction construction ships

Ten long runs (72–144 game hours, `/home/gerd/space-adventure-langlaeufe`) showed eight weaknesses. Causes were measured first (balance tool + a throwaway probe that logs removals by role/reason, per-ship trade histories, job switches with scores, police need over time).

**Measured causes.**
- **Water / ships hang on one sector (runs H, G).** A part of the map cut off by a closed gate keeps its consumers but loses the last producer (Alpha: the only water plant; Delta: the only yard) and nothing reacts: the lifecycle measures scarcity world-wide, and the Phase 18 rule `FleetGoodWithProducer` treated kits/modules as covered as long as a yard existed *anywhere*.
- **Trade fleet collapse with Gamma closed (run F, 3.7/14).** Not capacity counting: traders spent their whole wallet on one load; when the buyer's acceptance shrank before arrival (Phase 15: inputs of paused recipes only up to target — mostly hydrogen at the power plants) the rest stayed aboard, the ship had 0 credits and `FleetSystem.Abandon` removed it as bankrupt after 600 idle ticks *with the cargo aboard* (the 3000-tick stranded-cargo window never applied). Replacements need a trader with ≥ 4000 Cr as buyer; once every trader of a part was poor, nobody ever bought again.
- **Police buy/decommission cycle (344/344 in 144 h).** The desired count ⌈Σ(1 + 0.5 × threat)⌉ jumps from 2 to 3 with every single raid (threat +1); a third ship was bought, and decommissioned 6000 ticks *after the purchase* (the timer measured time since the last change, not how long the surplus lasted) once the threat had decayed. Need histogram: 2.00–2.25 most of the time, short spikes.
- **Fleet throughput (417 builds in 144 h).** Almost all removals were "bankrupt" traders with a stranded load (see F); idle retirement did not occur in open runs, but under closed gates poor traders (240–520 Cr) were retired as "idle" although no replacement could come.
- **Multirole flapping (3284/5459 switches).** A poor Tinker switched trade → mining (trade score tiny for lack of money), delivered one load, and right after the delivery the mining score was 0 (buyer momentarily full), so after `JobMinTicks` it switched back — a zero score at an instant was enough.
- **Never a construction ship at the end.** Builders lived from the 10 % fee, idled between waves, were abandoned as bankrupt and re-bought per site.

**Changes.**
- `Simulation/MapParts.cs`: physical map partition (BFS over open/restricted gates; closed/destroyed separate), part key = smallest sector id, `CutGate`; cached per tick/access version/gate states. Political locks do not split. Works for any number of sectors.
- `Simulation/SupplyPanic.cs` — `SupplyPanicSystem` (Lifecycle module, interval 20, before the lifecycle): only while the map is split. Per part: goods needed there (recipe inputs of its stations weighted by target × base price, export goods of its sinks, kits and modules of AI-buyable/police models if the part has an economy) without a producer in the part (live sites of a producing type count) for `PanicDelayTicks` (1000) → event `SupplyPanicRaised(Part, Good, Gate, TypeKey?)` (state `Universe.PanicSince`, saved/hashed, cleared when resolved or the map reconnects). Build: `SupplyPanic.PlanFor` = cheapest buildable producer whose recipe can work in the part (each input produced or minable there, else the producer of that input first, chain depth 3; null = not producible, e.g. platinum without a rare-ore belt). One panic site per part at a time, at most `PanicReserveStations` (2) panic stations per part — an emergency reserve beyond `MaxStations`/`MaxStationsPerSector`; investor from the part (weighted draw over treasuries of factions owning sectors there, stations and ships in the part); `BuildSite.Panic` → `Station.Panic` (saved, hashed); event `SupplyPanicBuild`. Sites complete through the normal path (builders, suppliers in the part, emergency build as last resort). `StationReview.TryRetireSurplus`: once the map is connected again and the economic stations exceed `MaxStations`, a useless, non-critical station (panic stations first) is retired without replacement (cooldown as Phase 19) — no oscillation. `FleetGoodWithProducer` now requires a finished producer in every part that holds the good. View: toast with `SupplyPanic.Describe` (one per tick); selftest stage 48.
- `Simulation/FleetCapacity.cs`: while split, a job's limit per part = limit × the part's share of finished economic market stations, used units counted per part (`FleetCapacity.For`); used by `FleetSystem` (expansion, model choice, quality fit), `JobAssignmentSystem` (`HasRoom`, crowding) and `UpgradeSystem`. Unsplit: identical to before.
- `FleetSystem`: bankrupt only with an empty hold (`BankruptNeedsEmptyHold`); faction construction ships are never abandoned; idle retirement only when the job is full in the ship's part (`Overstaffed`) and it blocks purchases for that job and part for `ExpandAfterRetireTicks` (6000, `Universe.IdleRetiredTick`, saved/hashed); `CanGrow` also refuses while a ship of the job in the part with at least the start capital has been idle `ExpandIdleTicks` (1000). New `BuyFromTreasury`: in a part whose used capacity fell below `FleetTreasuryFloorShare` (0.6) of its limit and where no ship of the job can pay, the treasury of the faction owning most sectors there buys a ship through the shared model choice (`ChooseModel` overload by location/credits) as a faction-owned operation (`TreasuryUpkeep` "fleet"). The build job is no longer expanded from builder wallets (builders are a faction asset).
- `JobAssignmentSystem.IdleLongEnough`: a job scoring 0 is left only after `JobIdleSwitchTicks` (1500) idle; positive scores keep the hysteresis.
- `SecuritySystem`: smoothed need `Actor.PoliceNeed` (EMA, `PoliceNeedWindowTicks` 20000) with hysteresis (`Thresholds`: buy below ⌈need − 0.5⌉, sell above ⌈need + 0.9 − 1⌉), decommission only after the surplus lasted `PoliceDecommissionTicks` continuously (`Actor.PoliceSurplusSince`); both saved/hashed. `PoliceUpkeepPerTick` 0.8 → 0.4 (code default): since the Phase 15 fiscal brake only part of the taxes reaches the treasuries, and the upkeep of the base strength alone was ≈ 12 % of treasury income (Phase 11 was calibrated to 6.7 %).
- `Simulation/FactionBuilderSystem.cs` (Construction module, interval 100): faction-owned builders pay `BuilderUpkeepPerTick` (0.2) from the treasury (`TreasuryUpkeep` "builder"), each faction keeps `MinBuildersPerFaction` (1) via `ConstructionRules.CommissionBuilder(u, faction, near, site)`, spare builders idle for `IdleRetireTicks` are retired (credits to the treasury).
- `TraderSystem`: optional `TraderCashReserve` (default 0; a fixed reserve of 300 was measured and rejected — it produced poor traders that could not trade at all and were retired).
- Balance tool: removals per job and reason ("Abgänge je Aufgabe"), panic events/builds/finished panic stations, per-part fleet capacity when the map is split, surplus retirements.
- Persistence additive (save stays v17): `SaveGame.PanicSince` ([part, good, tick]), `IdleRetiredTick` ("job@part"), `ActorSave.PoliceNeed/PoliceSurplusSince`, `StationSave.Panic`, `BuildSiteSave.Panic`; all in `UniverseHash`.

**Tests.** New `Phase20Tests` (17): map partition, per-part capacity, Alpha closed → water-plant panic is built and finished in the rest part, farms run again, reserve respected; Delta closed → platinum panic without build, kit rule only with a reachable yard, panic ends when the gate opens; short closure triggers nothing; surplus retirement after reconnection (not while split, cooldown); bankrupt only without cargo; idle retirement only when overstaffed + purchase block; no purchase while a capable ship idles; treasury buys a trader for a part without buyers; idle rule for job switches and ≤ 60 switches in 40000 ticks with Delta closed; police thresholds and ≤ 6 police changes in 60000 ticks; builders stay (upkeep, never abandoned) and the minimum stock is re-bought; save roundtrip with panic/purchase block/police need bit-identical. Adjusted on purpose: `MultiroleTests.Tinker_WechseltZumHandel…` shortens `JobIdleSwitchTicks` like `JobMinTicks`; `SecurityTests.Polizei_Abbau_NachDauerhafterUeberzahl` expects the decommission after the continuous surplus window (threat factor 0 so raids do not interrupt it). 566 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build; before = the long runs / HEAD at 400000 ticks).** Open runs all "Preise im Zielbereich" (exit 0): default 20000 (trade 14.4/14, mine 12.5/12) and 60000 (14.1/11.7), seeds 1–8 at 20000 (trade 13.9–14.4, mine 11.7–12.5), seeds 1–3 at 60000, 1036800 ticks (14.4/12.3). Gate runs (400000, gate closed at 50000) exit 2 only for the map-split warning, prices in range.
- Police: 1036800 open 176 bought/175 decommissioned, 17.4 % of treasury income → 2/2, 6.7 %; gate runs before 9–10/9–10 and 13.8–15.1 % → 1–3/0–3 and 7.0–8.3 %; 20000/60000 runs 0–2 purchases, 4–8 %.
- Fleet throughput (1036800 open): 280 builds → 56; removals: bankrupt traders 22, raid victims 19 (trader 9, miner 10), no idle retirements.
- Multirole switches: 191 (open, 1M) → 27; Gamma closed 400k 174 → 8, Delta closed 1054 → 22, Alpha closed 1858 → 19.
- Construction ships at the end: 0 in every long run → 2 in every run; emergency builds Delta closed 44 % → 13 %, Gamma 10 % → 14 %, Alpha 17 % → 25 % (2 of 8: sites in the isolated Alpha part without a reachable builder).
- Alpha closed (H): the rest builds a water plant (panic), farms 70 % running / 0 % starved, colony fully supplied (6/94 % → 100/0 %), end-good trips food 197697 → 224326 units, consumer goods 5161 → 39954, luxury 1740 → 13583; rest-part trade 10.5/10.1 (before 1.6/14 world-wide). Food export over 400000 ticks 170529 ≈ the open-run rate (H at 1M: 43166 = a tenth).
- Gamma closed (F): rest-part trade 9.6/10.0, mining 9.0/8.6; panic builds a luxury workshop and a weapons works in the rest and a water plant in the isolated Gamma part (its farm and life-support works).
- Delta closed (G): 24 panics; kits, platinum and fuel cells are not producible in the rest (platinum ore exists only in Delta's rare belt; every kit tier needs fuel cells = platinum), so the rest gets no yard: rest-part trade 3.0/9.8 (before 2.0/14); the rest builds a titanium works and a plating works instead, Delta a copper smelter and an electronics fab (ore-starved there: no silicon).
- 10 h closure (Alpha, ticks 50000–194000): panic stations built, after reopening two idle panic stations retired as surplus (ticks 194000, 209000), no new panic; trade 14.4/14, mine 12.0/12 at the end.
- ms/tick 1036800 open 0.071 → 0.068.

**Known weaknesses.** Delta closed: no replacement yard is possible in the rest part (raw-material geography), so its trade fleet stays small; the panic reports "nicht herstellbar". Traders still go bankrupt in thin-margin markets (winner's curse: the best-looking margin often reverts before arrival — 20 in the Delta run, 56 in the 10 h closure run, mostly in the cut-off phase). The emergency reserve can be spent on module works or a third panic station while a more important good waits (one panic site per part at a time, weight order). The cut-off single sectors (Alpha alone, Gamma alone) lose their fleets: they have no yard and kits are not producible there. Panic toasts are per tick and show the heaviest good only. Police share 6.7–8.3 % relies on the halved upkeep.

## Phase 21 (English summary): the default world grows from 4 to 11 sectors

**Map.** Everything of the 4-sector world is unchanged (sectors 1–4, stations 1–40, gates 1–6, goods, field positions); everything new is appended (sectors 5–11, gates 7–28, stations 41–75). Grid (`map`, +y = south): row −1 Borealis [0,−1] · Vesta [1,−1] · Hephaistos [2,−1]; row 0 Alpha · Beta · Gamma · Aurelia [3,0]; row 1 Nebelmark [0,1] · Delta [1,1] · Kalypso [2,1]; row 2 Abgrund [1,2] — no crossing lines. Gate pairs (first id ↔ second id): 1/2 Alpha–Beta (main), 3/4 Beta–Gamma (main), 5/6 Beta–Delta (main), 7/8 Alpha–Borealis (main), 9/10 Borealis–Vesta, 11/12 Vesta–Beta (main), 13/14 Vesta–Hephaistos (main), 15/16 Hephaistos–Gamma, 17/18 Gamma–Aurelia (main, dead end), 19/20 Alpha–Nebelmark (main), 21/22 Nebelmark–Delta, 23/24 Delta–Kalypso (main), 25/26 Kalypso–Gamma, 27/28 Delta–Abgrund (main, dead end). Four rings (Alpha–Borealis–Vesta–Beta, Beta–Vesta–Hephaistos–Gamma, Alpha–Nebelmark–Delta–Beta, Beta–Delta–Kalypso–Gamma): only closing 17 or 27 splits the map (and only the dead end falls off); the main routes form a spanning tree, so the Phase 10 main-route cap keeps every sector reachable for non-enemies. In-sector gate positions follow the map direction (east gate at +x, south at +y).

**Sectors and profiles (owner; belts; stations).**
| Id | Sector | Owner | Profile | Belts | New stations (ids) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | Borealis | Union | mining (ice, metal) | ice, metal | water plant 41, electrolysis 42, steelworks 43, solar 44, sector HQ 45 |
| 6 | Vesta | Union | industry + yard | silicate | copper smelter 46, wafer fab 47, electronics fab 48, plating works 49, **Werft Vesta 50**, solar 51, systems works 52, sector HQ 53, police station 54 |
| 7 | Hephaistos | Montanbund | mining/heavy industry + yard | rare, metal | titanium works 55, platinum refinery 56, fuel cell fab 57, solar 58, **Werft Hephaistos 59**, drive works 60, sector HQ 61 |
| 8 | Aurelia | Montanbund | planet "Elysion", agrar, dead end | ice | farm 62, water plant 63, solar 64, export sink "Handelsposten Aurelia" 65 (`trade_post`), Montanbund HQ 66, research centre 75 (only with research on) |
| 9 | Nebelmark | none (herrenlos) | border, mining only | rare, ice | — |
| 10 | Kalypso | Guild | planet "Tethys", colony | silicate | colony "Kolonie Tethys" 67, export sink "Handelsstation Kalypso" 68, farm 69, life support 70, goods factory 71, solar 72, sector HQ 73 |
| 11 | Abgrund | none (herrenlos) | border, dead end | metal | hideout of the second pirate group 74 |
Every ore is in ≥ 2 sectors that no single gate cuts off together: metal (iron/copper/titanium) Alpha, Delta, Borealis, Hephaistos, Abgrund; ice Alpha, Gamma, Borealis, Aurelia, Nebelmark; silicate (silicon, iron) Beta, Vesta, Kalypso; rare (titanium, platinum) Delta, Hephaistos, Nebelmark. No sector has more than two belt classes. Three yards (Delta, Vesta, Hephaistos — none behind a dead-end gate, so Phase 20's "Delta closed → no yard" cannot happen any more), two drive works, two systems works, four farms, three water plants, three electrolyses, two copper smelters/wafer fabs/electronics fabs/plating works/fuel cell fabs/titanium works/platinum refineries, two goods factories, two colonies, four planets with export sinks (Korvan, Thalassa, Elysion, Tethys). Placement keeps the rules of the earlier phases (stations > 101 from belt centres and > 50 from gates/stations, gates > 86 from belt centres; every belt reaches its target count). Content was moved where the planner failed: `AvoidanceTests` found two pairs whose planned leg grazed a rock (Alpha north gate at (100,−140); Borealis metal belt at the water-plant side) — the gate moved to (150,−110), Borealis has ice east/metal west.

**Factions and actors.** Third faction `Montanbund` (id 3, guild; aggression 0.35, greed 0.55, cooperation 0.6, lawfulness 0.6; relations 0.2 to both others, Union/Guild now name it with 0.2) with territory Hephaistos + Aurelia, HQ, two police ships at its HQ, one construction ship. Sectors may now be **herrenlos**: `sectors[].owner` 0 (or omitted) = no faction, no territory (`WorldBuilder`; an unknown non-zero owner still throws); ownership can only arise later from station influence. Second outlaw actor `blades` "Schwarze Klingen" (treasury 4000, two corsairs, hideout 74 in Abgrund). Start fleet: 17 traders, 13 miners (free), 3 builders, 6 police, 5 raiders.

**Rules (default world).** `maxTraders` 14 → 26, `maxMiners` 12 → 22, `maxStations` 37 → 66 (64 with fuel off; 60 economic stations at start, 4 free slots as before + room for 2 more), `maxBuilders` 4 → 6, `maxActiveSites` 2 → 3, `policeMaxPerFaction` 4 → 6. Fuel and research stay off. Fiscal brake and treasury rules unchanged (they scale with money and factions).

**Code.** `WorldBuilder`: herrenlose sectors. `MapParts.IfClosed(u, gate)` / `SplitsWhenClosed` (pure: the partition if that gate pair were closed). `StationLifecycleSystem.MissingRawInputs` + rule `BuildNearOre` (default true): a lifecycle build prefers sectors where its ore inputs are mined (before: the emptiest sector, which in a big world put steelworks far from any metal belt). `TradeActions.BestBuyerFor(..., allowed)`: a fleet ship with automation limits carries a rest only to buyers inside its radius (latent Phase 19 bug — the rest could leave the radius; it showed in the bigger world). Migration: `MigrateV13ToV14` adds only outlaw actors whose hideout sector exists in the save (`AddDefaultStations(..., ownerKey)`), so the classic-based migration tests stay hash-equal. UI: universe map with separate x/y scales (4 × 4 grid in 700 × 464), halo/node size from the spacing, names without "Sektor ", short security sub-line; star map (`MapOverlay`) fits the window instead of fixed 220 px, grey for herrenlos sectors; faction name "herrenlos"; taxi area as a scroll list of every destination with a waiting own ship or a rental (the rest summed in one line — 69 hangar stations); fleet target lists sorted by jumps. Balance tool: a "Tore:" line at the start (id pair, from → to, state, main route) and `--gatelist on` (per gate pair whether closing it splits the map and which part falls off, then exit).

**Tests.** The former default world is kept as embedded test resource `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/Content/classic-world.json` (`TestWorlds.Classic()`, `ClassicJson()`, `ClassicDef()`). Switched to it on purpose (they need the small controlled star map or are calibrated on it): `Phase20Tests` (Alpha/Gamma/Delta cuts), `SecurityTests` (single pirate group, police numbers), `PoliticsTests` (government station list, contested-sector mechanics, gate routes), `StationReviewTests` (slot counts, constructed retirement), the migration tests (`Migration_V3ToV4`, `V4ToV5`, `V5ToV6`, `AlterV5Save`, `V8ToV9`, `V9ToV10`, `V11ToV12`, realistic `V15`; the chain injects default content by sector id), `MultiroleTests.Kapazitaet_ZaehltEinheitenStattSchiffe` (start fleet 9/6, limit 14) and `Tinker_WechseltZumHandel…` (its first rest load finds a full yard shelf in the big world and stays aboard for the 3000-tick stranded window, longer than the test window). Generalised instead: factory/placement test (each factory type at least once, all spaced), sector/gate counts, planets (four), `maxStations` from the world file, relation entries = n × (n − 1), fuel/yard/kit tests zero the other yards' stock, demand test saturates every reactor shelf, research test completes the project for every faction and expects the third lab, taxi "blocked route" closes all of Delta's gates, emergency-build test switches builder commissioning off like its siblings, two float checks use a 0.01 tolerance instead of 2-decimal rounding. New `Phase21Tests` (10): ids unchanged vs classic, third faction/herrenlos sectors/second pirates/friendly relations, unknown owner still throws, ores in several sectors and no single gate cuts one off from the main part, every recipe good produced and key chains in ≥ 2 sectors, 2–3 yards outside dead ends, four planet sinks, second colony, ≥ 5 module works, placement and belts, only gates 17/27 split (dead end falls off) + main-route spanning tree + rings, ring gate closed → longer route without split, 20000-tick run (prices 0.3–3×, capacity, no ghost orders, all factions alive with police and money, no locks, map connected, every sink exports), determinism + save/load bit-identical. `AvoidanceTests` collects all failing pairs (sector and endpoints in the message). 576 tests green. Selftest: stage 48 (supply panic) cuts Alpha by closing all three of its gates; new stage 49 checks 11 sectors, route hops = `FindRoute` for every sector, two herrenlose sectors, the star map, and the taxi list (one row per rental stand, the rest summarised). `--ui-shots … --ui-shots-phase21` adds 58–62 (universe faction/ships/security layer, star map, taxi list); looked at on a display.

**Balance (fresh Release build, default world).** All open runs "Preise im Zielbereich", exit 0: default 20000 (trade 26.4/26, mine 22.4/22; money 1.36 M, treasuries 24 %; loss 0.65 %; police Union 5, Guild 4, Montanbund 2, 6.3 % of treasury income; export food/CG/luxury 19 299/2 928/711; 0.29 ms/tick) and 60000 (26.4/22.2, 1.49 M, 27 %, loss 0.71 %, 7.5 %, export 62 913/9 188/2 446, 0.197 ms/tick); seeds 1–8 at 20000 (trade 26.0–26.4, mine 21.0–22.4, loss 0.45–0.82 %, 0–3 HINWEIS lines, 0.28–0.32 ms/tick); seeds 1–3 at 60000 (0.18–0.19 ms/tick, loss 0.37–0.66 %, 0–1 HINWEIS); 400000 open (25.9/21.8, 1.52 M, 27 %, loss 0.47 %, police 8.4 %, export 437 886/63 258/17 271, 0 HINWEIS, 0.159 ms/tick); `--fuel on --research on` 20000 green (0.34 ms/tick). No lock ever (relations 0.12–0.25), map connected, 0 emergency fuel buys. Gate runs (300000 ticks, closed at 50000): (a) dead end 17 → exit 2 only for the split warning, prices green, Aurelia panics for kits/CG/luxury/modules "nicht herstellbar" (no site), rest-part trade 24.8/24.4, Aurelia 1.0/1.6; (b) ring gate 3 → exit 0, nothing splits, no panic; (c) gate 5 to Werft Delta → exit 0 (Delta stays reachable through Nebelmark/Kalypso); (d) gate 17 closed 50000–194000 → exit 2 (split while closed), green, reconnects cleanly. Hot spots (60000): TraderSystem 32 %, MarketSystem 26 %, MinerSystem 13 %, JobAssignment 9 %, Production 6 % — no optimisation needed (target ≤ 0.35 ms/tick at 60000; Phase 20 0.07–0.12).

**Known weaknesses.** Government upkeep takes 60–70 % of the taxes reaching the treasuries (seven sector HQs, two HQs, a police station; Phase 20: ≈ 35–40 %) — treasuries stay at 24–29 % of the money, so it is affordable, but little is left for station investments. Steel stays the tightest chain at 20000 ticks: early lifecycle steelworks (often in Kalypso, whose silicate belt yields some iron) run ore-starved and appear as HINWEIS lines (upper cap, empty cash) in 20000-tick runs; they disappear by 60000. With a dead end closed, the ships caught there count against its small share, so the world-wide trade capacity briefly exceeds the limit (max 29.4/26 in the gate runs). Montanbund keeps 2 police ships (its smoothed need stays below the buy threshold). Raid loss rate 0.35–0.93 % (target < 1 %; seed 1 at 20000 the highest). The universe map's sub-lines are 11 px and tight on a phone; the map is a fixed 4 × 4 schema (no zoom/scroll).

## Phase 22 (English summary): ships and stations disappear only for three reasons; factions stay viable

The user's rule: an AI ship or station may leave the world only when (a) **destroyed**, (b) **bankrupt** or (c) **long unprofitable**; no faction decision may leave a faction unable to act.

**Inventory of removal paths (all go through `Simulation/Departures.RemoveShip/RemoveStation`, which publishes `ShipDeparted`/`StationDeparted(…, DepartureReason, Detail)` before `Universe.RemoveShip/RemoveStation`; the removal reason string is now `DepartureNames.Key`: destroyed/bankrupt/unprofitable/sold/returned).**
| Path | Reason | Change |
|---|---|---|
| raid victim (`PirateSystem.ResolveRaid`) | a, detail `raid` | key "raid" → "destroyed" |
| raider lost in a raid / intercepted | a, `raid` / `intercept` | dampened (below) |
| broke AI ship (`FleetSystem.Abandon`) | b, `broke` | rule extracted to `Departures.ShipBankrupt` (credits < `ShipBankruptCredits` 50, no cargo, no order/transit, idle ≥ `ShipBankruptTicks`) |
| idle retirement of traders/miners (Phase 15/20) | c, `unprofitable` | replaced by the profitability measurement (`Departures.ShipUnprofitable`) |
| police decommissioning | c, `decommissioned` | "long without use and surplus"; never below `PoliceMinPerFaction` (also with zero need: `Thresholds` returned (0,0) before) |
| spare faction builder | c, `spare_builder` | unchanged rule, never below `MinBuildersPerFaction` |
| bankrupt station (lifecycle) | b, `broke` | additionally never the faction's last own supply (`FactionViability.KeepsOwnSupply`) |
| station review retirement/conversion (Phase 19) | c, `retired` | candidate = long unprofitable instead of activity < 10 %; independent of full slots |
| surplus retirement after reconnection (Phase 20) | c, `surplus` | same candidate rule |
| build site expired / abandoned site cleared | c, `site_expired` / `site_cleared` | (a project without progress over a long window) |
| player sells a ship / returns a rental | `Sold` / `Returned` | not decay paths; player property is never touched by decay rules |
Actor decline (`ActorSystem`) removes empty actors, not ships or stations.

**(c) Profitability (`Model/Profit.cs` `ProfitMeter`, `Simulation/Profitability.cs`, `ProfitabilitySystem` in the new `ViabilityModule` after Actors, interval 50).** Measured for every economy-AI ship (`IsEconomyAi`) and every finished station: worth = credits + cargo/stock at base price; `Rate` = EMA of Δworth per tick (time constant `ProfitWindowTicks` 20000); `LossTicks` counts ticks with `Rate < 0` and resets only above `ProfitableAbove` (0.02 Cr/tick, hysteresis); `Unprofitable` = grace `ProfitGraceTicks` (20000) since `Since` (first measurement = purchase/build) and `LossTicks ≥ UnprofitableTicks` (20000). Not business results, neutralised through `Profitability.Transfer` on the meter's reference: ship investments (fleet purchase, station funding via `Investor.Invested`, NPC upgrades), escrow refunds, wealth tax (ships and stations: "revenue − purchase − upkeep"), a station's type income and subsidies, capital moves within a firm. The maintenance refund counts (it offsets the material bought). **The clock rests** (`Profitability.Resting`: reference follows, EMA and counter frozen) while a physical gate closure separates the sector from the main part of the map (`MainPart` = part with the most economic stations) — a closure alone never makes anything unprofitable. Service units (police, builders, government/research/export/final-consumer stations, hideouts) are measured for display only; for them (c) means "long without use and surplus" (police/builder rules above). Saved additively (`ProfitMeterSave` on `ShipSave`/`StationSave`), hashed.

**Stations (c).** `StationReview.Useless` = reviewable (unprotected, recipes, not official/hideout/export sink/final consumer) + grace since `BuiltTick` + `Profitability.Unprofitable` + no product scarce; `MayRetire` = not critical (last reachable producer, also per map part), not the faction's last own supply (`KeepsOwnSupply`: last station of its type in the faction while another station of the faction consumes one of its products), no player storage/docked player ship. `TryRetire` retires the worst-rate candidate (cooldown `RetireCooldownTicks`); a persistent scarcity with full slots still converts the slot (Phase 19). New rule `BuildSkipStarvedShare` (0.5): the lifecycle and the conversion never build another station of a type whose existing stations starve for inputs more than half of the time — the first draft retired 57 ore-starved copper smelters in 1 M ticks and rebuilt each.

**(b) Cause of the trader bankruptcies (run M, seed 3, alternating gate closures: 215 bankrupt traders, 332 builds in 144 h).** A probe logged the last trades of every bankrupt trader (first half of M: 98 bankruptcies): not "the whole wallet on one load" but a slow death spiral — after a capital shock (37 after raids, 46 after jettisoning a whole hydrogen load bought for a power plant that paused its recipe before arrival, 551 hydrogen jettisons in M) the ship could only afford 1–2 units of energy per round (≈ 1 Cr margin) while paying `ShipUpkeepPerTick` × trip time, losing a little per round until broke. Rule fixes: (1) `TripCountsUpkeep` — `TraderSystem.BestTrade` subtracts upkeep × trip time (rounds that do not cover their upkeep are not taken); (2) `HonourPlannedDelivery` — a trader that bought for a station delivers up to the normal acceptance (target × `OverstockFactor`) even if the consuming recipe paused meanwhile (Phase 15 accepts inputs of paused recipes only up to target); `CargoDumped` is measured against that acceptance; (3) `FirmCapitalBalance` (`FirmCapitalSystem`, Viability module, interval 50) — within a promoted actor the richest ship (≥ `FleetExpandCredits`) tops up an empty sibling below half of `NewShipStartCredits` to `NewShipStartCredits` (no new money, `FirmCapitalMoved`).

**(a) Pirate cycle.** Raiders were destroyed and rebuilt ≈ 250 times per 72 h with 5 in stock. Default world rules: `raiderDestroyChance` 0.35 → 0.15 (a beaten raider flees damaged more often), `raiderRebuildTicks` 3000 → 6000, `maxRaidersPerActor` 3 → 2 (with fewer losses alone the stock rose to 3+3 and the loss rate doubled to 0.65 %), and `pirateTreasuryCap` 10000 (new rule `PirateTreasuryCap`, code default 0 = unlimited: the fence pays only until the pirate treasury reaches the cap — with fewer raider losses the pirate treasuries grew linearly, 267 000 Cr at the end of run M, and the treasury share of the money rose from 27 % to 43 %).

**Viability (`Simulation/FactionViability.cs`).** `Check(u, actor)` → `ViabilityIssue` list (pure): `TreasuryBelowReserve` (treasury < `Reserve` = obligatory upkeep per tick — government stations net of income, `PoliceMinPerFaction` × police upkeep, `MinBuildersPerFaction` × builder upkeep — × `FactionReserveTicks` 2400; Union ≈ 41 000 Cr in the default world), `PoliceBelowMinimum`, `BuildersBelowMinimum`, `NoSeat` (no HQ/company seat); outlaws only `NoHideout`; promoted independents have no invariants (they may perish, reason b). Discretionary spending uses `Spendable` = treasury − reserve − `ReplacementCost` (material value of a missing minimum ship): subsidies, maintenance refunds, police/builder purchases above the minimum, treasury fleet purchases, station investments (`Investors.ForStation`), research budget, lock enforcement (otherwise a paper lock). Obligatory upkeep (government stations, police, builders) may dip into the reserve. Replacement of a missing minimum has priority: `SecuritySystem.Commission(…, priority)` and `ConstructionRules.CommissionBuilder(…, priority)` need only the price (police also skip the change cooldown); if no yard can deliver or the treasury is empty, `FactionViabilitySystem` (Viability module, interval 100) restores it by **emergency commission** (`EmergencyCommission`: ship at the home base without yard material, the treasury pays at most `ViabilityRestoreShare` 0.5 of the material base value, the rest is waived — explicit money source, event `FactionRestored`, `TreasuryUpkeep` "restore"; brake `ViabilityRestoreCooldown` 10000 per faction, `Actor.LastRestoreTick` saved/hashed). `FactionKeepsReserve` false switches the reserve off.

**Display.** Station info row "Rentabilität" (`Profitability.Text`), faction detail line "handlungsfähig / nicht handlungsfähig: …" (`FactionViability.Text`), toast "Station aufgegeben: Name — pleite / länger nicht rentabel" when a station in the player sector is given up (`Departures.StationToast`). Selftest stage 50. Balance tool: "Abgänge je Aufgabe (Grund)" knows only zerstört/pleite/nicht rentabel (+ verkauft/zurückgegeben) and warns about removals without a departure event, "Anlässe" (detail per role, station departures, firm capital moves), departures in cut-off map parts, "Handlungsfähigkeit" per faction/pirate group (share of samples with a violation by kind, every 200 ticks after 10 % of the run), emergency restores.

**Persistence.** Additive (save stays v17): `ShipSave.Profit`, `StationSave.Profit` (`ProfitMeterSave`), `ActorSave.LastRestoreTick`; new rules in the saved `EconomyRules`. All in `UniverseHash`.

**Tests.** New `Phase22Tests` (25): every removal path only through a/b/c (raid/intercept, bankrupt ship with deadline/cargo/money cases, unprofitable ship with grace/window and money to the owner, police decommissioning down to the minimum, spare builder, bankrupt/unprofitable station, site expiry, player sale), measurement (EMA, hysteresis, transfers neutral, clock rests behind a closed gate, save/load bit-identical), reserve theory over eight discretionary spendings, minimum replacement with priority then emergency commission with brake, retirement never takes the last own supply or the last producer of a cut-off part, 40000-tick long runs on the default world and `TestWorlds.Classic()` (no minimum/seat violation at any sample, every removal carries a/b/c, treasuries ≥ half the reserve at the end), supply panic on the 11-sector world (Aurelia cut off), raid loss rate < 1 % on the 11-sector world, the fence stops at the pirate treasury cap. Adjusted on purpose: `Phase20Tests.Ueberzahl…` and `StationReviewTests` scenario/exceptions set the profit meter instead of the activity ("ausgelastet" → profitable; "freier Platz" is no longer an exception — new test that an unprofitable station decays with a free slot; "knappheit_kurz" retires without conversion), `StationReviewTests.Langlauf_Umwidmung…` runs 90000 instead of 60000 ticks (grace + window), `Bauschiff_WirdNachgekauft…` part 2 (priority purchase may use the reserve; with an empty treasury the minimum comes back through the emergency commission), `Phase20Tests.Ausmusterung…` and `EconomyFixTests.LangeUntaetig…` retire by measurement instead of idle time, `ActorTests.Zukauf…`, `EconomyFixTests` maintenance/subsidy and two `PoliticsTests` lock tests switch `FactionKeepsReserve` off (they test their own reserve rules/lock mechanics), `WalletTests.Migration_V6ToV7` switches the measurement off (the migration re-creates government stations without a meter), `ExportPlanetTests` export revenue with a 1 Cr tolerance instead of rounding (float sum on the rounding edge). 601 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build; before = the Phase 21 long runs in `/home/gerd/space-adventure-langlaeufe/elf-sektoren`).** All open runs "Preise im Zielbereich", exit 0: default 20000 (trade/mine 26.0/22.1, loss 0.73 %, 0.32 ms/tick) and 60000 (26.0/21.8, 0.57 %, 0.20 ms/tick); seeds 1–8 at 20000 (trade 26.0–26.5, mine 21.7–22.4, 0–4 bankrupt traders, loss 0.53–0.73 %, 0–2 HINWEIS lines); seeds 1–3 at 60000 (26.1–26.2 / 21.8–22.1, 1–5 bankrupt, loss 0.53–0.64 %); 400000 open (26.2/22.3, removals: miners 25 destroyed, traders 7 destroyed, 0 bankrupt, 0 unprofitable; 51 builds; loss 0.43 %; 0.16 ms/tick); 1036800 open (26.2/22.1; before (run A): trader bankrupt 8, raiders lost/built 258/254, 83 builds, loss 0.40 % → bankrupt 0, unprofitable 9 traders, raiders 156/154, 105 builds, loss 0.44 %, victims lost 53 → 73); run M (seed 3, 2073600 ticks, alternating gate closures): trader bankrupt 215 → 1, unprofitable 35, AI builds 332 → 185, raiders lost/built 424/422 → 274/272, hydrogen jettisoned 18744 units in 551 discards → 16 discards without hydrogen, loss rate 0.33 → 0.44 %, victims lost 90 → 123, money 1.50 M → 1.55 M (treasury share 27 → 30 %), 0.161 → 0.184 ms/tick. Every faction and pirate group viable in every run (0 % of samples with a violation), except seed 5 at 20000: Union treasury below the reserve 1.1 % of the samples (income side, early). No emergency commission was needed in any run. Gate runs (400000 ticks, closed at 100000; exit 2 only for the map-split warning, prices green): Alpha cut off (gates 1, 7, 19): the Alpha part ends with trade 1.0/3.2, mine 0.9/2.7 (Phase 21 run J: 0/0 — its fleet disappeared), removals there 2 traders + 1 miner bankrupt, none unprofitable (clock rests); Beta cut off (1, 3, 5, 11): Beta part 1.6/4.1 trade, 1.6/3.5 mine (run K: 0/0), 2 traders bankrupt in Beta; Aurelia cut off (17): Aurelia 1.3/1.6 trade, 4.6/1.4 mine (miners caught there count against its small share, world-wide mine max 28.1/22), 1 trader bankrupt.

**Known weaknesses.** Ships that are cut off with no work still go bankrupt (reason b, allowed): in the Alpha/Beta runs 2–3 ships per run; nothing replaces them inside the part (no yard). Raiders now live longer, so there are more raids and more destroyed victims (loss rate 0.33–0.40 % → 0.41–0.56 % in the long runs; < 1 % everywhere); the pirate treasury cap is a money sink. Firm capital balancing moves a lot of money (2.7 M Cr in 5134 transfers over run M) — it keeps poor siblings trading instead of dying, the profit measurement treats it as neutral. The reserve invariant can be violated by the income side (early game, seed 5: 1.1 %); spending never causes it. The emergency commission and the "last own supply" guard were exercised only by tests, never in the balance runs. Unprofitable retirements of traders (9–35 per long run) are mostly ships that lost cargo to raids repeatedly; station retirements stayed at 1 per run (an ore-starved steelworks). With a dead end cut off, the ships caught there exceed its share (mine 4.6/1.4 in Aurelia).

## Phase 23 (English summary): ship systems — runtime shield/bank/condition/magazine, power distribution, shield-first damage, ammunition as a good

First stage of the combat plan (`docs/KAMPF.md` sections 3, 5, 6). No hit rule, round combat, ammunition consumption, `rearm` service or module damage yet (Phases 24–26).

**Runtime state (`Model/ShipSystemsState.cs`, `Ship.Systems`).** Shield, power bank and magazine are stored as **deficits** (`ShieldMissing`, `ChargeMissing`, `AmmoMissing`; 0 = full), so every new ship is full without initialisation and an undamaged ship has only default values. Plus `Condition` per slot (0..1, empty list = all intact), `Mode` (`PowerMode` Balanced/Weapons/Shield/Engine) and `ShieldPauseUntil`. Read via `Ship.ShieldNow`, `Charge`, `AmmoLoaded`, `PowerMode`, `ConditionOf(slot)`. `ShipStats` gains `ShieldRegen` (Σ `shieldRegen`, now per second) and `AmmoCapacity` (Σ `ammoCapacity`); `ShipStats.Compute(cls, fit, condition, failBelow)`: a module below `EconomyRules.ModuleFailCondition` (0.25) keeps mass, storage (cargo, tank, magazine, bank) and armour, loses draw, grants and all other stats; reactors and engines never fail completely (half output/thrust, they keep their draw). `Ship.RecomputeStats` uses the condition and clamps the deficits to the new maxima. Nothing lowers the condition in Phase 23 (module damage is Phase 26); outfitter and upgrades reset the condition of a newly fitted module to 1.

**Power rule (`Combat/ShipSystems.cs`, pure).** Surplus = power output − draw. `Split(mode, PowerFocusShare 0.6)`: balanced ⅓ each, otherwise 0.6 for the chosen system and 0.2 for each other. `Flow`: the weapons share charges the bank (`ChargePerPower` 1 per second and power unit), the shield share regenerates the shield (`ShieldPerPower` 0.25, capped by `shieldRegen`), the engine share stays with the drive; a full or missing system passes its share to the other of shield/bank. `Advance(stats, state, seconds, paused, rules)`; after a hit the shield pauses `ShieldDelayTicks` (20) and its share charges the bank. Write points: `Absorb` (damage → shield first, returns the rest, starts the pause), `Drain` (bank; only tested — shots come with Phase 24), `SetMode`, `SetCondition`, `Ammo(u, ship)` (with `AmmoActive` off the magazine counts as full), `SpeedFactor` (Engine mode with surplus = 1 + `EngineModeSpeedBonus` 0.1; applied only to the player's flight in `Main`, NPC travel unchanged), `Wake`.

**`CombatModule`** (after Security, before Lifecycle) with `ShipSystemsSystem` (every tick, scales with `dt`): iterates only the derived index `Universe.Recovering` (`Combat/RecoveryIndex`, ship ids with shield or bank not full, ascending; rebuilt on load by `UniverseSerializer`; a stale entry never changes state, so save → load → continue stays bit-identical). Full ships cost nothing; a ship that is full again leaves the index and its pause is reset (default state, nothing saved).

**Abstract resolver.** `CombatRules.Strength` uses the current shield (`ShieldNow / 20`), and `CombatRules.Damage(u, side, fraction, floor)` (now with the universe) sends the damage (fraction × max hull, helpers half) to the shield first and the rest to the hull; the hull floor (player 25 %) applies to the hull only.

**Ammunition (data).** JSON good `ammo` "Munition" (solid, category `ammo`, base price 8, volume 1) appended at the end of the JSON goods: `DemoUniverse.Ammo = new(26)`, module goods now start at `FirstModuleGoodId = 27`. Recipe in the weapons works 1 steel + 1 copper → 10 ammo per 20 ticks (stocks ammo [10,10], steel [5,5]); stock-only shelves `ammo` [3,8] at shipyard, police station and trade station (no export good, no consumer). Module `weapon_magazine_s` "Munitionsmagazin" (utility S, `ammoCapacity` 60, steel 2 + hull plates 1; made by the systems works, yard shelf [1,2]; key sorts last so the other module goods keep their order). Switch `EconomyRules.AmmoActive` (code default true, default world `ammoActive: true` after measurement): off = magazines count as full and `WorldBuilder` drops recipes producing ammo and ammo stock entries (`FeatureSwitches.IsAmmo/OmitRecipe/OmitStock`).

**Migration chain frozen at v17.** `SaveGameJson` now reads the default world for its migrations through `MigrationBaseJson()`, which strips content added after v17 (`AfterV17Keys`: `ammo`, `weapon_magazine_s`; `AfterV17Stocks`: steel at the weapons works), so old saves migrate exactly as before (old saves are irrelevant by the user's decision; no new migration). Later phases append their keys there.

**Persistence.** Additive, save stays v17: `ShipSave.Systems` (`ShipSystemsSave`: deficits, condition, mode, shield pause; omitted while `ShipSystemsState.IsDefault`), new rules in the saved `EconomyRules`. `UniverseHash` mixes deficits, mode, pause and condition (hash values change).

**UI.** Flight HUD: shield bar real (current value, "–" without a shield module), power bar real (draw ÷ output, text "draw/output" + bank), three toggle buttons Waffen/Schild/Antrieb (96×56 below the credits, between the status panel and the turn buttons; tapping the active mode = balanced; `FlightHud.PowerModePressed` → `ShipSystems.SetMode`). The placeholders `UiPlaceholders.ShieldPercent/PowerText` are gone. Dock ship card shows the real shield; asset ship detail a systems line (shield, bank, magazine, power, distribution; `AssetsScreen.SystemsLine`); outfitter shows bank/shield/magazine and diffs for shield, shield regen and magazine. Selftest stages 51/52: HUD values equal Core values, button size/placement/no overlap, mode toggling and the speed factor, a test shield slot absorbs a hit (hull unchanged), the HUD shows the hit shield, the ship is in the recovery index and the shield recharges.

**Balance tool.** Lines "Stahl/Kupfer/Munition (Ø Preis/Basis letzte 25%)" (dense sample) and "Schiffssysteme" (recovery index max/now, ships with a shield not full, share of `ShipSystemsSystem` in the runtime); option `--ammo on|off`.

**Tests.** New `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/Phase23Tests.cs` (12): split and flow (overflow, regen cap, no surplus), advance scales with `dt` and the pause holds the shield, shield takes damage first (small/large hit, hull floor, helpers half, strength with current shield), the system leaves full ships alone (hash unchanged, empty index) and a hit ship recovers within the time the rule predicts and leaves the index, Shield mode recharges faster than Engine mode, condition (failed laser loses Mining, reactor/engine half, storage kept, saved and re-applied, intact = empty list), ammunition content (good, category, id, not exported, single recipe, shelves, magazine stats), magazine full on a new ship and full with the switch off, world without ammo content when off, save roundtrip with systems bit-identical incl. 2000 ticks of continuation, determinism, 20000-tick run (shield/bank invariants, index complete, encounters happened, ammo delivered, loss rate < 1 %, trade/mine capacity ≥ 80 %). Adjusted on purpose: module count 22 → 23, JSON goods 25 → 26, ammo exempt from "used somewhere" (consumer comes with Phase 24), ammo allowed behind propellant, module-factory "no module" case via `maxTier` 0 (every slot type now has a module), factory recipe test skips the ammo recipe, the Phase 21 id test compares JSON goods and the module-good order, `Miners_ExtractOreAndEarnCredits` checks the surviving founder miners (a raid loss is a legitimate departure since Phase 22), five migration tests run on `TestWorlds.Classic()` (`Migration_V6ToV7`, `V7ToV8`, `AlteKette`, the realistic v4 without refineries) or expect the classic world's first module good 26 (`AlterV5Save`). No test removed. 613 tests green, selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build; before = Phase 22 with the same tool).** All runs "Preise im Zielbereich", exit 0. Default seed 20000: trade/mine 26.0/22.1 → 26.3/22.3, loss rate 0.73 → 0.62 %, HINWEIS lines 2 → 0, steel 1.23 → 1.15×, copper 1.27 → 1.06×, ammo 0.94×, 0.316 → 0.334 ms/tick. 60000: 26.0/21.8 → 26.4/22.2, 0.57 → 0.56 %, HINWEIS 0 → 0, steel 1.05 → 0.98×, copper 1.18 → 1.18×, ammo 0.94×, 0.200 → 0.216 ms/tick. Seeds 1–4 at 20000: trade 26.0–26.5 (unchanged band), mine 21.9–22.1 → 21.9–22.3, loss rate 0.53–0.66 → 0.48–0.69 %, HINWEIS 0/0/0/0 → 0/1/1/1 (the known early ore-starved steelworks/copper smelter lines; control runs with `--ammo off` gave 0/4/0 on seeds 2/3/4, i.e. path variance, not ammunition), steel 1.11–1.23 → 1.11–1.20×, copper 0.92–1.27 → 1.01–1.17×, ms/tick 0.29–0.34 → 0.31–0.33. No emergency restores. Recovery index at most 3–6 ships; `ShipSystemsSystem` 0.11–0.19 % of the runtime. Raids on the default seed at 20000: 55 → 77 (raiders with shields survive more encounters), at 60000 172 → 171.

**Known weaknesses.** No start or AI model has a power bank, so outside combat the weapons share flows into the shield and Weapons/Shield modes act alike; only Engine slows shield recharge. Nothing drains the bank yet. The Engine mode only affects the player's flight. Ammunition has a one-time shelf demand only (≈ 25 units), the recipe then pauses; no consumption, no rearming. The magazine module is on no start model. Module condition is prepared but never lowered.

## Phase 24 (English summary): hit rule and round combat (NPC vs NPC), tactics, flight and cargo surrender, ammunition consumption, armed traders

Second stage of the combat plan (`docs/KAMPF.md` sections 2, 3.3, 3.4, 4.1, 4.2, 4.2a). Raids on NPC ships (incl. non-active player fleet ships) and intercepts by police/hunters now run as a round combat in the Core; the raid on the **active** player ship keeps the Phase 11 flow (warning, escape by docking, abstract resolution with the 25 % hull floor) until Phase 25.

**Weapon data.** Weapon values are free module stats (`damage`, `reload` s, `range`, `accuracy`, `tracking`, `ammoPerShot`) plus the module field `damageType` (`energy`/`kinetic`, `Module.DamageType`, saved additively in `ModuleSave.DamageType`); `Module.IsWeapon` = damage > 0. `WorldBuilder.ParseWeapon` validates (damage/reload/range > 0, accuracy and tracking in (0, 1], ammoPerShot ≥ 0, damageType required and known; weapon stats or damageType without damage throw). `weaponPower` is now derived (`ShipStats.WeaponPowerOf` = damage × accuracy ÷ reload × `WeaponPowerPerDps` 2; the free stat stays as fallback for old content) and still feeds `CombatRules.Strength` (AI evaluation, abstract resolver). `ShipStats.Armor` (Σ armor). Content: `weapon_laser_s` damage 7, reload 2, range 60, accuracy 0.85, tracking 0.8, peakDraw 1 (weaponPower 5.95); new `weapon_massdriver_s` "Kinetisches Geschütz" (kinetic, damage 10, reload 3, range 45, accuracy 0.75, tracking 0.4, 1 ammo per shot, own magazine 30; weapons works via the factory mechanism, yard shelf [1,3]; key sorts last so module good ids stay). `warden` and `corsair` now carry laser + massdriver + `weapon_magazine_s` (corsair also `chem_engine_s_mk2`, speed 8.6 > unit traders 8, warden 6.9). New trade models `mule_armed` (laser in the light freighter's weapon slot) and `hauler_armed` (massdriver + magazine). The classic test world's laser got the same values. `SaveGameJson.AfterV17Keys` gained the massdriver and both models, and `AfterV17Fits` restores the v17 fits of warden/corsair for the frozen migration chain.

**Hit rule (`Combat/Hit.cs`, pure).** `WeaponProfile`/`TargetProfile`/`HitResult`; `Hit.Chance` = accuracy × (1 − `HitRangeFalloff` 0.5 × (d/range)²) × 1/(1 + max(0, targetSpeed/`HitTrackingSpeedRef` 10 ÷ signature − tracking)) × shooter factor, clamped to [`HitChanceMin` 0.05, `HitChanceMax` 0.95], 0 beyond range; signature by class size (`CombatSignature` S 1, M 1.5, L 2.2, XL 3); fixed arc penalty per weapon `ArcFactor` = 1 − `CombatArcPenalty` 0.2 × (1 − arc/360), bow weapons on the run × `CombatFleeFireFactor` 0.5. `Hit.Damage`: shield takes damage × type factor (`EnergyVsShield` 1.25, `KineticVsShield` 0.6), the rest (converted back) loses armor × `ArmorPerHitShare` 0.05 × type factor (`EnergyVsArmor` 1.5, `KineticVsArmor` 0.5), the remainder hits the hull. `Hit.Roll` always draws exactly `DrawsPerShot` (2) numbers (hit, spread ± `HitDamageSpread` 0.2). `Hit.Apply` writes via `ShipSystems.TakeShield` (pause + recovery) and an optional hull floor.

**Encounter (`Model/Encounter.cs`, `Combat/CombatBook.cs`).** `Universe.Fights` holds the running encounters (id, sector, start tick, cause Raid/Intercept, raider + owner, victim, helping police, loot, fence, shots, hits) with `CombatParticipant`s (ship, side 0/1, tactic key, position X on a 1D combat line — the "distance number", target, last move/speed, reload timers per slot, courage, drop share, previous power mode, exit); the index ship → encounter is derived (`Rebuild` on load). Saved additively (`SaveGame.Fights`, `NextEncounterId`) and hashed. `Universe.RemoveShip` calls `Fights.OnShipRemoved` (any departure path): the ship leaves its encounter, and if a side becomes empty the rest is released at once; the evaluation follows in the next combat step.

**Lock and release (`Combat/Engage.cs`).** `Join` stops a command's executor like the emergency level (`IOrderExecutor.Stop`), ends a running trip where the ship is (`OrderMove.StopHere`, interpolated position, order and reservation released — no ghost orders) and draws exactly two numbers (courage U[`SurrenderHullMin` 0.05, `SurrenderHullMax` 0.6] — player ships at least `PlayerMinHull` + 0.05 — and drop share U[`SurrenderDropMin` 0.4, `SurrenderDropMax` 0.7]). Engaged ships are skipped by TraderSystem, MinerSystem, JobAssignment, ConstructionSystem builders, UpgradeSystem, OrderSystem (combat ranks above the emergency level), StepPolice, StepRaider, hull regeneration, bankruptcy/unprofitability (`Departures`), spare-builder retirement, `Victims`/`PoliceIn`/hunters. `Resume` after the exit: the command restarts (`Start`), raiders fly home, police re-plan, traders sell their cargo via `SellStranded`, miners with ≥ 1 unit deliver to the best buyer (`MineOrder` field −1).

**`CombatSystem`** (first system of the `CombatModule`, every tick, only active encounters; then `ShipSystemsSystem`, then `RearmSystem` interval 10). Per tick: every tactic decides on the same state (target, move Approach/Hold/Flee with wanted distance, power mode, fire, surrender); surrender: the participant drops its drop share of every good, the raider takes it (fence into the pirate treasury as before, capped) and breaks off; movement simultaneously (speed × `ShipSystems.SpeedFactor`, Engine mode +10 %); fire: per weapon slot the timer runs down, a ready weapon in range fires at the target (not with a failed module, not with an empty magazine — `ShipSystems.Ammo`; ammo weapons only from a hit chance of `AmmoMinHitChance` 0.3; `peakDraw` from the bank, otherwise the reload stretches to peak ÷ weapon share of the surplus; ammo `AmmoMissing += ammoPerShot` with `ammoActive`); event `ShotFired`; hull 0 → `Departures.RemoveShip` reason a, detail raid/intercept (player-owned ships never: hull floor `PlayerMinHull`); a fleeing participant beyond every active enemy's max range (at least `CombatMinEscapeDistance` 30) has escaped. End: a side gone, loot taken or `CombatMaxTicks` (240). `Finish` publishes `CombatExited`/`EncounterEnded` and — for compatibility with tools/tests — `RaidResolved` (won = loot or victim destroyed; threat + `RaidThreat` else `RepelledThreat`) or `RaiderIntercepted` (police won = raider destroyed or driven off; threat lowered); bounties for player participants when the raider is destroyed. Piracy is reported at the start of a raid. Starting geometry: raider at 0, victim at `CombatStartDistance` 30, police joining with the old response chance at +`CombatHelperDistance` 30.

**Tactics (`Combat/CombatTactics.cs`, `ICombatTactic`, registry `CombatTactics` raider/police/trader/escort).** Raider: keeps the victim as target, approaches to `CombatPreferredRange` 0.7 × its shortest weapon range (Engine mode while far behind, Weapons mode in range), flees under `RaiderFleeHull` 0.5 (0.3 before the review) or when its side is weaker than `RaiderFleeRatio` 0.6 × the other. Police: attacks the nearest raider (same approach/pursuit), retreats under `PoliceRetreatHull` 0.3, no pursuit beyond range. Escort (escorts/guards of the victim, Phase 12 `FleetGuards`): attacks, retreats under `EscortRetreatHull` 0.3. Trader (all economic victims incl. miners and player fleet ships): armed and own side ≥ `TraderFightRatio` 0.8 × enemy → holds and fires; otherwise flees in Engine mode and fires back if armed; surrenders below its courage when a raider attacks and cargo is aboard. Player fleet rules of engagement: Passive flees without firing, HoldFire holds without firing (both for trader and escort tactics). Victim choice of raiders: weight ÷ (1 + `RaiderArmedAversion` × weaponPower/6).

**Ammunition (`Combat/Rearm.cs`).** `AtStation` buys from the station's ammo shelf at market price (station paid), `AtBase` without stock at base price × `AmmoBaseMarkup` (sink), `AtHome` (police/pirates, called from `PirateSystem.ServiceAtBase`) pays from the owner's treasury (`TreasuryUpkeep` "ammo", event `AmmoRearmed`); `RearmSystem`: docked AI ships with missing ammo rearm at that station from their own wallet. Police return home when the magazine is below `AmmoReturnShare` 0.3. `ammoActive` off: no consumption, magazines full. No player `rearm` service yet.

**Armed traders.** `FleetSystem.ChooseModel` multiplies a model's value by 1 + `FleetSystem.ArmamentBonus` = `ArmedTraderWeight` (40) × `AreaRisk` (mean `ThreatMap.RouteRisk` from the buying sector to every reachable sector) × min(1, weaponPower/6): in calm areas the weapon is not worth its price, at the border it is.

**Phase 23 review.** (a) `ShipSystems.CanRecover`/`Recovers`: a ship without reactor surplus (or a shield without regeneration) no longer stays in the recovery index forever — `Wake` and `RecoveryIndex.Rebuild` add only ships that can recover, `ShipSystemsSystem` drops them, `Ship.RecomputeStats` wakes the ship after a module change. (b) Test that `ClampSystems` clamps the shield deficit on a module change. (c) `EconomyTests.Miners_ExtractOreAndEarnCredits`: the founder miner missing in Phase 23 was verified on the Phase 23 tree to be a raid victim (ship 38, tick 1565, destroyed/raid); the test now runs with `securityActive` off and again requires **all** founder miners to exist and earn > 1000.

**Display.** `Combat/CombatInfo` (pure: `CountIn`, `InSector` with title/detail/ships, `StartToast`); `SecurityInfo.ForSector` gains `Fights` and mentions running encounters in its summary. View: `World/CombatView` draws `ShotFired` events in the player sector as short beams (energy red, kinetic yellow, misses beside the target) plus a small burst on hits; toast on `EncounterStarted` in the player sector; command-centre sector view: red ring around engaged ships and a "Gefecht: …" line in the sector card (`SectorScreen.TestCombat`); universe map: red cross at sectors with an encounter and "Gefecht" in the security sub-line. No player intervention. Selftest stage 53 (raid in the player sector: toast, situation count, sector card, beams drawn, encounter ends and both ships are free).

**Balance tool.** Lines "Gefechte" (count by cause, running/max, mean duration, shots/hits, shield/hull damage), "Ausgänge" (by outcome and role/exit), "Munition" (consumed, rearmed units/cost, from treasuries, police share of treasury income), "Bewaffnete Händler/Schürfer" (now and bought), "Rechenzeit Gefecht" (CombatSystem, RearmSystem).

**Persistence.** Additive, save stays v17: `SaveGame.Fights` (`EncounterSave`, `CombatParticipantSave`), `NextEncounterId`, `ModuleSave.DamageType`; new rules in the saved `EconomyRules`. `UniverseHash` mixes the encounters.

**Tests.** New `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/Phase24Tests.cs` (24 test methods, 29 cases after the review): weapon data and derived weapon power, validation, hit rule (determinism, exactly two draws even out of range, distance/speed/signature/arc), damage types (shield/armor/hull order), unarmed trader surrenders (cargo dropped, loot = event, raider takes it and flies home), faster ship escapes / slower does not, armed trader fires back, police intercept (driven off or destroyed, only via `Departures`), player escort defends with hull floor and its command continues, Passive/HoldFire never fire, lock and clean release (trip ends in place, no order while engaged, no ghost orders afterwards), removing a participant frees the other side, ammo consumption/empty magazine/switch off, rearming with bookings (trader at the yard, police from the treasury as "ammo", pirates at base price), armament bonus by route risk, recovery index fix and shield clamp, save mid-combat bit-identical incl. 400 ticks of continuation, determinism, 40000-tick runs on the default and classic world (loss rate < 1 %, no ghost orders, engaged ships have no order/transit, encounters end within the maximum, every combat loss went through `Departures`, factions keep police minimum and seat, trade/mine capacity ≥ 80 %). Adjusted on purpose: module/model counts (24/24), the Phase 23 magazine test (warden now carries massdriver + magazine: capacity 90, clamps to 30 then 0), `CapabilityTests` save test skips armed models (Combat from their fit), `CapabilityTests.TraderInTransit` stops the traders where they are and gives them money (path: the chosen trader sat at its next buy station), `Phase20Tests.Panik_AlphaZu…` removes an early lifecycle water plant/site outside Alpha to restore its premise, `RespawnTests.FormatV2…` ignores deliver-only mine orders (field −1, unknown to the old format), `EconomyTests.Miners_…` sharpened (see above). 641 tests green (642 after the review), selftest OK.

**Balance (fresh Release build; before = Phase 23 tree with the same runs).** All runs "Preise im Zielbereich" (exit 0; the gate run exit 2 only for the map-split warning, as before). Default 20000: trade/mine 26.3/22.3 → 26.5/22.4, loss rate 0.62 → 0.50 %, victims destroyed 3 → 2, raiders lost/built 6/4 → 3/2, HINWEIS 0 → 0, 0.334 → 0.322 ms/tick; 66 encounters (47 surrendered, 14 fled, 4 destroyed, 1 timeout), mean 85 ticks, 46 % hits. 60000: 26.4/22.2 → 26.5/22.1, 0.56 → 0.42 %, victims 5 → 4, raiders 10/9 → 11/9, HINWEIS 0 → 0, 0.213 → 0.224 ms/tick; police share of treasury income 7.2 → 7.0 % (+0.06 % police ammo). Seeds 1–4 at 20000: trade 26.0–26.5 → 26.1–26.5, mine 21.9–22.3 → 21.9–22.5, loss 0.48–0.69 → 0.28–0.68 %, victims 2/0/0/1 → 2/2/0/2, raiders lost/built 4/2, 1/0, 3/2, 2/1 → 7/5, 6/4, 4/3, 9/6, HINWEIS 0/1/1/1 → 0/0/0/0, police 5.2–6.0 → 5.3–5.5 %. 200000 with gate 17 closed at 50000: 25.4/21.6 → 25.4/22.4, 0.55 → 0.18 %, victims 14 → 4, raiders 34/33 → 26/22, HINWEIS 0 → 0, police 8.3 → 7.3 %. 400000: 26.4/22.4 → 26.1/22.3, 0.46 → 0.20 %, victims 21 → 13, raiders 66/65 → 53/50, police 8.3 → 7.3 %, 0.173 → 0.166 ms/tick. Same run twice → same hash (default 20000: 85F8F74F59A99AB3). CombatSystem 0.4–1.4 % of the runtime. Armed traders bought 0–2 per run (`mule_armed`). All factions and pirate groups viable, no emergency restores.

**Correction after review.**
- (A) *Pirate economy.* With the first calibration the pirates spiralled down over long runs (300000 ticks: raids per 20000 ticks 59 → 13–31, 0–1 raiders per group, treasury 0–3000; fence 93k against 95k costs). Cause on the income side: raiders fought on until 30 % hull while police joined and were destroyed far more often per raid (53 losses per 572 raids vs 66 per 1056 abstract), so rebuilds (2000 Cr each) ate the whole fence income; the massdriver also burned ammunition (bought at base price) on long-range shots with little chance; the drop share 0.3–0.6 gave less loot per success than the abstract loot. Changes (data knobs): `RaiderFleeHull` 0.3 → 0.5, surrender drop share 0.4–0.7 (`SurrenderDropMin/Max`, separate from the abstract `LootMin/Max`), `AmmoMinHitChance` 0.3 (pure rule, no random draw). Balance tool: new line "Piraten über die Zeit" (raids per 20000 ticks and raiders/treasury per group). `FactionViability` for outlaws unchanged.
- (B) *Trader poverty trap.* The reviewer counted 8.75 bankrupt traders on average over four 200000-tick gate runs (rounds) vs 3.75 abstract. After (A) the same probe gives 4/4/1/1 (mean 2.5), the balance tool 4/4/1/1 on the default seed and seeds 1–3. The remaining bankruptcies show no combat in the last events: they are the known thin-margin spiral (Phases 20/22) — a trader that once bought at a price the buyer no longer pays (worth −200…−1000 Cr at one sale) can afford only 1–3 units of energy/electronics per trip afterwards and shrinks by a few credits per round. No clean new rule was found; the gap to the abstract resolver came from the pirate collapse (more and longer combats, lower threat → riskier thin routes), not from surrender losses.
- (C) `EconomyRules.JobMinSwitchScore` (0.5 Cr/tick): `JobAssignmentSystem.ShouldSwitch` needs at least that score for the new job (two Tinkers had switched 34–42 times between scores 0 and 0.1); test in `MultiroleTests`. Job switches in the gate runs now 1–2.
- (D) `Engage.Leave` restores `DockedAt` from the position (`Universe.StationAt`) when the ship is still parked at a dock point (test `Andockplatz_BleibtNachDemGefecht…`); `"-ammo"` is an expected purpose in the treasury explainability test; brackets in the `CapabilityTests` filter.
- Numbers after the correction (before = first Phase 24 calibration): default 20000 loss rate 0.50 → 0.44 %, victims destroyed 2 → 0, raiders lost/built 3/2 → 3/2, hash twice 75532A3F54AD3D9A; 60000 0.42 → 0.39 %, victims 4 → 1, raiders 11/9 → 6/5; seeds 1–4 at 20000 loss 0.31/0.55/0.68/0.28 → 0.45/0.89/0.79/0.48 %, victims 2/2/0/2 → 0/0/0/0, HINWEIS 0/0/0/0 → 3/1/1/1 (known early ore-starved steelworks/water plant Aurelia); 400000 0.20 → 0.50 %, 572 → 975 raids (48.8 per 20000), victims 13 → 9 (Phase 23: 21), raiders lost/built 53/50 → 21/20, police 7.3 → 8.2 % of treasury income (+0.11 % ammo), two raiders per group from 120000 ticks on, treasuries 7000–10000; gate run 200000 default/seeds 1–3: loss 0.51/0.37/0.79/0.68 %, bankrupt traders 4/4/1/1, victims 2/2/0/5. All runs "Preise im Zielbereich", gate runs exit 2 only for the map split. 642 tests green, selftest OK.

**Known weaknesses.** Seed 2 at 20000 reaches 0.89 % loss rate (below 1 %, but close); long-run loss rates spread 0.37–0.79 % between seeds. Single 20000-tick windows can still drop to ~30 raids when one group is wiped out and rebuilds. Remaining trader bankruptcies are the thin-margin spiral described in (B). Armed traders stay rare (0–2), the hauler with the massdriver was never bought (ammo logistics only through docking at stations with an ammo shelf). Police ships can now be destroyed (2–3 per long run; replaced by purchase; Montanbund sometimes runs at its minimum of one patrol in gate runs). The 1D distance number has no real positions; in the player sector combat participants stand still and only the beams show the fight (Phase 25). Reload timers live in the encounter, not on the ship. Tactics never use the power bank (no AI model has one), so `peakDraw` rarely limits fire. Courage and drop share are random per encounter; victim destruction is sensitive to `SurrenderHullMin`.

## Phase 25 (English summary): player combat — real-time shots, weapon bays, spatial combat in the player sector, assault and bounties, real ship loss, `rearm`, fighter

Third stage of the combat plan (`docs/KAMPF.md` 2, 4.3, 5). The raid on the **active** player ship is now a real round combat; the player fights in real time with the same hit rule as the NPCs.

**One model, two geometries (`Combat/CombatSpace.cs`).** `Encounter.Spatial` (saved, hashed): an encounter in `Universe.PlayerSector` runs with real positions (`CombatParticipant.Pos`/`Dir`, saved and hashed only for spatial encounters; `PrevPos` is display-only and not saved), everywhere else with the Phase 24 distance number `X`. `CombatSpace.Sync` (before every combat step) switches when the player enters or leaves the sector: line → space keeps every participant's distance to the anchor (first active defender at its real position, axis towards the real position of its nearest enemy); space → line projects onto the anchor–nearest-enemy axis (that pair's distance is kept exactly; ships stay where they last were). Neither transition draws a random number. `CombatSense.Distance` uses `Pos` when both participants are `InSpace` (derived, set by `Derive` after loading). Rule `SpatialCombat` (default true; false = line everywhere). New joiners in a spatial encounter get an explicit start position (`CombatSpace.Around`: raider at `CombatStartDistance` from its real direction, police at start + helper distance).

**`CombatAutopilot` (pure step function, like the mining autopilot).** `Step(CombatPilotInput(pos, dir, maxSpeed, target, move, wanted, reach, orbitSign), seconds, rules)` → `CombatPilotStep(pos, dir, speed)`: facing turns at most `CombatTurnRate` (120 °/s) towards the target (away when fleeing) so bow weapons bear; movement: approach until the band `wanted × [CombatBandMin 0.75, CombatBandMax 1.1]`, orbit inside the band at `CombatOrbitSpeedShare` (0.5) of the speed with a pull to the wanted distance, back off when too close, flee straight away; Hold stands still unless the target is out of reach. No obstacles (fights are short; the view pushes markers out of rocks). `CombatSystem.MoveInSpace` runs it for NPC participants simultaneously from the old state and writes the result to `Ship.Position` (the ship stays parked in its sector); the active player participant takes the ship position the view flies. `Materializer` draws engaged spatial ships at `Lerp(PrevPos, Pos, fraction of the tick)` facing `Dir`. In space, NPC weapons fire only when the target lies inside the slot's real firing arc (`FiringArc.Contains`, no fixed arc penalty or flee factor).

**Shared shot rules (`Combat/Gunnery.cs`)**, used by NPC fire and by the player: `WeaponAt`, `BayOf` (bay from slot facing/arc and module range), `HasPower`/`HasAmmo`, `DrawPower` and `Shoot` (ammo, `Hit.Roll` with exactly two draws, `Hit.Apply`, timer, `ShotFired`, destruction). **Power distribution "Waffen" now has its own effect — overcharge:** in Weapons mode, a ship with a power bank holding peak × (1 + `OverchargeDrawFactor` 2) draws that much and reloads in `OverchargeReloadFactor` (0.65) of the time; otherwise peak from the bank, or fire at the pace of the weapons share of the surplus. Ships without a bank (all NPC models) behave exactly as in Phase 24. The new fighter carries a bank.

**Player shots (`Combat/PlayerGunnery.cs`).** `TryFire(u, ship, slot, target, headingDeg)` → `GunShot(GunResult, HitResult, encounter, message)`: active ship, undocked, weapon in the slot, target in the sector and allowed (`PlayerCombat.TargetIssue`: not own ships, not docked, not an ally in the same fight, not a ship in another fight), in range (real distance), reloaded, in the firing arc (heading from the view, `FiringArc` bearing scale), ammo, power — any failure changes nothing (no draw, no encounter).
