Default hash at 20000 ticks unchanged (F27F5C7D53338CBD).

## Round 22 (English summary): assets value and follower state

- **Ship value in "Besitz":** the active Bollwerk showed "Wert ≈ 0 Cr" because `SectorEconomy.TotalValue` of a player ship is only its cargo (money is in the wallet). New `SectorEconomy.ShipSaleValue` (= `Hangar.SellPrice`: material × sell share × damage factor; rentals 0) and `PlayerShipValue` (sale value + cargo at base prices); the row and `PlayerNetWorth` use the same function (header label "Kasse + Schiffe + Fracht"). `TotalValue` keeps its NPC meaning. Test `Runde22Tests`.
- **Follower state:** a ship with a follow/escort command and no dock reads "im Verband" (also while travelling after its leader) instead of "geparkt" (`AssetsScreen.StateText`; "angedockt" only with `DockedAt`). Checked in selftest stage 100.
- **Auto-fire over two raids (device h3: second corsair next to the ship, "Kein Ziel", "Dauerfeuer" pulsing off):** auto-fire is reset only on a new session (`AttachSession`) and at the end of an own siege — not at the end of a raid, on target loss or on bounty messages. Selftest stage 101 (`SelfTest.Round22.cs`) replays the combat test through the view (auto-fire tapped on, first corsair down, second raid announced and started): auto-fire stays on (state, button `On`, no pulse), the second corsair becomes the target and is shot. The "off" on the device was not reproduced. Found and fixed: a raid that came due during another fight is re-scheduled without a new warning (`PirateSystem.ResolvePending`), so the announcing corsair never became the target ("Kein Ziel"); the view now takes it as target once per announcement (`Main.UpdateHud`, `_pendingTargeted`). The raid alert reads "Dauerfeuer läuft." when auto-fire is on, else "„Dauerfeuer“ an?".
- Default hash at 20000 ticks unchanged (F27F5C7D53338CBD).

## Trade-chain test (English summary)

User request "test the whole trade chain with the player trading himself". No rule changes (default hash F27F5C7D53338CBD unchanged). `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/HandelsketteTests.cs`: (1) NPC side on the default world, 20000 ticks — table per chain good (mined, bought at producers, trips/units, delivered to consumers/shelves, exported); fails only if a chain good has no producer/rock class or no taker (kits excepted: they are built into ships at the yard), if an ore/refined/component/end good has no trip or neither consumption nor export, or if one of the four planet sinks exports nothing. (2) The player trades the whole chain only through the `trade` service the UI uses (`Offers`/`Execute`; travel by setting the ship at the dock point): mines every rock class with the player's contact rule (`OreChunks.Contact`) and sells all six ores; buys every chain stage at a producer and sells at `MarketInfo.BestBuyerForGood`; sells end goods to all four planet sinks (each batch exported after its delay with paid × 1.18); buys a module at a module works and sells it to a yard. Checks per trade: offer amount > 0 executes and moves exactly that amount, wallet + station cash conserved, the player ship keeps 0 credits, hold never over capacity. Round-trip table and findings in the test log. (3) Player fleet automation (trade + mine commands) earns on the wallet. (4) Same seed → same table. Selftest stage 102 (`SelfTest.Chain.cs`): a short chain through the trade screen with taps (mined ore at the smelter, steel bought at the steelworks with "Abwerfen" next to the reason, sold at the best buyer, food sold to a planet sink, station info shows "Export an …"). Findings: medium/heavy kits have no taker besides the yard itself (by design); the light kit's only other taker (trade station shelf) is often full.

## Round 23 (English summary): honest buyer hint, trade editor reasons, dock menu, margin probe (no economy rule changes)

- **Honest buyer (`Trading/MarketInfo`, pure):** `IncomingTo` (what other traders already bring — the planners' ReserveIncoming arithmetic, read only), `QuoteBuyer` (units a delivery of q would really sell now: sale rule `SaleCaps`/`SaleUnits` with acceptance minus incoming and cash ÷ price; revenue, jumps, travel ticks; null when nothing is taken or paid), `HonestBuyers`/`BestHonestBuyer` (ranked by expected revenue for the quantity, not by unit price) → `BuyerQuote`. Device case: "best buyer" Gamma 66.4 named, 49.8 paid, 3 of 22 sold.
- **Trade editor (view):** three live lines (redrawn every 0.25 s from the market, not from the row snapshot — the 1426 vs 1377 Cr difference was a stale row price): "Kauf q × p = s Cr" or "Kaufen: <reason>" (export sink "Nur Ankauf (Export)", no credits, no hold space, stock empty, wanted, build site) plus "Verkauf hier n Cr"/"—"; "Abnehmer: <station> (<sector>, n Sprünge) zahlt p · nimmt jetzt m" or "Derzeit kein Abnehmer"; "Erwartet: +x Cr" / red "Verlust −x Cr" / "Abnehmer nimmt nur m von q" with "Preise ändern sich unterwegs". Lines fitted with the font. Editor taller (134/200 px); opening a row keeps the tapped row in place, the opened row incl. "Abwerfen" scrolls fully into view.
- **Dock menu:** subtitle keeps "Kasse n Cr" visible (type dropped / faction shortened first), card 662 px high (footer no longer touches the frame), new "≡ Menü" button (camera, sound, save/load/new game reachable while docked; closing returns to the dock menu, also from the command centre). Taxi tile without a bookable destination is dimmed with "Kein Ziel buchbar".
- **Flight HUD:** "Station angreifen"/"Tor angreifen" moved one row down and narrower (764, 330, 136 px; "Ziel folgen" 764, 392) — clear of the context and autopilot buttons.
- **Margin probe** `--probe spielerhandel` (balance tool, `PlayerTradeProbe`, read only; hashes unchanged): every 200 ticks per chain good the cheapest producer with stock and the honest buyers → share of profitable round trips with a 100 hold (a: full load; b: only what a buyer takes now), average/best profit, per tick, early (< 5000) vs later; end goods also to planet sinks only; NPC unit freighter earnings per tick for comparison.
- Tests: `Runde23Tests` (accepted units = sale rule incl. cash, incoming subtracted, ranking by revenue); selftest stage 103 (`SelfTest.Round23.cs`).

## Fleet test (English summary)

User request "build several fleets, command them, automatic player trade fleets". `tests/SpaceSim.Tests/FlottenTests.cs` (default world, UI paths: `shipdealer`/`hangar`/`rental` services, `Commands`, `Squadrons`, `PlayerFleet.SwitchRemote`): purchases at the three yards with booking checks and a deliverability table (at tick 200 only 5 of 33 model × yard combinations are deliverable — engines M, universal cargo 60, reactor Mk2 missing on the shelves; after 20000 ticks 33 of 33); command capacity (the squadron limit bites — 5 ships allow 1 squadron —, the single-command limit never does, because the size class grows with every ship: 6 ships = Firm (16), 15 = Corporation (32), 30 = State (64)); squadrons (three kinds, max 8, slowest speed, work command to capable members, leader succession, disband); every player command runs (goto, hold, patrol over two sectors, guard, trade, escort across sectors, follow, mine, build at a site, return) and unfit ships are refused with the capability; escort answers a raid by rules of engagement (Defend/Attack join, Passive/HoldFire never fire); automatic trade fleets 40000 ticks with 2/4/8 traders + 2 miners (limits kept, ships keep 0 credits, wallet explained by events to < 1 Cr, profit per ship 1.7 → 1.4 → 0.9 Cr/tick, NPC trade revenue −13/−23/−34 %, no NPC bankruptcies); emergency repair resumes the command, remote switch into a travelling ship, hangar sale, rental not sellable; save/load mid-operation bit-identical, offline catch-up keeps the fleet trading. Fixed on the way (player ships with limits only, default hash unchanged): `TraderSystem` now re-checks the automation limit "max buy price" at the purchase (it was only checked when planning — fleet traders bought food at 1.5×, consumer goods at 1.9× with a 1.3× limit); automation text "2 Sprünge" (was "Sprunge"). Selftest stage 104 (`SelfTest.Fleet.cs`): two purchases at Werft Vesta by tap, Kommando → Flotte: mine command, trade command with limits, form a squadron, escort with target choice, assets, HUD running costs, remote switch and back; no button overflows; tap depths logged.

## Round 24 (English summary): fleet-test findings fixed

- **Loss guard for the player automation:** `TraderBuyGuard`/`TraderPlanGuard` now also apply to player ships with a trade command (`TraderSystem.LossGuarded`: AI always, player while `AutoLimits.AllowLoss` is false — new record field, default false, saved as an optional third value of the limits, hashed only when true). Refusals publish `TraderBuyRefused` with `Reason` "loss" or "price_limit" (the max-buy-price limit of the fleet test fix). Fleet screen: toggle "Verlustkauf gesperrt/erlaubt" next to the limit steppers (steppers narrower). NPC path unchanged.
- **Yards deliverable at the start:** shipyard shelves (type stocks; mostly full start stock at small targets): `cargo_universal_60` [5,5], `cargo_universal_100` [5,6], `chem_engine_m` [2,2], `reactor_mk2` [2,2], `missile_launcher_s` [2,2], `shield_m` [2,1], `armor_plating_m` [2,2], `reactor_m` [2,1], `weapon_laser_m` [3,1], `reactor_mk3` [1,2], `cutting_wheel_m` [1,2], `cutting_drum_l` [1,2]. At tick 200 (fresh world, each combination alone) every yard sells a trader, a miner and a combat model, and every model except the Leviathan is deliverable at ≥ 1 yard. Side effect: factions can now replace police (and buy warships) from the start; the war-test start package holds faction purchases for 2400 ticks (`ScenarioDef.PoliceHoldTicks`: `Actor.LastForceTick` set into the future; police purchases, minimum replacement, viability restore and warship purchases wait for it — never in the future in normal games). Migration base and `Phase29Tests.RevertRunde24` restore the old shelves (reference-hash tests stay green).
- **Command level:** `Commands.CapacityClass` — the player's size class for `CommandShips`/`CommandSquadrons` with own ships weighted `EconomyRules.CommandShipWeight` (0.25; stations, sectors, money as before); factions/pirates keep the size class. Single ship: 4 commands, 1 squadron; 8 ships: Small firm (8, 1); 100 000 Cr or a station: Small firm. "Meine Fraktion" shows "Befehlsstufe"; messages "Befehlskapazität erreicht (1 Verband) – …" with what to do.
- **Escort in three taps:** with an armed ship selected, every other row in the fleet list carries "Begleiten" (escort by the selected ship); escort/follow target labels show model, id and place.
- Fleet test: ghost-order check for all order kinds, soft scaling asserts (total profit positive, not dropping below 80 % of the smaller fleet, no NPC trader bankruptcies), per-ship results split into traders and miners, refusals by reason; it gives the measurement fleet's commands with `ignoreCapacity` (the limit is tested separately). `dotnet test` takes ~6–7 min.
- Selftest stage 79 (autopilot to HQ Alpha from four starts) now starts its own fresh world ("new:fighter"); with the changed start economy the state left by the earlier stages made start 1 hang 86 m from the HQ. Default hash at 20000 ticks: F27F5C7D53338CBD → D3567DA325B7FED9 (yard shelves; the loss guard, command level, scenario hold and UI changes do not touch the NPC economy).

## Round 25 (English summary): autopilot in dense rock fields; trade-margin measurements (no balance change adopted)

- **Autopilot hang (selftest stage 79, state after the earlier stages):** cause measured — the player's `PathFollower` planned through a dense rock cluster in Alpha (rocks 2 m apart, safety margin 5.5 m): `PathPlanner`'s detour recursion exhausted depth/budget and returned zig-zag points a few metres apart plus segments that still crossed rocks; the ship stalled, re-planned and circled 86 m before the HQ for 45 s. Reachable in normal play (autopilot through a belt). Fix (player autopilot only; NPC travel plans unchanged, hash unchanged): `PathPlanner.Smooth` (string-pulling to the farthest clear later point), `PathPlanner.Clear`, and `PathPlanner.Detour` (fixed ring candidates around start, goal and midpoint — 16 directions × 4 radii, no randomness — shortest one- or two-waypoint path whose segments are clear) when the smoothed plan still crosses rocks. Test `Runde25Tests.Autopilot_DichtesFelsfeld_…` with the measured rocks; stage 79 runs again in the state left by the earlier stages (Round 24's fresh world removed) and docks from all four starts.
- **Trade margins measured, nothing adopted** (`--probe spielerhandel`, 60000 ticks; new balance options `--exportbid X`, `--wcshare X`, `--sellcap X`, new probe `--probe kassen`): export bid for consumer/luxury goods 1.4/1.5/1.6 → "consumer goods → planet sink" stays 2–3 % profitable late (1.25: 5 %), money +4/+5/+12 %; working-capital share 1.5/2 → margins unchanged, money +22/+42 %, module works still cannot pay a 100-unit load 26–37 % of the time; producer price cap at 0.9/0.95 × best buyer bid (new rule `ProducerPriceCapShare`, default 0 = off) → shares rise (food 97 %, electronics 70 %, life support 58 %) but trips earn 5–25 Cr, NPC unit freighters drop from 2.09 to 1.02/0.79 Cr/tick and consumer goods fall to 0.44×. Reason: NPC traders arbitrage every margin at once; the end-good chain sits at producer ask ≥ buyer bid in equilibrium. Colony life-support bid (`ConsumerMaxBidFactor`) not changed: the colonies' life-support price is 0.84–0.96× base, far below the 2× cap, so the cap has no effect. Payment ability of processors (`--probe kassen`): only the module works (weapons 46 %, drive 38 %, systems 30 % of samples), lifecycle-built electronics fabs (37 %) and yards (22 %) cannot pay a 100-unit load of their dearest input; all others ≤ 18 %.
- Small items: balance tool counts `TraderBuyRefused` by reason and AI/player; fleet screen resets the loss-guard toggle on a new session; yard shelves `shield_m` [2,1] and `weapon_laser_m` [3,1] keep their start stock above the target on purpose (one destroyer's worth at the start without adding permanent demand — a target equal to the stock would ask the weapons works for 2–3 more M modules per yard for good; not measured, left as is); fleet-test log labels the sequential purchase and the 20000-tick line as what they are.
- Default hash at 20000 ticks unchanged (D3567DA325B7FED9).

**Round 25 addendum (device test of rounds 23/24).**
- *Buyer hint at arrival:* `MarketInfo.ArrivalPrice(u, st, good, incoming)` uses the market's own stock formula (`MarketSystem.StockPrice`, now public) on stock + deliveries other traders already bring; a capped price stays capped. A sale settles one unit price for the whole quantity (trader and `TradeActions` alike), so the own load does not move the price. `QuoteBuyer` returns the arrival price as `Price` (revenue = units × arrival price) and the current one as `BuyerQuote.PriceNow`; the ranking follows the expected revenue at arrival. The trade editor shows "zahlt jetzt p, bei Ankunft ≈ p′" and "Erwartet bei Ankunft: +x Cr" / "Verlust bei Ankunft −x Cr"; every hint line has shorter variants picked by measured width (no "…"). Quality (`--probe hinweis`, 20000 ticks, 1226 samples: estimate at departure vs a sale of the same quantity at arrival after the travel time): unit price error 90 % quantile 3.0 % → 0.0 % (median −0.1 / −0.2 %); revenue error median 0 % both, 90 % quantile 72 → 70 % — the remaining tail is the accepted quantity (stations full or short of cash on arrival because of traders who plan later), not the price. The margin probe (`--probe spielerhandel`) uses the arrival price too; shares profitable late fall by 1–16 points (e.g. food 46 → 34 %, electronics 45 → 40 %, copper 39 → 32 % on the default seed).
- *Autopilot detour (war test, "Route nach Vesta" from Beta flew via Delta):* reproduced in the Core — the path from the start 80 m before Sektor-HQ Beta to the Vesta gate passed 3 m from the Delta gate and any gate within 10 m makes the player jump. `Navigator.PlayerObstacles` adds every other gate of the sector with its jump radius (`Navigator.GateJumpRadius` 10) as an obstacle for the player autopilot (`Navigator.Next` and `PathFollower`), except the goal gate and a gate the ship stands in after a jump; NPC travel unchanged (hash unchanged). Test `Runde25Tests.Autopilot_Kriegstest_RouteNachVesta_OhneUngewolltenSprung`.
- *Command capacity:* over the capacity (e.g. after disbanding a squadron whose members keep their work commands) running commands stay and may be changed, new single commands are refused (`Commands.OverCapacity/OverCapacityText`); the fleet header turns red ("zu viele, neue gesperrt"), disband/leave messages name it; fleet messages wrap to two measured lines. **Round 36:** disbanding/leaving that would push single commands over the capacity is now refused (the disband loop is closed); over capacity now only happens when the command level drops. Escort button in the fleet list reads "Von #x / begleiten lassen".
- *UI:* assets status follows command and activity ("schürft", "handelt", "… · angedockt", "unterwegs nach …", "hält Position", "im Einsatz"; "geparkt" only without command and travel); fleet detail command line stops before "Übernehmen"; `CommandScreen.Row` shrinks the value to font 11 and then trims inside the card ("Laufende Kosten" in "Meine Fraktion"); the shipyard drops its last purchase message when the area is opened again (`DockAreaScreen.OnOpen`); a hangar sale the station cannot pay shows the reason with the station's cash ("Kasse 59 von 2140 Cr") on the disabled button (`ServiceOffer.Note`).
- Tests `Runde25Tests` +4 (war-test route, arrival price formula, over-capacity rule, sale reason); selftest stage 103 checks that the editor lines fit, stage 104 the miner status, two-line capacity messages and the running-costs row. Default hash unchanged (D3567DA325B7FED9).

## Round 26 (English summary): honest buyer hint (careful estimate, traffic light), player flight time, small device-test fixes

User decision: "Handel so lassen" — the trade economy stays unchanged (no balance change for player margins: export bids, working capital, colony bid, stock targets; no delivery contracts/bonuses, no player advantage). That manual trading pays only with few goods and chances after the start phase is accepted (money comes from mining and the own fleet). Round 26 is strictly information/display; `ProducerPriceCapShare` (off) and the balance-tool measuring switches stay; no further margin experiments.

- **Cause (measured, new `--probe hinweis` in the device setup: start package "Händler" via new balance option `--start trader`, the player buys 10 units at the cheapest and at a random seller, the editor's BEST buyer, the player's real autopilot flight time, from tick 0; seeds 11–15 × 8000 ticks, ≈ 12 700 trips):** (a) the hint named the buyer with the highest momentary price — exactly the spikes NPC traders and miners head for after the player leaves: 26–33 % of the best-buyer trips see NPC deliveries at the buyer on the way, which `IncomingTo` cannot know (they had no order yet; miner deliveries were never counted); (b) the arrival estimate used the NPC estimate (`Travel.Estimate`, 12 s median), the player really flies 24–25 s (`Flight/PlayerTrip`); (c) the early phase (< 2000 ticks, stocks start at half target) is worse: 90 % quantile of the price error 25–32 % vs 16–20 % later; (d) most "green but loss" trips (1211 of 1511) were not a price drop but a buyer whose cash was gone on arrival; (e) the device showed only "zahlt p" because the Round 25 line printed both values only when they differed — the arrival estimate had subtracted nothing (no trader underway). Before (Round 25 rule): price error median 0 %, optimistic > 5 % in 25 % (early) / 21 % (late) of the trips, **"green but loss" 30 % / 25 %** of the trips shown green.
- **Careful estimate (`MarketInfo.CarefulPrice`):** min(arrival price by the stock formula, fast moving average of the buyer's price, midpoint between arrival price and base price), and the observed inflow into the buyer's stock (traders and miners) continued over the player's flight time (`PlayerTimeFactor` 1.9 × the NPC estimate; consumption not subtracted). `PriceMemory` (Core, `Trading/PriceMemory.cs`) holds the moving averages and inflow per station and good — fed by `GameSession` every 20 ticks, not saved, not hashed, empty after load. `BuyerQuote.Price` is the careful price (`PriceArrival`, `PriceNow`, `CashCover`, `AcceptCover` added); the ranking follows it. **Traffic light** `MarketInfo.Verdict`: green only if the careful price is ≥ 15 % above the purchase and the buyer's cash and acceptance cover the load twice; red if even the careful estimate is a loss or the buyer takes less; otherwise yellow "Unsicher (Kasse des Abnehmers knapp / Lager fast voll / knappe Spanne)". After (same setup): median error −11 % / −9 %, optimistic > 5 % 12 % / 8 %, **"green but loss" 14 % / 12 %** (energy and ice, the device goods: 17 % / 20 % → 10 % / 14 %); the green trips together earn 368 k Cr early (old-rule green trips: 140 k — the losers drop out) and 149 k late (old: 192 k); 43 % / 37 % of all profitable trips are shown green.
- **Display:** "Abnehmer: X (Sektor, n Spr., ~0:25 min) zahlt jetzt p · bei Ankunft etwa p′ · nimmt m" (always both values; flight time from `PlayerTrip`, cached per standing point in `HintState`), line 3 "Erwartet bei Ankunft: etwa +x Cr" (green) / "Unsicher: …" (orange) / "Verlust bei Ankunft etwa −x Cr" (red). Hysteresis: the named buyer stays while its careful revenue is ≥ 90 % of the best (`HintState`, per station and good). Buy/sell messages name the booked amount ("Gekauft 10 × 5,3 = 53 Cr") — the price moves with the ticks between the 0.25-s display refresh and the tap.
- **Flight time:** `Flight/PlayerTrip.Seconds` simulates the player autopilot (same `PathFollower`, speed 45 u/s, acceleration 40, slowing near the goal and obstacles like `Main`, 1 s per jump, 1.5 s start, 2 s docking); selftest stage 79 compares it with the real view flight from four starts (estimate/real 8.3/7.5, 14.5/14.9, 14.8/14.5 s — and 7.3/12.6 s from a start where the ship first had to turn and clear a rock; checked within 0.4–2×).
- **Small fixes:** `Navigator.PlayerObstacles` caches its list per tick (thread-local, the reviewer saw a copy per frame); fleet header "Einzelbefehle n/m · Verbände k/l · Verband zählt als 1" (members of a squadron count through the squadron); disabled squadron buttons name the reason ("nur Anführer", "kein Verband frei"); "Meine Fraktion" ability notes fit their column; the target card's name/info lines no longer stretch under the radar ring.
- Tests: `Runde26Tests` (careful price, price memory, traffic light, ranking, player flight time, booked amount in the message, obstacle cache); `Runde23Tests` revenue at the careful price. Default hash unchanged (D3567DA325B7FED9).

## Round 27 (English summary): buyer hint simplified — price range instead of a forecast; one price in trade; tick rate measured

Device test of Round 26: three green energy trips ended at −35/−3/+2 Cr, the named buyer changed every ~5 s, prices jumped several-fold within a minute, row price, editor price and booking differed (6.0 / 7.5). A point forecast is not honestly possible in these markets (one NPC load of 100 units halves a price at stock targets around 100), so the hint now promises nothing.
- **Tick rate:** `GameSession.TickRate` (ticks per real second over 2 s) is shown at the start of the camera diagnostic line ("Takt 4,0 Ticks/s", "(×10)" while time-lapse is on). Selftest stage 105 (`SelfTest.Round27.cs`): 4.00 ticks/s after "Neues Spiel", 40 at ×10, 4.00 after switching it off and 4.00 after loading (time-lapse off after new game and load). The price jumps are real simulation behaviour: per minute (240 ticks) 2.5–3.1 % (early) / 1.5–2.0 % (later) of all market entries more than double or halve; the largest one-minute jumps in the trader start (seeds 11–15) were water ×4.6–5.7, wafers ×3.4–6.4, copper ×4.0–5.7, energy ×3.5–4.3.
- **Buyer changes (cause):** the Round 26 hysteresis kept a buyer only while its careful revenue stayed ≥ 90 % of the best — with prices jumping several-fold it lost that test every few seconds. Now `HintState` holds the named buyer of the open row until the row is closed or the quantity changes (no automatic change; if it stops accepting, its own quote is shown).
- **Price mismatch (cause):** the row drew the price of the last list rebuild, the editor redrew every 0.25 s, the booking used the price at the tap. Now row and editor redraw on every tick change and read the market price of that tick (`LivePrice`); a tap whose price moved more than 2 % against the player since the last display books nothing and says "Preis hat sich geändert: jetzt p statt q — nochmal tippen" (the next tap books at the new price; Core unchanged).
- **New hint (no promise):** line 2 "Abnehmer: X (Sektor, n Spr., Flug ~m:ss) zahlt jetzt p · zuletzt lo–hi · nimmt m" — lo/hi are the lowest/highest price of that buyer for the good over the last 1200 ticks (`PriceMemory`, one sample per 20 ticks, view only: not saved, not hashed; "zuletzt –" without history). `BuyerQuote.Price` = worst case = min(arrival price by the stock formula, lo); `PriceHigh`, `HasHistory` (≥ 600 ticks observed). Ranking by quantity × worst case, ties by today's price. Traffic light `MarketInfo.Verdict`: GREEN "Lohnt sich auch im schlechten Fall: mind. +x Cr" only with history, worst case ≥ 10 % above the purchase (`GreenMargin`), buyer cash ≥ 4 × the load (`GreenCashCover` — NPC deliveries empty a cash box within seconds) and acceptance ≥ 2 ×; without history there is never green (rule, not wording); RED "Verlust: etwa −x Cr" if today's price already loses or the buyer takes less; otherwise YELLOW "Riskant: zwischen −a und +b Cr". No "bei Ankunft" any more, "etwa" only. The Round 26 careful estimate (`CarefulPrice`, inflow, `PlayerTimeFactor`) is gone. Flight time is labelled "Flug ~m:ss" (flight only; undocking, menus and docking are not included — the device measured 40–55 s against 0:17–0:19 announced including those).
- **Probe** `--probe hinweis` (start "Händler", seeds 11–15 × 8000 ticks, 12 737 trips, the editor's buyer, real player flight time): green 12.6 % of the trips early (< 2000 ticks) / 15.3 % later, **green but loss 9 % / 7 %** (Round 26: 14 % / 12 %, Round 25: 30 % / 25 %); yellow 57 % / 58 % (loss 34 % / 30 %), red 31 % / 26 % (loss 94 % / 93 %). Of the trips that actually paid, 23 % / 25 % were shown green, 73 % / 72 % yellow, 3 % red. The worst case was undercut by the price at arrival in 15 % / 8 % of the trips; the remaining green losses are mostly buyers whose cash was spent by NPC deliveries during the flight (unsold units count as lost revenue in this measure). The probe also reports the one-minute price jumps.
- **Small:** `Navigator.PlayerObstacles` cache key also includes the sum of rock and station radii (rocks shrink within a tick while the player mines); fleet header adds "· Verband zählt als 1" only when a squadron exists (confirmed).
- Tests: `Runde26Tests` reworked (price range window, traffic light by the worst case, ranking, cache with a shrinking rock). Default hash unchanged (D3567DA325B7FED9).

## Round 28 (English summary): trade hint and trade list after the device test of round 27 (information, display and operation only)

- **"Green broken" (consumer goods Tethys → colony Gamma: "zuletzt 121,6–155,3 · GRÜN" → seconds later "zuletzt 74,0–157,0", paid 84.7–86.3 at arrival) — cause:** the lower value of the line was the worst case, i.e. min(history low, price after the deliveries other traders are already bringing), not the history. Right after the purchase an NPC trader had taken a load for the colony (stock ≈ 89 at 157 Cr; 140 × 100 ÷ 189 = 74 with 100 units underway — matching the 85 paid on arrival). Before its decision no information could know it. The colony's price itself is a saw-tooth (Core series in `r28/kolonie_reihe.txt`, seed 11: slow rise with consumption, −25 … −45 % on every NPC delivery, e.g. 193 → 155 → 112 within ~550 ticks). Changes: `PriceMemory.Observe` runs every tick and keeps min/max per 20-tick slot (a one-tick valley between two samples is no longer missed); history counts only with a full window (`EnoughTicks` 1200, was 600); `BuyerQuote.HistoryLow` is the history alone, the line shows "zuletzt lo–hi" (or "zuletzt stabil p") and separately "· andere bringen n → etwa p" when deliveries underway lower the price. `Runde28Tests.Kolonie_LieferungAndererHaendler_NichtGruen` rebuilds the case (a trader with consumer goods for the colony → arrival price by the stock formula, not green). Probe (start "Händler", seeds 11–15 × 8000 ticks): green 6.8 % early (fewer: full window needed) / 14.8 % later, **green but loss 8 % / 7 %**; the remaining green losses on end goods are 12 of 316 trips.
- **Open row closed by itself / second tap hit another row — cause:** a row (and a tile) toggled on any release inside its header — also a release whose press had happened before a rebuild (hangar change, ticks: the dock areas rebuild on tick changes) on the old node or on another row. Now the press is remembered in the screen by good and screen position (`TradeScreen._press`, survives rebuilds) and a tap counts only if the release is on the same good's header within 14 px and no scroll drag runs. Open row, chosen quantity and held buyer already lived in the screen.
- **Quantity:** the chosen quantity stays when another row is opened (default 10, no jump to the affordable amount); the buy line says "Kauf 10 × p = s Cr — nur 6 bezahlbar / auf Lager / passen in den Laderaum" and the button reads "6 kaufen" (never silently fewer); hint and traffic light calculate with the units really bought.
- **Messages:** "Gekauft 10 × 10,15 = 101,5 Cr" — unit price and amount with decimals so the message equals the booked amount. Texts: "zuletzt stabil 12,6", "Riskant: etwa +13 Cr" when worst and today are equal; no buyer line for a good that can neither be bought here nor is aboard (export sink "Nur Ankauf").
- **Selftest** stage 106 (`SelfTest.Round28.cs`): in the longest list (Werft Delta, 54 rows) five rows are opened one after another by real taps while ticks run, scrolling rows into view first (each time the tapped row opens), an open row survives a rebuild with a held, moving finger, the quantity stays 10, "3 kaufen" and "nur 3 bezahlbar" with limited money, and the price check appears on the real tap on "Kaufen" when the price rose 50 % between display and release (stage 105 covers the same through the screen call). The probe runs the price memory per tick (`HintProbe.Observe`, run hash unchanged).
- Default hash unchanged (D3567DA325B7FED9).

## Round 29 (English summary): buy-back loop measured (no rule changed), yellow band, small trade-screen fixes

- **Buy-back loop (device: fusion reactor bought for 359 at Handelsstation Beta, sold back at once for ~538) — measured, nothing changed** (`--probe rueckkauf`, `tools/SpaceSim.Balance/ExploitProbe.cs`, start "Händler", 2000 ticks lead-in, seeds 11/12): a purchase is settled for the whole quantity at the price before it (`TradeActions.Buy`: n × e.Price), the next tick the market prices the emptied stock by the stock formula (up to 4× on small targets) and the station takes the goods back at that price for the whole quantity. Shelves (stock-only entries, accept up to target) and consumer inputs (accepted up to the overstock limit) are affected — 46–52 % of the shelf entries and 46–50 % of the consumer entries give a profit per loop (median 730–740 / 295–362 Cr, highest 4 875 / 5 870 Cr: Mk1 mining laser 325 → 1 300 at a yard, platinum at the luxury workshop); producer goods never (`NoIntake`). Twenty minutes of looping (one loop per two ticks, best expected entry whose buy-back the station can pay) at one Beta station: up to 43 000–47 000 Cr (electronics fab Beta, 2 160–2 350 Cr/min), the trade station 24 600–24 900 Cr, until the station's cash is empty (it refills from income, deliveries and subsidies). Ship sale is safe (the hangar pays 0.5 × the material at the lowest current market price of each good, at most its base price — always below the purchase price); outfitter removal and ammunition/missile shelves are the shelf loop. **Correction (review, Round 30):** the gap was open in Round 29 and had two directions — the probe measured only buy → sell back; selling first at the pre-sale price and buying back cheaper a tick later (stock risen, price fallen) also paid (reviewer, seed 11: 232 of 352 entries, median 249 Cr, electronics at the luxury workshop +12 943 Cr with unlimited station cash). It has existed since stock-only shelves (Phase 5a) and the per-quantity settlement (Phase 1); NPC traders cannot use it (they buy only at producers and never sell at the station they bought from — `TraderSystem` lines 128/152). Variants (not built): (a) per-unit settlement along the curve closes the loop exactly, honest 10-unit buys at producers +3.7–5.4 % (90 % quantile 13.7–14.7 %); (b) price after the trade: loop loses, honest buys +8.5–12.5 % (90 %: 33–37 %); (c) a fixed buy-back spread would need up to 300 % on small shelves and hits every honest sale; (d) buy-back cap only for a time window leaks — after 5 / 10 / 20 minutes still 121–144 / 100–117 / 89–94 of ~250 entries are > 5 % above the purchase price (80–175 k Cr possible); (d') a cap without time limit (what the player bought here is taken back at most at the price paid, cleared when the stock is back at its pre-purchase level or the units are sold back) closes it without touching honest trade. Recommendation: (d') as saved per station × good state, hashed only when set (default hash unchanged); awaiting the user's decision.
- **Yellow band:** the lower number of "Riskant: zwischen a und b Cr" is now the result after ONE more NPC load at the buyer (100 m³, `BuyerQuote.PriceAfterLoad`; `PriceMemory.MaxDelivery` records the largest one-tick stock increase for the probe): "actual result below the stated lower bound" among yellow trips 37 % / 32 % (early/late) → 15 % / 17 % (largest delivery seen in the window: 32 % / 29 %; max(largest delivery, 50): 18 % / 19 %); average band width 80 → 355–520 Cr. A fully positive yellow band names its reason ("noch kein voller Verlauf", "Kasse des Abnehmers knapp", "Lager des Abnehmers fast voll", "andere liefern", "Spanne unter 10 %").
- **Small:** tile view — bar, note and stock each on their own line (note no longer over "Bestand"); the buy button and the buy line follow the live `TradeActions.MaxBuyable` every tick (a stale row snapshot drew an active "Kaufen" next to "Lager leer"); opening a row scrolls only when its editor would be cut off (selftest stage 106 checks a fully visible row does not scroll; when the editor does not fit the minimal scroll puts its bottom at the list bottom); the buyer line switching between a loss buyer and "Derzeit kein Abnehmer" is the real state (the held buyer's acceptance or cash flips).
- Default hash unchanged (D3567DA325B7FED9).

## Round 30 (English summary): buy-back cap (both directions of the same-station loop closed)

- **Rule (`Trading/PlayerLedger.cs`, state `Universe.Ledger`, `Model/LedgerPost`, rule switch `EconomyRules.BuybackCap`, default on):** posts per (player actor, station, good) — on the actor, so a second own ship or a fleet ship cannot bypass it; NPCs never get posts. A purchase records the quantity at its market price: selling that good back at THE SAME station pays for up to that quantity at most the price paid (min(market, paid)), beyond it the market price. A sale records the mirror: buying back pays for up to that quantity at least the price received (max(market, received)). The opposite trade first reduces a post, only the rest opens a new one; the cap price is the lowest open purchase price / the highest open sale price (a weighted average let an older, dearer remainder lift the cap for new cheap units — found by the probe). Expiry without a time limit (the Round 29 measurement showed windows leak): a purchase post expires once the station's stock is back at its level before the first purchase AND the market price is no longer above the price paid (stock alone was not enough — consumer prices follow the willingness ceiling, e.g. copper at the electronics fab 20.6 at stock 170, 50.0 a tick later at stock 250); a sale post mirrored. Expiry runs every tick in `LedgerSystem` (Trade module; nothing to do without player trade) and is final; empty posts are deleted. Mixed settlement: capped/floored units first, the rest at market; messages name the booked amount and "davon n zum Rückkaufpreis p (dein Kauf hier / deine Lieferung hier)".
- **Wired:** `TradeActions.Buy/Sell/SellAll/Sellable/Quote/MaxBuyable` (hence the trade service offers and execution: offer = execution), player fleet trade in `TraderSystem` (fleet purchases open posts, fleet sales use the cap), outfitter (fitting from station stock = purchase, removal with sale to the station = sale), `Rearm.AtStation`/`Missiles.AtStation` (magazine filled from the shelf = purchase), ship purchase (consumed kit and modules = purchases at their market price). Not affected: upgrades and repair (material consumed), storage (no market), hangar sale (see Round 29, safe).
- **Display:** buy/sell offers carry the note "Rückkauf deiner Lieferung: mindestens p für n E." / "Rückkauf: höchstens dein Kaufpreis p für n E."; the trade editor's buy and sell sums use the effective prices and show the note, the traffic light compares with the effective price here.
- **Probe** `--probe rueckkauf` (now: buy → sell back, buy with ship A → sell back with ship B, mirror sell → buy back, outfitter removal/refit both ways, ammunition, 20-minute continuous loops per Beta station in both directions; `--buyback off` for comparison; seed 11). Without cap: shelves 114 of 147 entries with profit (median 550, max 4 875 Cr), consumer goods 55 of 102 (max 10 522), two ships 169 of 375, mirror shelves 64 of 171 (max 235), mirror consumer goods 63 of 103 (max 5 629), outfitter 9 of 32 (max 277), ammunition 5 of 6 (192), continuous loops 0–41 109 Cr per station and direction in 20 minutes (smallest non-zero value 4 053 Cr; the solar plant and the systems works give 0 for buy → sell back). With cap: **every single loop ≤ 0** (0 entries with profit in all categories) and every continuous loop ≤ 0 (losses, the station cash is no source any more). Honest trade unchanged: `Runde30Tests.EhrlicherHandel_FlotteUndRundreise_MitUndOhneDeckelGleich` (three trading fleet ships for 6000 ticks plus a manual round trip A → B: wallet and world hash identical with and without cap); NPC worlds have no posts and hash identically (default hash D3567DA325B7FED9 unchanged).
- **Persistence:** additive `SaveGame.Ledger` (only with open posts), hashed only when present; save → load → continue bit-identical with open posts.
- Tests: `Runde30Tests` (shelf loop, consumer mirror loop, second ship, mixed quantity and message, expiry by stock return and no revival, outfitter, NPC world, save/load, offer = execution over 108 combinations, honest trade); selftest stage 107 (`SelfTest.Round30.cs`): buy → tick → sell back at a yard shelf and sell → tick → buy back at a consumer through the trade screen, both without profit.
- Remaining ways around (honest): buying at station A and selling at station B is ordinary arbitrage (allowed); a post expires only when the price has come back — as long as the market price stays above the price paid the cap holds (deliberate consequence: speculation at the same station is capped; selling elsewhere is not); the reviewer found one remaining gap (a post expired while the player still held the goods: 74 consumer goods bought at 188 at colony Gamma, deliveries refilled the stock, the post expired, 26 bought on top raised the price to 280, 100 sold → 74 uncapped, once +6 627 Cr, as a continuous loop at most ≈ +1 000 Cr per 20 minutes; closed in Round 31); a ship purchase priced its materials at the market without the mirror floor (selling a module to the yard first and then buying a ship that consumes it saved at most +455 Cr per cycle; closed in Round 31).

## Round 31 (English summary): buy-back cap leftovers closed; UI stage E0 (check tool) and E1 (layout module, UI size switch)

**Part 1 — buy-back cap.**
- *Post persists while the player holds the goods:* `PlayerLedger.Prune` no longer drops a purchase post whose expiry conditions are met while the player still holds goods of it — held = cargo of every ship of the player actor + `PlayerStorage` at that station; the post shrinks to min(post quantity, held) and expires only at 0. Honest trade (bought at A, sold at B) expires as before.
- *One booking helper:* `PlayerLedger.BookBuy/BookSale` (→ `Booking(Units, Money, Ledgered, LedgerPrice)`) is the single booking path for trade (`TradeActions.Buy/Sell/SellAll`), player fleet trade (`TraderSystem`), outfitter (fit from stock / removal sale), `Rearm.AtStation`, `Missiles.AtStation`; `BuyResult.Floored/FloorPrice`, `SaleResult.Capped/CapPrice` carry the capped part. `ServiceOffer.Price` of the trade offers is the effective unit price (`PlayerLedger.BuyUnitPrice/SaleUnitPrice`), not the market price.
- *Ship purchase:* `ShipDealerService.Quote` prices materials the player sold to this yard at least at the price received (mirror floor via `PlayerLedger.BuyCost`).
- *Proof (reviewer's attack program, copy in the r31 archive):* expiry variant (buy, deliveries refill, post would expire, buy on top, sell all): every cell negative (before up to +1 037 / +3 481; colony Gamma consumer goods now −2 490 … −9 481); ship purchase after a module sale: 78 models, best −725 Cr net per cycle (before +455); save/load + second ship: 920 cases, 0 with profit. **Correction (review of round 31):** the remaining positive cases of the random fuzz (max 7 973 Cr with the cap) were declared honest price gains on carried stock — that was wrong, they were real loops: the cap only covered the post quantity, everything beyond ran at the market price the player had moved himself. Loop A (bring q0 = 50 from elsewhere, buy Q = 50 at X — pays p0 and drives the price up —, one tick later sell Q + q0: the first Q capped at p0, the carried q0 at the raised price): extra gain per trip with cap (without) colony Gamma consumer goods 183 → 280 +4 848 (+9 695), colony Tethys consumer goods +4 118, luxury workshop Gamma platinum +4 048, colony Gamma life support +3 835, systems works Beta titanium +3 160, scaling with the hold or a second ship. Loop B (mirror: sell q0, price falls, buy back more than q0 — only the first q0 cost ≥ p0): luxury workshop Gamma luxury goods 1 101 → 524 indicatively +25 965, electronics fab Beta electronics +6 037, consumer goods factory Beta +5 230. The fuzz run without carried stock (max 0) only showed that the loops need carried stock. `attack_fuzz_fair2` valued the carried stock at the market price after the attack — the player's own sales had depressed that price, so the baseline was too low and the numbers too high; the right baseline is the undisturbed price (round 32 metric). Closed in round 32.
- *Trade messages (device test of round 30):* the header message wraps to two lines (font 15/14/13 measured with the font); the buy-back part is short (", davon n zum Rückkaufpreis p"); "Alles verkaufen" names units, amount and the capped part ("Alles verkauft: 7 Einheiten für 258,7 Cr"); a booking at a price that became better since the display (a delivery between display and tap — editor and booking use the same market price and the same `BuyCost`; the Round 27 guard books favourable changes on purpose) says so: "(Preis seit der Anzeige 24,7 → 12,2)".
- Tests: `Runde31Tests` (post held, honest expiry, ship purchase floor, offer price = effective price, "Alles verkaufen" message); `Runde30Tests` expiry test clears the cargo first (selling elsewhere).

**Part 2 — UI adaptation (docs/UI-ANPASSUNG.md), stages E0 and E1.**
- *E0 `--ui-check <dir>`* (`godot/scripts/UiCheck.cs`, needs a display): opens flight HUD (normal, raid warning, combat, auto-mining, station attack), hamburger menu, alert + toast, star map, dock menu and every available dock area of a shipyard, every command-centre area and the start-package chooser in six resolutions (1280×720 @294 ppi, 1560×720 @282, 2340×1080 @396 = A15, 1920×1200 @224, 2048×1536 @264, 960×540 @245 — assumed DPI per resolution) and three UI factors (1, 1.15, 1.3); resolutions larger than the test display are emulated by a window of the same aspect ratio (canvas_items/expand: the logical layout depends only on the aspect ratio), millimetres use the target resolution. Checks: overlapping interactive elements (visible part only — clipped by scrolling ancestors; while a screen/menu/map is open only its elements), truncated texts ("…" or text wider than its field), tap targets below 7 mm / 5 mm, elements outside the visible area, fill share of full screens. Each state is measured twice 6 frames apart (the calmer result counts — a rebuild in mid-measurement otherwise counted duplicate buttons). Output `ui-check.txt` (table screen × resolution per factor + single findings), `ui-check.json`, PNGs at factor 1.
- *IST (factor 1):* 0 overlaps and 0 elements outside in every state and resolution; tap targets: a 56 px button is 4.8 mm (1280×720), 5.0 (1560×720), 5.4 (A15), 9.5 (10″ tablet), 8.6 (4:3 tablet), 4.4 (960×540) — on phones nearly every button is below 7 mm, the smallest 3.5–4.0 mm (map layer buttons, Autopilot/Auto-Mining, area rail, "Liste/Kacheln"); truncations in the station info (long good names), factions/universe cards and long toasts; full screens fill 95 % at 16:9, 78–79 % at 19.5:9 (A15), 85 % at 16:10, 71 % at 4:3. *Factor 1.15/1.3:* every state has elements outside the visible area (the fixed 1280×720 screens and the HUD grid do not fit the smaller logical area) — scaling is useless until the screens are adaptive.
- *E1:* `SpaceSim.Core/Ui/UiLayout` (pure, `UiLayoutTests`): `UiSize` klein/normal/groß, stretch scale, logical size, millimetres, DPI factor (7 mm target), `Factor(size, w, h, ppi, cap)` = klein 1, normal min(1.15, DPI factor), groß min(1.3, DPI factor × 1.1), capped, never below 1 (also for an invalid window size — headless reports 0 × 0, which first gave 0.78; `UiScale` then takes the root window size); `FixedScreensCap` = 1 while screens are fixed 1280×720 (E2–E5 lift it); usable area (safe insets), class compact/normal, columns. The cap 1.3 keeps the logical area at ≥ 985 × 554 for a 1280×720 base, i.e. above the accepted minimum 960×540. Godot `Ui/Kit/UiScale` sets `Window.ContentScaleFactor`, recomputes on window size change, stores "ui/size" in `user://settings.cfg` (selftest/ui-shots/ui-check use `settings-test.cfg`; `AudioSettings.Save` keeps other sections), switch in the System area ("Bedienung": klein/normal/groß) and the hamburger menu (cycles, toast names factor and target). Today every stage gives factor 1 — pixel-identical to round 30; target values after E2–E5: A15 normal 1.15 (6.2 mm), groß 1.3 (7.0 mm); 720p phones 1.15/1.3; tablets 1 (≥ 8.6 mm already).
- Selftest stage 108 (`SelfTest.Round31.cs`): default normal = factor 1; "groß" saved in the test settings file and still factor 1; forced factors 1.15 and 1.3 (`UiScale.TestCap/TestPpi`): root content scale factor set, visible area = window ÷ (stretch × factor), camera screen↔world roundtrip of the ship, a tap pushed in window coordinates selects the station under it; back to factor 1.

## Round 32 (English summary): buy-back cap V1 (whole trade), honest measurement with carried goods, V2 compared; mirror floor for repair/upgrade; UI size text

- **Rule V1 (`EconomyRules.BuybackWholeTrade`, default true):** while a purchase post of the player actor is open at (station, good), EVERY sale of that good there — any quantity, any origin (carried goods, second ship, fleet ship) — gets at most min(market, lowest open purchase price); while a sale post is open, EVERY purchase there costs at least max(market, highest open sale price) (`PlayerLedger.SaleValue/BuyCost/Affordable`). New posts carry the price actually paid/received (a sale under an open purchase post opens its mirror at the capped price). Expiry unchanged (stock back AND price back; purchase posts additionally not while the player holds goods of that kind, `BuybackHeldKeepsPost`). `false` = round 31 rule (only the post quantity), kept for measurement.
- **Same mirror floor for services:** `PlayerLedger.MaterialSurcharge/MaterialPrice/OnConsumed` — ship purchase, upgrades (`ShipUpgrade.Make/Attempt`) and repair (`RepairService.PricePerHull/PricePerModulePoint/Perform/PerformEmergency`, optional ship parameter) price material the player sold here at least at the received price, and consumed material opens a purchase post. NPCs unchanged (default hash D3567DA325B7FED9).
- **Display:** notes "Solange dein Kauf hier offen ist: Verkauf höchstens zu deinem Kaufpreis p" / "Solange dein Verkauf hier offen ist: Kauf mindestens zu deinem Verkaufspreis p"; offer prices and the trade editor's traffic light use the effective prices. Diagnostic bus event `LedgerApplied` (never saved).
- **Measurement (attack program `r32/attack`, seed 11, start "trader", 2000 ticks lead, station cash ≥ 1 M):** extra gain of every player trade at the station against an honest price taker = Σ sales (revenue − units × p_undisturbed) + Σ purchases (units × p_undisturbed − cost), p_undisturbed from a twin world without player trades; held goods cancel out. Two baselines: strict (same tick) and best honest timing (highest undisturbed price since the attack began for sales, lowest for purchases). With V1 every targeted loop family of this program came out at ≤ 0 against best timing (A, B, multi-step A/B, A+B combined, two ships both directions, save/load, using expiry both directions; one multi-step case +88 Cr = an NPC delivery after the player's purchase, bought at market) — **correction (review of round 32): that statement was wrong for V1 as built**; the program booked every sale in one booking (`Sell(9999)`), the random scripts waited ≥ 1 tick between bookings, so two bookings in the same tick were never tried: a counter-booking emptied the post and deleted it, and the rest went through at the price the player had moved (loop A in two bookings +4 847.5 Cr exactly as in round 31; mirror B +25 965.5 Cr with 1 M station cash, +6 617.6 with real cash; also with a second ship and as single-unit sales, up to +1 675). Fixed in round 32b. Without cap / round 31 the same families reached up to 54 816 / 25 965 Cr per attack. Random scripts with carried goods (8 scripts × 14 steps × 570 entries): 71 of 4 560 > 0 (max 5 398) — all from purchases at the market price while NPC traders had delivered more than in the undisturbed world (one case with a sale part of 331 Cr); no sale above the best honest price.
- **Remaining leak, not a buy-back issue:** two goods at one station — buying a station's product with no outside buyer bid (ship kits, modules) raises its own scarcity price and with it the willingness ceiling for the inputs (`MarketSystem`: product value = own price when nobody else bids); the input price jumps (Werft Hephaistos: 4 medium kits bought, hull plates 108.5 → 400 at unchanged stock 0.8, 49 carried plates +14 283 Cr with 1 M station cash). 113 of 649 product→input pairs > 0, median 1 354 Cr, independent of the cap (same without it). With the stations' real cash (reviewer): 112 of 649 pairs > 0, max ≈ 1 749 Cr (Werft Hephaistos, medium kit → hull plates) — a one-off effect per market state; further cycles of the same loop are negative. Open for a decision (station-wide snapshot cap or a market-side ceiling for goods without buyer bids); not patched. **Closed in Round 36** (input cap from the product post, see there).
- **V2 measured (`BuybackPiecewise`, default off, measurement only):** player dock trade unit by unit along the stock curve. Honest trade: a 10/50/100-unit sale earns −13/−18/−40 % in the median (every tenth one worse than −57/−56/−53 %), a purchase costs +5/+24/+45 % (every tenth one +66/+74/+128 %); the player fleet automation went broke in 40 000 ticks (wallet −2 / −290 Cr instead of ≈ 402 000). Same-good loops stay open (up to 1 864 Cr per attack, random scripts 945 of 4 560 > 0); only the two-goods leak mostly disappears because the product purchase itself becomes expensive. Posts could be dropped with V2, but it is worse on both counts.
- **Honest trade with V1:** player fleet automation, 9 ships (4 commanded), 40 000 ticks, seeds 11/3: V1 = round 31 within 9 Cr (3 capped sales, 95 Cr lost of ≈ 372 000 Cr earned, seed 11; none for seed 3); the "held" rule changes nothing there; compared with no cap at all −0.08 % / −0.5 % (planning differences already present since round 30).
- **UI size text:** System area and menu name the real tap size of the current stage ("Tasten ≈ 5,4 mm" at small on the A15) and that the stages only take effect with the next stages (`UiScale.Describe`, `UiScale.TapMm`).
- Tests: `Runde32Tests` (loops A and B with carried goods for V1 and the round 31 rule, multi-step, second ship and fleet booking, honest trade elsewhere and NPCs, repair/material mirror floor, notes and offer prices); `Runde30Tests.GemischteMenge…` now expects V1 (all units at the purchase price); selftest stage 107 checks the new note, stage 108 the UI size text.

## Round 32b (English summary): buy-back cap — counter-bookings no longer end a post early

- **Cause (reviewer, `r32-pruefung`):** `OnSold`/`OnBought` reduced a post by every counter-booking and deleted it at 0, while the market price stayed the one the player had moved until the next tick. Loop A in two bookings (bring 50, buy 50, one tick, `Sell(50)` capped → post gone → `Sell(rest)` in the same tick at 280): +4 847.5 Cr; mirror B (sell, tick, `Buy(q0)` → post gone → `Buy(more)` at the depressed price): +25 965.5 Cr (1 M station cash) / +6 617.6 (real cash); also with a second ship and as single-unit sales (up to +1 675).
- **Rule now (the declared one):** a post's purchase and sale parts carry `BoughtOpen`/`SoldOpen` (`Model/LedgerPost`); the cap/floor applies while the part is open, regardless of its remaining quantity (`PlayerLedger.BuyActive/SaleActive`). Counter-bookings only reduce the quantity. A part closes only in `LedgerSystem` (tick) when stock and price are back — purchase part additionally only when nothing of the purchased quantity is still held (`min(quantity, held) = 0`; held = cargo of all player-actor ships + storage at that station) — and at the earliest in the tick after the last booking (`LedgerPost.LastTick`; the market has repriced by then). With the round 31 rule (`BuybackWholeTrade` false, measurement only) the flags follow the quantity as before. Saved additively (`LedgerSave.BoughtOpen/SoldOpen/LastTick`), hashed with the post; NPC worlds have no posts (default hash D3567DA325B7FED9 unchanged).
- **Proof (reviewer's program, copy in `r32b/pruefer`, seed 11, best-honest-timing metric):** S4, S5b, S5c, S5d, S7c, S11 with 1 M station cash: 4 847.5 / 1 675.3 / 4 847.5 / 4 847.5 / 25 965.5 / 4 847.5 → **0** each; with real cash (`REALCASH=1`): S5c 2 833.9 → 0, S5d 2 833.9 → 0, S7c 6 617.6 → 0, the others 0. All 15 reviewer scenarios ≤ 0 with 1 M cash; with real cash 14 at 0 and S10 one case +21.6 Cr (post expired regularly, then an NPC delivery lowered the price and the player bought at market — the NPC-reaction class). Own program (`r32b/attack`) with same-tick multi-booking families (partial sale then rest, single units, mirror partial/single units, alternating buy/sell × 5, two ships in the same tick, both directions): every family 0 with V1 (round 31 rule: up to 4 847.5 / 25 965.5); same-tick random scripts with a second ship (8 × 18 steps, waits mostly 0): 50 of 4 560 > 0, none with a sale part — all purchases at a market lowered by NPC deliveries. The reviewer's original random scripts (waits ≥ 1 tick between bookings) give the same positive cases with V1, round 31 rule and without cap (36 cases, max 762 with 1 M cash; 13, max 60 with real cash) — independent of the cap. The second review's own random scripts with many same-tick bookings (6 840 runs each) separate the rules clearly: V1 0 cases (with one tick pause after each booking 9 cases, max 10.7 Cr, all purchases of energy at a market lowered by NPC deliveries, no sale part), round 31 rule 750 cases up to 23 204 Cr, no cap 1 115 cases up to 11 886 Cr.
- **Honest trade (reviewer mode `honest`, real cash, same numbers before/after 32b):** H1 (buy 5, 100 ticks, sell 50 carried) 18 of 49 with a loss against the undisturbed price, sum 1 978, max 894; H4 (buy 10, jettison, 3 000 ticks, sell 30 carried) 16 of 80, sum 2 151, 6 posts still open; H6 (speculation) 29 of 128, sum 1 899. Player fleet automation (9 ships, 40 000 ticks, seeds 11/3): wallet 401 921 / 401 834 Cr, 3 capped sales (95 Cr) / none — identical to round 32. The "held anywhere" rule changes nothing measurable there.
- **"Eternal" posts (not built, measurement switches `BuybackStaleTicks`/`BuybackStaleIgnoresStock`, default off):** the 6–8 posts still open after 3 000 ticks in H4 never see their stock return (consumption), not "price above purchase". Variant 1 (nothing held, stock back, 2 400 ticks since the last booking, no price condition): the reviewer's 15 scenarios stay 0 but those posts stay open, and the mirror side leaks a little (+7.2 Cr). Variant 2 (time only, nothing held): H4 posts all close (losses 25 → 23 of 103, 7 148 → 5 010 Cr with 1 M cash), but the mirror loop opens wide ("expire and buy back": 369 cases, up to 11 429 Cr) and the purchase side +4.3 Cr. Recommendation: keep the current expiry; neither variant is loop-free.
- **Display:** notes say that the cap applies to any quantity and lasts until price and stock are back ("Wegen deines Kaufs hier: höchstens p je Einheit, bis Preis und Lager zurück sind" / mirror "Wegen deines Verkaufs hier: mindestens p …"); short forms "Deckel p"/"Boden p" in the editor's buy/sell line, a short variant for line 3 picked by measured width.
- Tests: `Runde32Tests` +5 with real ticks (partial sale empties no post and the rest in the same tick stays capped, mirror, two ships in the same tick, single units, a post with quantity 0 survives save/load and expires in a tick). Adjusted on purpose: `Runde30Tests.Verfall_…` runs two ticks and `Runde31Tests.EhrlicherHandel_…` advances the tick before `Prune` (expiry is checked at the earliest in the tick after the booking).

## Round 33 (UI stage E2, English summary): flight HUD in zones, UI factor per layer, menu "UI-Größe" fixed

- **Zones as a pure Core function** `SpaceSim.Core/Ui/FlightHudLayout.Compute(w, h, safeInsets, bays)` → rect per `HudItem` (tests `FlightHudLayoutTests`). *Classic* (usable area ≥ 1280 × 720): the old positions, anchored left/centre/right as before and now also vertically (bottom zone: turn buttons, RÜCK, menu, throttle, hit/escape lines at the bottom edge), safe area on all four sides; at exactly 1280 × 720 every position is unchanged. *Compact* (smaller, i.e. UI factor > 1 or safe-area insets): fixed corners (status top left, command/map/time + target card top right, menu/turn/RÜCK bottom left, throttle bottom right 320–600 wide), sector chip and status lines shrink to the room between status and "Kommando" (a long faction name shows its tag), "WENDEN" hidden (hit/escape lines take its place), "n von m bereit" moves into "Alle Feuer", and the combat column (bays, Alle Feuer, Dauerfeuer, Nächster Gegner, Ziel folgen, attack, Ergeben) flows into 176 × 56 cells from bottom right, row by row leftwards/upwards, never touching a fixed rect; cells are reserved for all combat buttons (nothing jumps). At 985 × 554 (16:9 at 1.3) four bays plus all six combat buttons fit; with five or six bays the *last combat buttons* ("Ergeben" onto "Station/Tor angreifen", with six also "Ziel folgen") would have shared a cell — corrected in round 33b (the layer factor is lowered instead, `FlightHudLayout.FitFactor`); without lowering six fit from 1113 × 696 (16:10 at 1.15) / 1200 × 554 (A15 at 1.3). `FlightHudLayout.FindSpot` places the flight alert/toast where the preferred spot would cover HUD rects (alert: second pass avoids only buttons).
- **UI factor per layer** (decision, docs/UI-ANPASSUNG.md section 8): `UiScale.Factor` scales the adaptive layers via `Control.Scale` (today the flight HUD, plus alert/toast/debug line while flying — `Hud.ApplyLayerScale`); the root `content_scale_factor` stays 1, so picking, target ring, edge arrows, combat framing (`Main.FramingField`), label hiding and the firing-arc cones (drawn with the inverse layer transform) keep working in screen pixels via `GetGlobalRect`. Fixed full screens, the hamburger menu and the star map stay at `UiScale.ScreensFactor` = 1 until E3–E5 give their layers the same factor; once all are adaptive E5 may switch to the global factor. Flight cap = minimum logical area 960 × 540 (`UiLayout.MaxFactor`), so the switch now works in flight: A15 small 1.00 / normal 1.15 / large 1.30, tablets 1.00.
- **`--ui-check`** (all screens, 6 resolutions × 3 factors): flight states (normal, raid warning, combat, auto-mining, station attack, hamburger menu, alert + toast) summed over all resolutions — outside 0/320/523 → 0/0/0 at factors 1/1.15/1.3, overlaps 0 → 0, truncated texts 30 → 30 (same findings); full screens now A0/Ü0 at every factor (they stay at 1). New `--ui-check-flight` (flight states only, also writes images at 1.15/1.3). `UiCheck` measures mm with the root factor (global rects include the layer scale).
- **Device test round 32b (hamburger menu):** (1) the "UI-Größe normal (1,00×, …" line was cut — now "UI-Größe: normal" with factor and tap size in the sub line ("1,15× · Taste etwa 6,2 mm"); (2) tapping it did nothing — cause: the menu reports `ui_size` to `Main.OnCommandAction`, which had no case for it (only `OnMenu` had one, same bug type as "Musik" in round 13), so it fell into the default branch; now handled, the menu stays open, the line shows the new stage, the toast lies on the alert layer above the menu; (3) "≈ 4,7 mm" instead of ≈ 5.4 mm — cause: `DisplayServer.ScreenGetDpi()` on Android returns `DisplayMetrics.densityDpi` (density class 450 on the A15, physical ≈ 396 ppi; the Godot 4.7.2 Android library's `GodotIO.getScreenDPI` references only `densityDpi`, and 84 px ÷ 450 × 25.4 = 4.74 mm). Texts now say "etwa"; the DPI factor is ≈ 14 % too high on the A15 (1.48 instead of 1.30), without effect there because the stages are capped at 1.15/1.3.
- Check runs (selftest, `--ui-shots`, `--phone-shots`) set `UiScale.PinnedFactor = 1` (headless the window is 64 × 64 and the DPI math gave "normal" = 1.15 and a compact HUD), so their images stay in the 1280 grid; stages 108/109 and `--ui-check` test higher factors explicitly.
- Selftest: stage 108 checks the factor on the flight layer (root 1); new stage 109 (`SelfTest.Round33.cs`): classic positions at 1280 × 720, menu line fits and a real tap switches the stage (label and toast), factors 1.3/1.15 (all visible buttons inside, no overlap, alert on no button), safe area on four sides. No simulation change (default hash D3567DA325B7FED9). Nothing was checked on a device.

## Round 33b (English summary): review of E2 — never two buttons in one cell, Leviathan bays, own ship never covered

- **Correction:** with five or six bays at 985 × 554 (16:9 at 1.3) it was not the bays that lost their cell but the *last combat buttons* ("Ergeben" lay exactly on "Station/Tor angreifen", with six bays also "Ziel folgen"); with pessimistic safe-area insets it hit the A15 at 1.3 already with four bays. Fix (pure Core) `FlightHudLayout.FitFactor(w1, h1, safe1, bays, wanted)`: the largest factor ≤ the stage (steps 0.01, never below 1) at which every bay and all six combat buttons have their own cell; `FlightHud.ApplyScale` uses it (16:9 large: four bays 1.30, five 1.26, six 1.25). `FlightHudLayoutTests` now run 6 resolutions × 3 factors × with/without insets (60/20/60/30) × 0–6 bays without exceptions, also labels/warning lines against buttons.
- **Leviathan (10 weapon bays, the HUD has 6 buttons):** the sixth button summarises the rest ("+5 weitere", "Schacht 6–10", "n bereit · feuern"; a tap fires all ready ones — `FlightHud.MoreBaysDown` → `Main.FireReadyBays(from)`). Before, bays 7–10 only fired via "Alle Feuer"/auto-fire without any display.
- **Own ship never covered:** `FreeField.LargestNear` (Core) — when the screen centre lies under HUD buttons (compact HUD), the largest free rect around a free grid point (before: the whole screen, so the ship stayed under "Ergeben"/"Dauerfeuer"); without a combat target `CombatFraming.CenterShip` puts the ship in the middle of that field; the pull-back loop towards the screen centre only runs when the centre itself is free. Alert and toast avoid the ship ring (`FlightHud.ShipRectLocal`). `--ui-check` marks "S!" when a button, the alert or the toast covers the ship centre (±16 px).
- Safe insets moved to `UiScale.SafeInsets1()` (pixels at factor 1, `UiScale.TestInsets`), each layer divides by its factor.
- Device test r33: the toast after switching the UI size over the open menu ran off the bottom — over full screens it now ends 12 above the edge (height measured) and is short (`UiScale.ShortText`).
- Selftest stage 109 extended: war test (Bollwerk, five bays) at stage 1.3 → layer 1.26, all buttons inside without overlap, real taps on ◀, the throttle (75 %) and "Dauerfeuer", ten bays → "+5 weitere". Tests `Runde33bTests` (free field with covered centre, ship centred in the field).

## Round 34 (UI stage E3, English summary): dock menu and dock areas adaptive

- Dock menu and all dock areas live in their own layer over the whole screen (`Hud._dockLayer`, no longer centred in 1280) with their own factor — "factor per layer" as in E2. Command centre and hamburger menu stay at 1 (E4/E5).

Dock layer factor = the largest factor ≤ the stage at which every area keeps its minimum inner area (with ship column width ≥ 600, without ≥ 1008 = the design width — narrower truncated station info, outfitter, shipyard and hangar texts —, height ≥ 400; dock menu ≥ 480 high): A15 normal 1.15 / large 1.24 (tap ≈ 6.7 mm), 16:9 phones and tablets 1.02 — superseded by round 34b (full factor everywhere). `UiScale.DockFactor` holds the effective value.

The big alert over a dock area sits in the top bar gap (`DockAreaScreen.AlertSlotGlobal`), over the dock menu left of the card (`DockMenu.CardGlobal`).
- **Device test r33 (trade):** the buy-back-cap row note was cut hard at the row end — now the longest variant that fits measured with the font, last resort with "…" (`TradeScreen.RowNote`); editor line 3 said "hier: Wegen deines Kaufs hier: …" — the cap note is a full sentence and stands without a prefix, only station acceptance reasons get "Verkauf hier:"; an opened row lay cut off at the top — after opening, the list re-scrolls for 30 frames from the real node positions (top first), so a later restore of the old scroll position (rebuild per tick) cannot shift it.
- **`--ui-check`** (also docks at warehouse, sector HQ and trade station; research is off in the default world and not measured): dock states summed over six resolutions — outside 0/356/471 → 0/0/0 at factors 1/1.15/1.3, overlaps 0/0/3 → 0/0/0, truncated 36 → 31/38/46 (station-info market column; corrected in round 34b: names were indistinguishable and one measurement had caught a half-built state, so the numbers were unreliable). Fill of the dock areas at factor 1 per resolution: 95/78/78/90/82/95 % → 95/96/96/96/96/95 % (worst 71 % → 95 %).
- Selftest stage 110 (`SelfTest.Round34.cs`): classic dock shell at 1280 × 720, energy bought at a farm (cap active) → row note fits measured, editor line 3 without the double "hier", first row opened by a real tap while the last was open and the list at the bottom → fully visible, toast over the open menu fully on screen, stage 1.3 → dock layer at the fitted factor, all buttons inside without overlap, real tap on "Kacheln". No simulation change (default hash D3567DA325B7FED9). Nothing checked on a device.

## Round 34b (English summary): review of E3 and device test r34 — "large" works in the dock everywhere, messages never over buttons/text, names distinguishable

- **B1:** the dock layer stayed at 1.02 on 16:9 phones/tablets because of the minimum inner width 1008/600. Instead of lowering the factor, the areas now cope with an inner width of 740 (height 380): `UiKit.At` shrinks single-line labels to font 11 before "…" (Core `UiLayout.FitFontSize`), the hangar stacks its right column, the station info puts "Politik & Hinweise" under the other two cards, outfitter rows put the button below the texts under 560 width, and the ship column collapses when 600 would not remain beside it (`DockShell.ShipCollapsed`; cargo and hull move into the top bar). Dock factor now = the stage everywhere (16:9 large 1.02 → 1.30, 56-px tap 4.9 → 6.3 mm at 1280×720; A15 1.24 → 1.30, 7.0 mm). The device reports its safe-area insets to the log and the camera debug line (`UiScale.InsetsText`).
- **B2:** message strips — `DockShell.MessageStrip`/`CommandShell.MessageStrip` (classic: the footer between "← Menü" and "Abdocken"; compact: a strip under the centre), `DockLayout.MenuStrip` (left of or under the dock-menu card), hamburger menu: left of its window. Screens implement `IMessageStrip`; `Hud` puts alert and toast there. In flight the alert also searches a free spot in the classic HUD. `--ui-check` counts "M" (alert/toast over a button or text).
- **B3:** station-info market rows two lines (name over the full column, measured: smaller, then `UiLayout.MiddleEllipsis`), same rule for trade-list names and shipyard stats; 0 truncated texts in all dock states.
- **B4:** `KitScroll` divides the finger distance by the layer scale (device: 400 px → 470 px). **B5:** `--ui-check` measures until two measurements agree (element count and button positions; max 8, else "?!"), reports "B" (content-covered share by grid sampling) and the smallest tap in mm instead of the hull fill. **B6:** in combat the flight layer keeps its bay cells and factor (a failed weapon module removes its bay). **B7:** re-scrolling after opening a trade row stops when the user scrolls. **B8:** `DockLayoutTests` check the minimum inner area also at factor 1; `WeaponBays.ReadyToFire` is the one rule for "Alle Feuer" and the summary button (test). **B9:** selftest text, doc corrections (A15 large was 1.24, the 31/38/46 statement).
- Device test r34 also: compact rail shows only whole entries (`DockLayout.SnapRail`), trade tiles share the full width, the dock menu stays a 580-wide card (decision, documented), an open trade row shows the cap only in short form.
- Selftest stages 109 (B6), 110 (B4 swipe 1:1; waits for a settled layout before tapping), docs `docs/UI-ANPASSUNG.md` section 11.

## Round 35b (English summary): ui-check tool extended, Dock/command-centre/flight defects at UI factor 1.3 fixed

- **Tool first (`--ui-check`, `godot/scripts/UiCheck.cs`).** Round 35's "0 truncated" was only the old tool's definition. Now: images for every factor; **T** = text over text or over a button with font-measured text rects (alignment, wrapped lines, glyph core, clipped by clipping ancestors, faded elements ignored); **C** = clipped: a text/button beyond its card frame (PanelContainer or control whose first child is a Panel) or a clip — vertical cut allowed only inside a vertically scrolling container (incl. the whole-area scroll); drawn texts report through `Ui/Kit/DrawnText` (helpers `UiKit.DrawFit`/`DrawFit2`: fit, draw, report; used by universe/sector maps, `LabelLayout`, trade rows/tiles/editor, assets/factions/comms rows, dock-menu chips); **S** = smallest effective font (size × factor) per state; wrapped labels count as truncated only if lines are hidden. Options `--ui-check-noflight`, `--ui-check-sizes=1280x720,…`, `--ui-check-factors=1.3`. The text rule is shared with the selftest as `UiCheck.TextChecks`.
- **Fixed:** Comms narrow (inbox height from rows, counter and message below), assets row (three lines + fixed right column below 954 px), outfitter (section headers wrap — the long header made the list wider than its frame), universe (node space from column spacing, smaller halos, sub-line 2/1/0 lines with short form, player marker left of the node, detail card fixed footer with three buttons and scrolling text, owner line wraps), sector card (fixed footer), fleet narrow (list height from content), hangar stacked (rental right below), storage rail "Nicht hier", taxi "Sprünge", start-package titles wrap, faction relation column wider, trade (wider name column on wide rows; names that do not fit even at the floor go to two lines, tiles too, compound words hyphenated), station-info market pairs grouped. Flight: alert height from the measured text (no ellipsis), placement tiers (beside everything ≤3 lines → narrower ≤6 lines → everything except sector chip/situation line → taps and ship only) with font down to the layer floor, a free spot is kept; `FlightHudLayout.FindSpot` adds an edge-based second pass (`EdgeSpot`, test); the toast measures its wrapped height and uses the same tiers also in the classic HUD; target name shrinks to the floor, then drops the owner name ("Korsar #22 [RSC]").
- **Font floor** `UiLayout.FontFloor(base, factor)` (Core, tests): at "klein" down to 11, at normal/groß never below base ÷ factor (rounded up, ≥ 11), so the effective size never falls below "klein"; then wrap, truncate last. Used by `UiKit.At`/`FitLabel` (wraps when two lines fit), `DrawFit`, trade names, target name, alert, toast.
- **Collapsed ship column:** rail entry "Dein Schiff" (sub: cargo and shield) opens the full ship card over the centre.
- **Proof** (`space-adventure-scratch-archiv/e4b`): Dock + command centre 468 states (6 resolutions × 3 factors): outside/overlap/text-over/clipped/message-over 0, truncated 0 except an occasional dock-menu ship chip (126 px, middle ellipsis, depends on the random start state — justified); flight 126 states all 0. Trade-list rebuild at Werft Delta 9.9 ms (9.1 without label fitting); name fitting per redraw 2.0 ms → 0.003 ms with a cache.
- **Selftest** stage 111: per command-centre area at 1.3 on 1280×720 the tool's text rule (0/0); real taps on a universe sector and a sector-map station in the scaled layer; real swipe in the factions list (1:1); combat lock through the real path (raid started, weapon removed → cells and factor stay; fight ends → released); `[perf]` line. 1144 tests green, selftest OK, balance hash D3567DA325B7FED9. Nothing checked on a device.
